# CLAUDE.md

Guidance for Claude Code (claude.ai/code) when working in this repository.

> High-level map. Deep specs live under `docs/`; ticket-level history lives in git.

## Project Overview

**agent-studio** is a local Matrix-to-Claude-Code stack for an enterprise context. It provides chat-based AI agents extensible with domain-specific skills for **Mainframe Migration** (COBOL, JCL, DB2, IMS, etc.).

The stack runs locally with Podman as a single compose file: **Conduwuit** (Matrix homeserver); **Orchestrator** (control-plane, spawns/manages one container per agent, no Matrix identity); **Admin Console** (web UI on `:4000` for agents, environments, SSH keys, skills, etc.; embeds a chat UI at `/chats/:agentId`).

The LLM backend is reached via an Anthropic-compatible proxy: `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` + `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN` in `.env` (gitignored). Default Sonnet/Haiku models set there too.

**Model Provider auth types.** A registered Model Provider (`/workspace/llm-endpoints/index.json`, tokens in `_keys/` at 0600) carries an `authType`: default **`api-key`** (Bearer against `baseUrl`) or **`oauth-token`** (Claude *subscription* auth via `claude setup-token`). For `oauth-token` the orchestrator injects `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN` and strips `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL`/`ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN`/`ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` so the CLI talks directly to `api.anthropic.com`. The login flow runs server-side under a PTY (`orchestrator/llm-auth-flow.js`, `/api/llm-auth/flows/*`); the token is claimed server-side, never reaching the browser. **Compliance:** consumer-subscription OAuth tokens are single-user only (Anthropic Consumer Terms) — creating/re-keying one requires a single-user confirmation recorded as `complianceAck`. One-shot server-side generation (`resolveAssistTarget`) skips `oauth-token` providers.

**Model Provider health (#1843).** A background poller in the admin-console API (`startLlmHealthPoller`, kicked off in the `app.listen` callback — the API's first background timer) probes every configured provider on an interval (default ~5 min, `AEE_LLM_HEALTH_INTERVAL_MS`) by reusing the existing `probeLlmEndpointTest` (`GET <baseUrl>/v1/models`), and persists the last-known status per provider in a **separate** `/workspace/llm-endpoints/health.json` (keyed by endpoint id — never inside `index.json`, so status rewrites don't race the CRUD read-modify-write of the endpoint list). A failing endpoint backs off exponentially (`AEE_LLM_HEALTH_BACKOFF_BASE_MS`/`_MAX_MS`, mirrors the github-poller curve) so a dead endpoint isn't hammered. Saving a provider (`POST`/`PUT /api/llm-endpoints`) probes immediately so a freshly-broken endpoint is flagged on save. `GET /api/llm-endpoints` merges each endpoint's `health` (`{ status: 'ok'|'error'|'unknown', reason, checkedAt, httpStatus }` — honest **`unknown`** when never probed, never a guessed green), rendered as a Health column on the Model Providers page; `GET /api/agents/:id/model-options` exposes the bound provider's `endpointHealth` and triggers a fresh on-demand probe when that status is stale (`AEE_LLM_HEALTH_STALE_MS`), so Agent Chat shows a **non-blocking** warning when the agent's provider is currently red. Pure decision logic (backoff, due-selection, result-folding, stale/view) in `admin-console/api/llm-health.js`; client view helpers in `admin-console/src/pages/provider-health-view.js`. First cut is Model Providers only — MCP servers + plugin brokers are a deferred fast-follow.

## Architecture

All human↔agent communication happens through **direct messages**: each agent has its own Matrix user; the human chats with it in a dedicated 1:1 room. There is no shared multi-agent room and no central routing identity.

```
Admin Console chat UI (or any Matrix client) → Conduwuit (localhost:8448)
       └── 1:1 DM rooms (one per agent, e.g. @cobol-agent:local ← as-agent:latest)

orchestrator.js (control-plane container, no Matrix presence)
  ├── Manages agent lifecycle (create/update/delete via Admin Console)
  ├── Spawns ONE Podman container per enabled agent over the libpod
  │     socket (/var/run/podman.sock) via spawn-container.js
  ├── Provisions per-agent network + volume isolation; reconciles live
  │     containers (by the `aee.agent.id` label) against its map
  ├── Provisions new agent Matrix users via the admin client
  └── Internal HTTP API on :4002 for the admin console (auth-gated, #1456)
```

- **Agents** are dynamically created Matrix users, each in its **own** Podman container (`as-agent:latest`), lifecycle-managed by the orchestrator. Configs persist in `/workspace/agents/<id>/` (`config.json` + `CLAUDE.md` + `AGENT-PROMPT.md`); DM room id in `dm-room.json` (created lazily by the console).
- Each agent is bound to a **Working Environment** (below) defining what gets seeded into its workspace.
- **Container-per-agent isolation** is three-way: per-agent **network**, per-agent **filesystem** via a SubPath mount of the pool volume (`AEE_WORKSPACE_ISOLATION`, default on), optional **dedicated volume** (`AEE_PER_AGENT_VOLUME`, opt-in). Optionally a fourth: an admin can bind-mount one or more **host folders** inside the container (podman/local only — see `docs/context-mount.md`), **read-only by default** or **read-write** per folder. A single legacy folder mounts at `/context` (`contextMount` + optional `contextMountRw` in `config.json`); multiple folders (#1290) persist as `contextMounts: [{ path, name, slug, readWrite? }]`, each at a distinct `/context/<slug>` target (`resolveContextMounts` reconciles either shape). The mounts exist **only in the agent's container**, so the console browses each *through* that container (#1286) and the agent is told about them by a gated CLAUDE.md section.
- **Agent runtime**: the container entrypoint `agent-image/scripts/agent-entrypoint.py` — Matrix client + Claude CLI invocations + trigger scheduler.

### Teams (human collaboration & visibility scope)

A **Team** (the *human* grouping) is the tenant/scope resources and human users are assigned to. **Do not confuse it with the `role: "team"` agent construct**, always called an **Agent Team** (an agent squad working a repo); they only share a word. Data model: three JSON collections under `/workspace` (`teams/`, `users/`, `memberships/index.json`); roles: **System Admin** (global, `user.systemAdmin`), **Team Admin**, **Team Member** (per-team). Every agent construct, env, and credential carries a `teamId` (default `default`). **Exception — personal credentials (#1567/epic #1558, Phase 1):** the new `personal` ownership tier carries a `userId` (the owner) and `teamId: null`; it is not team-owned.

Source of truth: `orchestrator/teams-store.js` (pure factory). The orchestrator bootstraps the default team + the **first** System Admin (seeded once from the configured initial user — see *Admin Console authentication* below, #2059) and backfills `teamId` at startup. CRUD at `/internal/admin/{teams,users,memberships}` (proxied `/api/admin/...`) — **distinct** from `/internal/teams/*` and `POST /api/teams`, which manage Agent Teams. Driven by **one** page since #2085 (epic #2076 P6, D10): **Administration** (`/users`, `Users.jsx` + `users-view.js` + the pure `administration-view.js`), a System-Admin-only surface with three tabs — **Users** (human-user CRUD over `/api/admin/users`), **All Teams** (the D5 clean-up view over every Agent Team, with delete) and **Resources** (the D7/D12 tiered ownership of agents and Working Environments). The former **Access** page (`/teams`, `Teams.jsx`) is **dissolved, not renamed** — `/teams` redirects to `/users`, its `ManageTeamPanel` is deleted (the Share dialog #2082 superseded it) and its two System-Admin halves are the other two tabs. This also discards #1910's rename, whose whole deliverable D10 declined to pay for a third time.

**Ownership (#2077, epic #2076 P1; design of record `docs/decentralised-access-concept.md` D1/D6/D8 + Amendment 1).** Every `role: 'team'` and standalone `role: 'agent'` config now carries an **`ownerId`** (the `users/index.json` id of the creating human) plus an **`ownerSource`** recording *how* the owner was decided — `creator` (the authenticated caller of a create route), `migration` (the D6 placeholder) or `conversion` (carried onto the new team by *Convert into a team*). A `role: 'team-member'` deliberately carries **neither**: `resolveAgentOwnerId` resolves it from the parent Agent Team (D8), and a copy on the member would be a second source of truth an owner reassign never updates. The owner is always stamped **console-side from the session** (`ownerFor(req)` in api.js, five create paths) — `POST /internal/agents` is unauthenticated, so an owner accepted from a request body would be self-asserted. A boot-time, idempotent `migrateOwnershipModel()` (pure core `orchestrator/ownership-migration.js`, the `backfillResourceOwnership` precedent) backfills owners over pre-existing configs and fans each *(user ↔ access-scope)* membership out to one *(user ↔ Agent Team)* membership per Agent Team in that scope, via the store's `appendMemberships` (which the migration needs because `setMembership` validates `teamId` against `teams/index.json`, and the new referent is an Agent Team config the store cannot see). **The fan-out is ADDITIVE and the access-scope memberships stay** — at P1 enforcement still keyed on each resource's access-scope `teamId` stamp, so replacing them would have emptied every list for every non-admin (P2/#2078 re-pointed the **agent** gates onto the fanned-out Agent Team grants but left the scope memberships in place, because environments, credentials and MCP servers keep their scope stamps until P5); §8.3 states the no-lockout property as `⊇` precisely to allow this, and `admin-console/api/ownership-no-lockout.test.js` is the proof (the real `scope.js` predicates over the full user×resource cross-product, plus a mutation that proves a *replacing* migration is detected as a reach loss). **Two ACs of #2077 were not deliverable as written and are recorded in Amendment 1**: retiring `teams/index.json` is the same edit as the P2 enforcement re-point (doing the store half alone changes what the Access page and five team dropdowns show), and "no null owners" cannot hold for an access scope with no members — D6's own rule ends in "else unset", so such a resource is reported at boot rather than given a fabricated owner. **The one behaviour change this forced** is in the personal-credential switch: see the `credential-resolution.js` note under Credentials below.

**Per-human Matrix identity (#1458, epic #2076 P1 — the identity half of `docs/multi-user-concept.md` §4).** Creating a user record through `POST /internal/admin/users` also provisions **that human's own Matrix account** — `@u-<16 hex of sha256(subject)>:local`, derived from the stable subject so a re-created record recovers the same account (and therefore its rooms), via the *same* admin-room `create-user → reset-password → enable-login` sequence + retried `/login` that every agent account goes through (`ensureHumanMatrixIdentity` in orchestrator.js over the pure `human-matrix-identity.js`). Continuwuity exposes no admin REST API, so those commands are **fire-and-forget** and the login is the only success signal: provisioning is therefore **best-effort, idempotent and backgrounded** off the create response, and a failure leaves the record intact without an identity for the next call to repair. `DELETE` deactivates the account and deletes the stored token alongside the existing personal-credential offboarding. Three deliberate boundaries: the account name is on the record (`matrixUserId`) but the **token is not** (see `human-matrix-store.js` in Key Files); the human-localpart shape is **reserved** at `ensureMatrixUser` so an agent id can never occupy a human's account; and provisioning is wired to the **route**, not to `teamsStore.createUser`, so the startup `ensureSystemAdmin` bootstrap admin is *not* handed a fresh account — that identity has to **adopt** the existing shared-admin rooms and their `sessions/<roomId>.jsonl` when the room model lands. **The room model itself is unchanged**: still one `dm-room.json` per agent holding a single `{ roomId }`, created (and invited/joined at creation time) by the shared `MATRIX_ACCESS_TOKEN` admin — §4's per-`(human, agent)` map (a separate room per human) is the second half and stays unimplemented. A single-operator stack creates no user records, so it provisions nothing and is unaffected end to end. Full correction of §4's stated mechanism: `docs/multi-user-concept.md` **Amendment 1**. **The send path itself moved off the shared admin (#2173, identity *in* the shared room, not the deferred per-human-room model above):** `POST /api/matrix/rooms/:roomId/messages` now sends as the acting human's own Matrix account (`human-matrix-store`) when one is provisioned, inviting+joining it into the agent's one shared room on first use, and the runtime's chat-turn gate (`is_human_sender` in `agent-entrypoint.py`, see *Chat-turn gate* below) no longer requires exactly two room members — so `event.sender` on a human message is that person, not `@admin:local`. The `io.aee.author` stamp (#2065, immediately below) still rides alongside it and is what a client without per-message sender resolution (or the admin-fallback path — no user record, or an identity still provisioning) falls back to. **Eager room membership on team share/unshare (#2174, epic #2166 B2, depends on B1).** The #2173 lazy invite above only reaches a shared human on their FIRST message to a given team member, so a freshly shared-in colleague's chat looked broken until they happened to write one. `POST`/`DELETE /api/admin/memberships` now reach every existing room immediately: a middleware mounted the `auditMutations` way (`res.on('finish')`, watching the routes' real final status rather than wrapping their own `canManageTeam`/`proxyAdmin` plumbing) calls `syncSharedMemberRooms(teamId, userId, action)` on a successful write. It targets only the team's already-materialized `team-member`s (`membersOfTeam` — the shared `agent-team-cascade.js` helper; a `role: 'team'` agent hosts no chat and therefore no room of its own) and is best-effort + idempotent (the #1458 discipline): a human whose Matrix identity has not finished provisioning yet is silently skipped (the lazy path above still covers them once it has), and a member with no `dm-room.json` yet has nothing to invite into or remove from. Invite reuses `ensureHumanJoinedToRoom` verbatim rather than a second implementation; removal is a plain Matrix `kick` on the shared admin token, which is already a member of every DM room (it was invited at that room's creation). Runs as fire-and-forget background work — a Matrix outage degrades to "the lazy path will catch it later," never to a failed or delayed membership response.

**Resource scoping.** List endpoints are filtered by the caller's team membership via `admin-console/api/scope.js`. The env admin + any System Admin get full visibility (`all: true`); others see only resources whose `teamId` is one of their memberships (global/pool skills always visible). **Whether any of that applies is the `AUTH_ENABLED` mode (#1457, `admin-console/api/auth-mode.js`):** off — the **default**, so an upgraded stack is unchanged — is *single-user compatibility*, in which `computeScope` short-circuits to the implicit System-Admin scope for whoever is signed in and every filter and gate is a no-op; `true` enforces the per-membership scoping above. It governs authorization only (the login is identical in both modes, an unauthenticated request still resolves to the empty scope, and the #1415 IAP auto-login gate is untouched because `isKnownPrincipal` reads `envAdmin`/`userId` and never `scope.all`), and the Default-Team adoption pass runs unconditionally, so activating the flag needs no migration step. **Exception — personal credentials (#1567):** the personal tier is checked *before* `scope.all`; only the credential's owner (by `userId`) can see or manage it — not a System Admin, not the env single-admin. Full spec: `docs/teams-data-model.md`. **Exception — global model providers (#2136):** an `llm-endpoint` may carry an additive `global: true` flag (orthogonal to `scope`) that makes it visible and assist-selectable to **every** user and every agent with no team assignment at all. The reachability rule is `isGlobalProvider` in `admin-console/api/llm-endpoint-global.js` (`global === true && scope === 'system' && authType !== 'oauth-token'`, re-read on **every** call), composed *only* at the model-provider call sites — api.js `providerVisible`/`providerUsableByTeam` (`isGlobalProvider(ep) || visibleCredential(…)`) and scope.js `assistSelectableEndpoint`, ⇄ orchestrator `assist-binding.js` `endpointUsableByTeam` (both tightened to `scope === 'system'`). It deliberately does **not** live in the shared `resource-tier.js` or in the generic `scope.js` `visibleCredential`/`credentialVisibleToTeam`, which also gate SSH keys and GitHub tokens (a `global: true` there would widen reach to a secret — #2136 finding 2). Only a System-scoped, non-oauth provider can be global, and management stays System-Admin-only. Publishing/retracting the flag is a System-Admin act enforced at the route (`resolveGlobalFlag` in `admin-console/api/llm-endpoint-global.js` — 403 for a non-admin, 400 for an oauth seat), and that gate also clears a kept flag on the unholdable oauth transition so the stored shape and the predicate can never disagree.

**Work-plane tenant isolation (#1459 §2 — governance GOV-ACCESS-3 / GOV-PLATFORM-4).** #302 scoped the agent **config** plane; the **work** plane — the workspace browser, the git actions in it, the session log, the context folders, the chat upload and the Matrix chat rooms — authorized nothing beyond the id's shape, so any authenticated user could read or delete any agent's files, run `git discard` in its checkout, read any agent's transcript and post into its room as the shared admin token. `docs/governance-concept.md` calls that *"the widest live hole"*; `docs/decentralised-access-concept.md` §11.2 records it partially closed (the cross-tenant half; the D9 write split is still P2). **One gate, not 34 checks** — a missing per-handler gate is invisible (the route answers correctly for the caller who is allowed *and* for everyone else), so `admin-console/api/work-plane-access.js` owns the rule and api.js mounts it as express middleware over a **prefix list** immediately after `app.use('/api', auth.gate)`, ~1 400 lines before the first route it protects: express walks its stack in registration order, so a route added later in the wrong place cannot bypass it. **The boundary is api.js's own parameter naming** — every work-plane route is keyed `:agentId`, every config-plane route (which carries its own #302 gate) is keyed `:id` — so the mount list is *all* of the `:agentId` family rather than a curated subset, and `work-plane-access.test.js` **re-derives it from api.js's route table** and fails if a route escapes it. Four decisions worth knowing. (1) **One predicate, uniform 404 — and it closes the *tenant* boundary only.** GOV-ACCESS-3 and #1459's AC both word the rule in two halves ("reads require `visibleAgent`, writes require membership in the agent's team"), but with today's two roles those halves are the same test — `visibleAgent` → `visibleTeam` → the caller's membership set — so a second branch would be unreachable code and a guard no mutation could falsify; the equivalence is instead **pinned by a test** over the user × agent × method cross-product. **The halves do diverge, and the successor is already designed:** #2041 **D9** (`docs/decentralised-access-concept.md` §6) rules workspace writes and `git-action` **Team-Admin-only** while a Team Member may read and chat, and §11.2 assigns that to **P2** — deliberately not done in this P1 ticket, because it is a behaviour change *inside* a team and it cannot be method-based (posting a message, opening a DM room, attaching a chat file and stopping your own run are all writes D9 allows a member, so P2 needs a per-route read/chat/manage classification). The refusal is **404, never 403**, because a private agent's *existence* is the thing being protected (the `model-options` / `resolveAgentRepoAccess` / `draft-access.js` AC7 precedent); the P2 split's own refusal is a 403, since a caller refused a *write* on an agent they can see already knows it exists. (2) **`scope.all` short-circuits before any disk read**, so on the shipped single-operator default (`AUTH_ENABLED=false`, where every caller is `all: true`) the gate adds exactly zero reads to the request path — without it the room gate would scan every agent directory on each chat poll and SSE reconnect. That is also the real staging mechanism GOV-ACCESS-3 asks for with "reads observe 1 week": the gate cannot bite until an operator turns multi-user authorization on. (3) **An unresolvable agent is refused, not waved through** — no config, no directory, or a room belonging to no agent all arrive at the predicate as `null` and answer the same 404, so an unknown tenant is never guessed as `default`. A *malformed* id is handed to the route instead, which keeps its existing 400. (4) **The Matrix room routes needed a reverse lookup**: `/api/matrix/rooms/:roomId/*` carries no agent id and is the same transcript by another door (the console's chat panel reads it there), so `findAgentTokenByDmRoom` was split into `findAgentByDmRoom(roomId) → {agentId, cfg}` — the token router's own `cfg?.token ?` rule stays where it belongs, because a gate reading that `null` as "no agent owns this room" would wave a tokenless agent's room through. Denied writes are still audited: `audit.auditMutations` is mounted before the auth gate and therefore before this one. **Not in scope, both flagged rather than guessed:** GOV-PLATFORM-4's second half (a short sensitive-path read-audit over `.claude/settings.json` / `users.md` / `_keys/` / session-log streams) is an observability tripwire, not a gate, and is untouched; and D9's intra-team write split is P2 (above).

**Enforcement re-pointed onto Agent Team membership (#2078, epic #2076 P2; design of record `docs/decentralised-access-concept.md` D1/D5/D8 + Amendment 2).** "Which resources can I see and change" is now answered per **Agent Team**, not per access scope — for **agents**. The boundary is read straight off the config by the pure `resolveAgentTeam` (`orchestrator/agent-team.js` ⇄ byte-identical `admin-console/api/agent-team.js`, two-sided-guarded): a `role: 'team'` **is** its own boundary, a `role: 'team-member'` is bounded by its `parentTeamId`, and a standalone `role: 'agent'` has **none** and is decided by its `ownerId` alone (D8 — the pre-#2078 `visibility === 'private'` condition is retired unread, nothing ever wrote that field). `agentMetaForScope` feeds it to `visibleAgent`/`canManageAgent`; `canManageAgent` mirrors the owner branch, or a standalone agent would be visible to its creator and editable by nobody but a System Admin. `computeScope`/`canManageTeam`/`describeAuthz` are **unchanged code** — the re-keying is a property of the data, since P1's fan-out made `memberships.teamId` an Agent Team id. **`scope.all` is untouched** (D5): all twelve System-Admin short-circuits survive, pinned by a census guard in `scope.test.js`. **The `|| 'default'` fallback is gone** from six predicates (concept §8.4) — an unstamped resource is invisible to a non-admin rather than collapsing into the default team; it is safe to drop because the orchestrator's boot-time adoption pass stamps an explicit `teamId` on every environment, MCP server and credential-index entry. **Two things deliberately did NOT collapse.** (1) **The credential axis**: `resolveAgentTenant` and `orchestrator/agent-tenant.js` survive unchanged with exactly two live callers (`usableEndpointsForTeam`, `resolveAssistBindingForTeam`), because credentials/providers/environments are still stamped with access-scope ids until **P5** re-points them — collapsing it would answer an Agent Team id where the credential index says `default` and make every team-scoped provider unusable, which is the bug the hop was introduced to fix. Do not reach for it to answer an authorization question. (2) **The scope memberships**, still present and still gating those stamps; `team-membership-enforcement.test.js` asserts that dropping them changes no agent's visibility or manageability, which is what makes P5's clean-up safe. Also out of scope by #2078's own Scope-out: the §11 leak fixes, the D9 Team-Member mutation boundary, agent-**creation** gates and the convert-to-team mint gate (all P3+).

**Self-service team creation (#2081, epic #2076 P3; design of record `docs/decentralised-access-concept.md` D1/D4/D6 + Amendment 5).** Any authenticated user can create an **Agent Team** and is recorded as its **Team Admin** in the same operation; the team is **private by default**. The interesting finding is that `POST /api/teams` and its stream twin were **already ungated** (A4.5) and already stamped `ownerId` (#2077) — what was missing is the **membership**: a `role: 'team'` agent IS its own access boundary (`resolveAgentTeam` → `cfg.id`), so `visibleAgent`/`canManageAgent` take the team branch and never reach the owner, and a membership-less creator could not see the team they had just made. The condition for writing it is the pure `admin-console/api/creator-membership.js` (`planCreatorMembership` — only when this request actually minted the team, so an `extend` re-asserts nothing and the write is idempotent; `null` for a caller with no user record, exactly like `ownerFor`), called by **both** handlers and **fatal** (the existing rollback undoes the create — a team its creator cannot see is worse than no team). `creatorEnvironmentOwnership` stamps the auto-created Working Environment with the Agent Team id, because that record used to carry **no tier fields at all**, which since #2078 dropped the `|| 'default'` fallback made a fresh team's own environment invisible to everyone but a System Admin (A5.5); a **reused** env is never re-stamped (that would be a re-tier, System-Admin-only). **Two enabling changes below the console.** (1) `teams-store.js` takes an injected **`agentTeamExists(id)`** resolver (wired in `orchestrator.js` to "a readable `role: 'team'` config"), because `setMembership` validated `teamId` against `teams/index.json` — which is also what would have made the D4 successor un-nameable on exactly the teams this phase creates; it defaults to a **closed** `() => false` and a throwing/unparseable config answers `false`. (2) Deleting an Agent Team now cascades its memberships (`removeTeamMemberships`, deliberately not `removeMembership` in a loop — that path enforces D4, and a team that no longer exists cannot become un-administrable): without it a dangling grant still counts towards the last-admin rule, so its holder could never be deleted again. **The D4 last-admin rule** (pure `orchestrator/team-admin-succession.js`) is enforced in the **store**, not on §2.3's three api.js routes: the orchestrator's own `/internal/admin/*` API writes memberships too, and an invariant of the form "this team can never become un-administrable" has to hold for every writer. Removing or demoting the last `team-admin` is refused unless a `successorUserId` **already in that team** is named (naming an outsider would be a second, unaudited invitation path), and the promotion + the removal/demotion land in a **single write**. Deleting a user is refused while they are the last admin of any team, re-checked at the top of `DELETE /internal/admin/users/:id` so the refusal precedes the destructive offboarding steps (#1571). The console needed no code for any of it — `adminQuery`/`proxyAdmin` already forward `successorUserId` and relay the 409 (now carrying a `code`) verbatim, and the Access page's Manage panel already rendered `e.message`, which IS the instruction — a property the Share dialog (#2082) inherited when #2085 deleted that panel. **Also in scope, and the one live hole:** `mode: 'extend'` on both team-create handlers was ungated, so under private-by-default any authenticated caller could grow a stranger's team — now `canManageAgent(scope, agentMetaForScope(cfg))`, the same pair every other team-mutating route uses (A5.6). **AC6** (create with no infrastructure) is the absence of a repository becoming legal rather than a new path: no `environmentId` **and** no repository sets the same `repoLess` flag #1623's reused-env branch sets, and the PAT requirement follows the repository rather than the environment. **AC3**: `POST /api/admin/teams` answers **410 Gone** naming its successor and the Access page's create form is deleted (the whole page went in #2085); the other scope verbs stay, because `teams/index.json` still backs that page and five dropdowns until A3.3's successor retires it — the stated consequence is that **no new access scope can be minted**, so a new team-tier credential must name an existing one or be personal/system. **Deliberately NOT done** (A5.8): `resolveOwnerTeamId` is untouched — widening it to accept an Agent Team id works for environments/MCP servers but would let somebody create a **credential no agent can use**, the tenant hop still resolving against a `'default'` stamp (A3.3); the Share dialog and AC13's e2e journey remain P4.

**The Share dialog (#2082, epic #2076 P4; design of record `docs/decentralised-access-concept.md` §7.1 + Amendment 6).** The visible half of the redesign: **Share…** in an Agent Team's context menu in the Agent Chat is now the whole member-management surface, so growing a team needs no admin console. It is a **front end over an existing mechanism** — `POST`/`DELETE /api/admin/memberships` have been `canManageTeam`-gated since #2081 and the D4 rule lives in `teams-store.js` — plus the D4 **successor picker** Amendment 5 owed this phase. Decisions are the pure `admin-console/src/pages/agent-chat/team-share-view.js`; the renderer is `ShareTeamPanel.jsx`, hosted in a `SidePanel` by `RailLifecycleHost` (a manage-a-list surface, the `ManageTeamPanel` precedent — deliberately not a `ConfirmDialog`, which closes on confirm and would shut on the first role change). **Five things are load-bearing.** (1) **AC8 and AC9 contradict each other and AC9 wins** (Amendment 6): AC8 asks for the entry to be *disabled* for a non-admin, AC9 asks that a Team Member *"sees the list read-only"* — a disabled entry opens nothing, and AC12/P6 retires `Teams.jsx`'s `ManageTeamPanel` as "superseded by the Share dialog", so disabling it would leave a member with no surface at all for reading their own team's membership (which D9 §6 explicitly permits). So `share` is the **one entry in `buildTeamMenu` that stays live without rights**, carrying `MEMBERS_READ_ONLY_HINT` instead of `NO_RIGHTS_HINT`, and `agent-lifecycle-menu.test.js` pins that as a deliberate exception to its AC8 loop. (2) **Read and write are different rights**: `GET /api/admin/memberships` is `visibleTeam`-filtered, so the panel gates its controls on `canManageTeam` and **never on whether the fetch succeeded**. (3) §7.1's recommendation was taken — `GET /api/matrix/agents` ships an **explicit `canManageTeam`** on `role: 'team'` rows and `buildTeamMenu` reads it as its own `canManageMembers` input (defaulting to `canManage`, so an un-migrated caller is byte-identical); the two predicates coincide today, which is exactly why the field is separate, and `team-share-routes.test.js` pins the coincidence so a successor that breaks it fails there rather than silently opening the member list. (4) **`POST` takes `teamId` in the body, `DELETE` in the query**, because that is where each route's own gate reads it from — a DELETE sending it in a body arrives at `canManageTeam(scope, undefined)` and fails closed for everyone but a System Admin, which is why both request shapes are built in the pure module. (5) The picker reads the **open directory** `GET /api/users/directory` (D2, built by #2068), never `/api/admin/users`, and `directoryIndex` re-asserts the `{id, displayName, email}` projection client-side so a widened payload cannot reach the DOM — D2 forbids the `systemAdmin` flag, D11 the GitHub handle, and a System Admin therefore appears in the results as an ordinary colleague. The D4 hand-over goes out as **one** request (the store applies the promotion and the removal/demotion in a single write), offers only people **already on the team** (an outsider would be a second, unaudited invitation path and the store refuses it), and states the sole-member dead end rather than showing an empty picker. `team-share-routes.test.js` is the net-new server guard #2041's P4 row demands — a predicate cross-product over the real `scope.js` proving a Team Member's write is refused, plus a mutation-proved source read that each route still calls the gate. **AC13's e2e journey is delivered by #2086** (see below) — it needed infrastructure rather than YAML, exactly as recorded, and that infrastructure is what #2086 built first.

**The AC13 journey — two humans, end to end (#2086, epic #2076; design of record `docs/decentralised-access-concept.md` Amendment 8).** The closing proof of the epic: `e2e/journeys/team-sharing.yaml` drives the whole loop (create → private-by-default → share → read-only member → promotion → manage → D4 hand-over) as **two separate signed-in people**. Four things are load-bearing. (1) **The blocker was the stack, not the YAML**: `AUTH_ENABLED=true` is now set for the e2e stack in `docker-compose.e2e.yml` (the e2e override — stack policy, not a credential). It is safe for the other 14 journeys *as a property of the code*, not as a hope: `computeScope` returns `all: true` for `username === adminUsername` in **both** modes and the storageState session `e2e/global-setup.js` persists IS that env admin, so only a *user-store* identity's view changes. (2) **Two identities without touching the journey schema** (journey-spec-concept.md §9's anti-DSL rule): `journeys.spec.js` builds `ctx = { page, request }` **once** and passes that same object everywhere, so `e2e/steps/identity.js` (a helper module, deliberately absent from `STEP_MODULES` — the `team.js` arrangement) hangs the extra identities off `ctx` and `access.actAs` re-points `ctx.page`/`ctx.request`; the whole existing step library and the recorder (which reads `ctx.page` fresh at each capture) follow the active human for free. (3) **Every "cannot" is asserted twice** — the control is absent from the screen *and* the same person's session is refused by the API — because the phasing table's own strategy note says a disabled button proves nothing; the D4 refusal keys on the stable `code: 'successor-required'` rather than the prose. (4) **AC13's literal step order cannot fire its own AC2** (Amendment 8, A8.1): its step 5 promotes B, which creates a second admin, so by step 7 the last-admin rule can no longer engage — the journey therefore probes D4 where it is live (A sole admin) and exercises the successor hand-over at the end, when B has become sole admin. Deliberately **not** a Helper-Chat tour chapter: the tour narrates one operator's onboarding and this journey's key beats are refusals, so it carries no `voiceover`+`screenshot` on the same step (the tour builder's exact candidate test) and is absent from `build-tour-manifest.js`'s `ROUTE_MAP` by design, not by omission.

**Layered resource ownership — the shared tier (#2083, epic #2076 P5; design of record `docs/decentralised-access-concept.md` D7/D12 + Amendment 3).** Working environments and MCP servers carried a single `teamId` and **no shared tier**, which is what made "creating a team requires wiring up a repo, a key, a token and a provider first" structural rather than incidental. Both now carry the same `{scope, teamId, assignedTeamIds, userId}` triple credentials have had since #798, defined once in `orchestrator/resource-tier.js` ⇄ byte-identical `admin-console/api/resource-tier.js` (two-sided-guarded, the `agent-team.js` arrangement) — the orchestrator needs it because its boot adoption pass must leave a shared record's deliberate `teamId: null` alone. `scope.js` gains `visibleEnvironment` / `canCreateEnvironment` / `canRetierEnvironment` and re-points `canManageEnvironment`; **one predicate family serves both kinds**, since an environment and an MCP server are the same thing under D12. `normalizeCredential`/`ownsPersonalCredential` become named aliases of the shared implementations, and the D5 census guard follows the personal-tier rule into its new home *and* now pins that every tiered predicate checks the personal tier **before** its `scope.all` bypass. **Two deliberate divergences from the credential predicates** (A3.1): visibility stays **membership**-based, not `canManageTeam` (that is #798's deferral of Team-Member credential access, and D9 says a member may read); and a shared record is **manageable by every assigned team's admin**, because `canManageCredential` refuses the system tier outright and copying that would make the eventual re-point a manage-axis lockout for any access scope holding two Agent Teams — measured, not argued, by a counterfactual in `ownership-no-lockout.test.js`. What stays System-Admin-only is the half that carries the risk: **which** teams reach a record, and moving it between tiers (`canRetierEnvironment`), decided by `isRetier` so an ordinary content save is not an operator act. **Three §11 holes closed on the way**, because a tier the read/write paths ignore is decorative: `GET /api/environments/:id` (§11.4) and `GET /api/mcp-servers/:id` were ungated reads, and `POST`/`PUT`/`DELETE /api/mcp-servers` had **no authorization at all** (§11.3 — a behaviour change for a plain Team Member, who could register, edit and delete registry entries). The environment `PUT`'s last literal `|| 'default'` goes too (§8.4). UI: both the Working Environments and MCP Servers forms rendered the **shared** `CredentialOwnership` control — neither page contained the string `teamId` before this ticket — seeded by the new pure `ownershipFromRecord`, because the environment panel both creates and edits and a create-shaped seed would silently un-share a shared record on the next ordinary save. **Removed again by #2169** (A3 of epic #2166): visibility must never be *chosen* on the form that creates or edits the resource, only *derived* from the owning team, so the control (and its `ownership`/`ownershipPayload`/the environment form's now-dead `/api/admin/teams` fetch) came back out of both forms; re-tiering an existing environment stays reachable only through **Administration → Resources** (`Users.jsx`'s `EnvironmentOwnershipRow`, untouched by #2169) — MCP servers have no equivalent surface yet, so an ownership-less `POST /api/mcp-servers` now resolves to an **unassigned system tier** (`resolveTieredOwnership` in api.js) rather than the prior bare-credential default of the `'default'` team. **AC3 (re-pointing the credential / provider / skill team tiers at Agent Team ids) is NOT delivered and is blocked on an edit no phase owns** (A3.3): `resolveOwnerTeamId` validates every stamp against `teams/index.json` at six sites and that file still backs the Access page and five dropdowns, so re-pointing a stamp means retiring it — which A1.2 deferred to "the P2 enforcement re-point" and P2 did not take. Independently, no lossless promotion exists for a credential (its shared tier already means System-Admin-managed), and collapsing the read path onto `resolveAgentTeam` would leave every standalone agent with no usable provider (`null`, A2.2). So `credentialVisibleToTeam`, `resolveAgentTenant` and the scope memberships all survive P5 together. AC4/#2041 AC11 is proven by `admin-console/api/team-needs-no-infrastructure.test.js`. Also unchanged by design: plugin install stays System-Admin (D12), toolchains stay System-Admin-only, and the **personal tier is refused** for environments and MCP servers (A3.2 — §5.1 asks only for the shared tier, and a tier with no `scope.all` bypass would make an environment the operator can neither repair nor delete).

**The Administration surface — the Access page is dissolved (#2085, epic #2076 P6; design of record `docs/decentralised-access-concept.md` D5/D10/D12 + Amendment 7).** Once sharing is decentralised (#2082) the central *Access* page has nothing left that a team's own admins cannot do, so **D10 dissolves the entry rather than renaming it** — which also discards #1910, whose whole deliverable was that rename. `src/pages/Teams.jsx` and its `ManageTeamPanel` are **deleted**, `/teams` is `<Navigate to="/users">` (the `/agents` → `/chats` precedent, #1930), and `/users` becomes **Administration**: one `adminOnly` nav entry whose three tabs (`?tab=` in the URL, so a link opens on one) are **Users / All Teams / Resources**. The page already sat behind `AdminRoute`, which is what makes every tab System-Admin-only with no second gate. Decisions in the pure `src/pages/administration-view.js` (`resolveAdminTab`, `allTeamRows`, `teamDeleteImpact`, `environmentTierSummary`, `environmentOwnershipPatch`, plus `groupAgentsForOwnership` moved in from the retired `teams-ownership.js`, whose `agentTeamTenant` collapsed unread). **Four things are load-bearing.** (1) **All Teams lists Agent Teams, and manages no memberships** — a membership has named a `role: 'team'` agent id since P1, so that IS the platform's team list; a role dropdown here would be a second, differently-warned copy of an operation that can lock a team's own admins out, and the tab needs **no new endpoint** because both delete cascades already exist (member agents with the parent #1960, memberships with the team #2081). (2) Each row carries a **no admin** marker, which is what discharges D5's claim that no orphan-recovery machinery is needed *because a System Admin can re-admin an orphaned team* — an argument that only holds if they can find one, and nothing showed it before. (3) **The Resources tab fixes a live bug rather than being moved**: since #2083 an environment is tiered, and `PUT /api/environments/:id { teamId }` — exactly what the old flat selector sent — normalizes to `scope: 'team'`, so one click **silently un-shared** a shared environment; the row now states the tier, edits it through the shared `CredentialOwnership` control and shows the resulting tier before the save. Agents keep the plain selector (an agent has no tier; an Agent Team still moves as one unit). (4) **A stated capability removal** (A7.4): an access scope can now be neither created (#2081, 410) nor renamed nor deleted from the console — `PUT`/`DELETE /api/admin/teams/:id` survive with no UI, deliberately, because A3.3 retires `teams/index.json` outright and a management surface for a record on its way out is work the next phase undoes. Help: `access-users.md` is **folded into** `users.md`, which becomes the Administration topic with three new chapters, and the seven topics naming *Access* in `seeAlso` are re-pointed at `/users` — except `agent-chat.md` and `team-setup.md`, where the link is **dropped**, the successor surface for what it promised being the **Share…** dialog on the reader's own page while Administration is System-Admin-only. That last choice moved the worst-case grounding context **back** to `/quickstart` and *lowered* it, 314 511 → **312 384** of 340 000, even though the merged topic is ~9 000 characters bigger than either half. Removal guard: `src/pages/access-page-removed.test.js` (mutation-proved).

### Working Environments

A **Working Environment** is a reusable **infrastructure template** at `/workspace/environments/<envId>/environment.json`: `id`/`name`/`description`; `repositories` (array of `{url, branch, sshKeyId, readOnly}`, zero or more repos cloned at creation — `[]` is valid for LLM-only / credential-free agents; `readOnly` (#1813, default off) still clones the repo but annotates it *reference-only — do not commit or push* where it is listed in the agent's generated context, changing awareness not availability; legacy single `repository` is migrated to a one-element array on read by the API, ~api.js line 695); `mcpServers` (optional `{serverId, credentialId}` refs into the MCP registry); `additionalContext` (optional text appended to CLAUDE.md); `toolchainId` (optional, #1940 — see below); `isTemplate` (optional, #2170 — see below).

**The general-purpose Working Environments page is removed (#2170, epic #2166 A4).** `/environments` is now `<Navigate to="/chats" replace />` — there is no page listing every environment to every user any more, mirroring the `/agents` → `/chats` (#1930) and `/teams` → `/users` (#2085) precedents. An ordinary team edits its **own** environment from Agent Chat's **Settings** tab, **Working Environment** section (#2167) — unchanged except it gained the **GitHub Token** select (`admin-console/src/pages/agent-chat/TeamEnvironmentPanel.jsx`), which had nowhere else left to live once the standalone page went. The page's other half — a System Admin curating a shared, reusable environment — becomes **Environment Templates** (`/environment-templates`, `admin-console/src/pages/EnvironmentTemplates.jsx`, `adminOnly` nav entry in **Administration**): a curated catalog of `isTemplate: true` environments that is a **copy source, never a live-shared binding**. `isTemplateEnvironment`/`resolveTemplateFlag` (`admin-console/api/environment-template.js`) mirror `global-resource.js`'s publish-gate shape (System-Admin-only to flip, omitting the field keeps the current value) — but where the retired `global: true` flag made a record *directly usable* by every team with no admin action, a template can **never** be bound: `POST /api/agents`, `POST /api/teams` and `POST /api/teams/stream` all refuse an `environmentId` that resolves to a template (`template-not-bindable`), and the ordinary `GET /api/environments` list excludes templates entirely (`?isTemplate=true` is the separate catalog read, open to any authenticated caller so the Agent Team Wizard's "start from a template" picker needs no admin rights). Starting a team from a template instead **copies its structure**: `POST /api/environments` accepts an optional `copyFromTemplateId` that seeds the new environment's repositories, MCP pairings, model provider, toolchain and additional context from the named template via `copyEnvironmentStructure` — every credential reference (`sshKeyId`, `credentialId`) is stripped, so the copying team always supplies its own (the same "copies structure, prompts for credentials" rule #2171/A5 states for team cloning). The wizard (`TeamSetup.jsx`'s `selectTemplate`) mints this new environment eagerly on template pick, then hands it to the same `selectEnvironment` path picking an ordinary existing environment already used — no separate code downstream. A pre-#2170 `global: true` environment is read as a template on the catalog list (`isTemplateEnvironment`'s migration rule — "the global idea becomes copyable templates") and becomes a real, on-disk `isTemplate: true` record the first time it is saved from the new page; an unrelated edit through any other route never triggers this (the flag's *current* value for gating is read from the explicit field only, never the migration inference, so an ordinary content save can't silently reclassify a record).

**Agent toolchain: which image the environment's agents run (#1940, concept `docs/agent-toolchains-concept.md` §3).** The default agent image (`AGENT_IMAGE`, default `as-agent:latest`) carries Python + Node and **no other language runtime** — an agent on a Java repo can edit but not build it. An environment may name a **toolchain** (`toolchainId`, written only when set so a toolchain-less env is byte-identical on disk to a pre-#1940 one); its agents are spawned from that toolchain's image and every other environment keeps the default. **The client sends an id, never an image reference** — `startAgentProcess` injects the env's SSH identity, GitHub PAT and model credential into every agent container, so whoever picks the image picks what runs with those secrets. The authorisation split copies the plugin registry: a **System Admin** registers a toolchain (`POST`/`DELETE /api/toolchains`, 403 otherwise — the *only* place an image reference is accepted from a client; UI = the **Toolchains** panel on the Environment Templates page (#2170, moved from the retired Working Environments page)), a **Team Admin** only selects a registered one (`resolveToolchainSelection` on env create/update). Registry = `/workspace/toolchains/index.json`, console-writes / orchestrator-reads like `/workspace/llm-endpoints`, **re-validated on every read** on both sides (the `plugin-registry.js` discipline). The pure decision core is `orchestrator/toolchain-registry.js` ⇄ byte-identical `admin-console/api/toolchain-registry.js` (`resolveAgentImage` = env → toolchain → image with the fallback chain, `validateToolchain`, `normalizeRegistry`, `missingImageMessage`); client-side view decisions in `admin-console/src/pages/toolchain-select-view.js`. Four consequences worth knowing: (1) **the container-runtime seam now carries the image** — `spawnContainer({ id, env, contextMounts, image, toolchain })`, resolved by the orchestrator and used verbatim by both implementations, which no longer read a module-level image constant for the agent container; (2) **an unhonourable selection refuses the spawn** with a message naming the environment and the toolchain (unregistered / wrong runtime / image absent-or-unpullable) — never a silent fallback, which would boot a build agent without its toolchain and fail at the first `mvn`; (3) **changing it respawns the env's running agents** via the existing `githubTokenId` path (`POST /internal/environments/:id/respawn-agents`), because a spawn-time image is immutable for a container's lifetime — the console warns first; (4) **`evictStaleAgentContainers` keys on the `aee.agent.id` label, not the image ancestor** — an ancestor filter would leave a stale variant-image container running forever (the `aee.instance` foreign/legacy skip is unchanged). The per-agent-volume **seed/reseed helpers deliberately run on the DEFAULT image**, not the toolchain's: they are `cp -a` plumbing that uses nothing a variant adds, one code path for every agent, and it keeps a missing-toolchain-image failure in the one place it belongs. **Toolchain images (#1941, concept §4/§5; operator + project guide `docs/agent-toolchains.md`).** A variant is a source tree `toolchains/<id>/` = `toolchain.json` (validated by `validateToolchainManifest`, the stricter sibling of `validateToolchain` — `description` and `versionSource` are REQUIRED, `bootstrap` must name its `versionManager` and `baked` must not) + a `Dockerfile` that starts **`FROM as-agent:latest`** and may only ADD layers. `make toolchain-images` builds every variant (context `toolchains/`, because each `COPY`s the shared bootstrap from `_shared/`; `_`-prefixed dirs are shared material, never a variant) and is chained into **neither `up` nor `build`** — deliberately diverging from `plugin-images`, since a variant downloads a JDK and only installations that register one need it. `make seed-toolchains` lays the manifests into `/pool/toolchains/` like a reference plugin, so `POST /api/toolchains {id}` (bare id, no image) installs a SHIPPED variant by reading its manifest server-side while the #1940 hand-registration door stays open — two doors, one `validateToolchain` gatekeeper. Three things worth knowing: (1) **"derive, never fork" is enforced, not reviewed** — `toolchains/_shared/base-contract-check.sh` probes a BUILT variant for the entrypoint, `/opt/ace/scripts`, `/opt/ace/CLAUDE.md`, the `/opt/aee/plugin-bin` PATH entry and the four platform CLIs, and is a hard gate in the CI `toolchain-images` job AND on the release path, because a forked base builds cleanly and only fails at runtime as "command not found". (2) **A variant ships a version MANAGER, not a version** — `mise` (not `sdkman`, which needs a login shell the Claude CLI's `sh -c` never is); the project repo's committed `.mise.toml` decides the version, so a JDK update is a commit there rather than a platform release. The shared, stack-agnostic `toolchains/_shared/aee-toolchain` resolves it per invocation and redirects the manager's data dir (plus Maven's local repo / `GRADLE_USER_HOME`, via the variant's `cache-env.sh`) to `<workspace>/.aee-toolchain` — a volume, so it survives the respawn that wipes the container filesystem, and per-agent, so two projects on two Java versions never share a cache. Everything stack-specific lives in the variant's `toolchain.env` (pin files, shims, alt-pin files, baseline), so the next stack is a Dockerfile + two config files and no code change. Reachable non-interactively through a fixed `/opt/aee/toolchain-bin` on PATH + one symlink per tool — the plugin-bin hook's shape, for the same `podman exec` reason. (3) **AC11: the deployment names the image, the manifest does not** — a manifest declares the bare `as-agent-jvm:latest`; `AEE_TOOLCHAIN_IMAGE_PREFIX` + `AEE_TOOLCHAIN_IMAGE_TAG` (both Deployments, pinned per release by `set-images.sh`, asserted by `render-check.sh`, pushed by `deploy-gke.yml`) are joined onto it by `resolveToolchainImage` inside `resolveAgentImage` at **spawn** time, never persisted into the registry entry — so a release re-pin reaches a toolchain installed long before it, and a hand-registered full reference (contains `/` or `@`) is never rewritten.

**Environments do NOT carry skills — never re-add them.** The skill *grant* is **team-owned** (`orchestrator/skill-grants.js`): team agents (`role: team`) carry `skills[]` (inherited by every member without a `skillsOverride`); standalone agents/orchestrators carry their own; bp-instances inherit from their parent orchestrator. `effectiveSkills(cfg, loadCfg)` resolves the grant everywhere (seeding, CLAUDE.md gating, preview).

**Skill seeding & activation.** Every seed writes a per-agent manifest `/workspace/agents/<id>/.agent-studio/seeded-skills.json` (`{name: {tier, seededAt}}`) — the truth for whether a running member actually has a skill. One-click (de)activation is an **additive file sync, not a respawn** (skills are static files read per invocation): `POST /internal/teams/:teamId/skills/{activate,deactivate}` (or `/internal/agents/:id/skills/...`) edits the list + copies/removes per-member files via `planSkillSync`, rebuilds each CLAUDE.md, mirrors into isolated volumes (proxied `POST /api/knowledge/{activate,deactivate}`). At **autonomy level 3** the curator's auto-promotions are auto-activated (learn → bundle → promote → activate, no human).

**Agent creation seeding** (orchestrator): seed `.claude/` + `memory/` from the skill-pool volume (or bundled defaults); copy the resolved skill grant (team-first, then global); patch absolute `/workspace/` paths in memory; materialize the env's MCP refs into the agent's `.claude/settings.json` (per-agent credential substitution); `git clone` if the env has a repo (SSH URLs → `GIT_SSH_COMMAND`; HTTPS GitHub → PAT via `GIT_CONFIG_*`); build CLAUDE.md via the prompt-assembly pipeline.

**Propagating repository changes to existing agents (#1342, reworked by #2066).** Repos are cloned at creation only, so a repo added to an environment afterwards never reaches its existing agents. #1342 closed that with two manual actions; #2066 **removed the environment-level one and replaced it with an offer made at save time**, and widened the core from "clone what is missing" to "reconcile the declared list against what is actually on disk".

**The core** is `orchestrator/repo-sync.js`, still pure with injected probes but now driven by `deps.statuses` (an `inspectAgentRepoStatuses` array) rather than a `hasGit` predicate — the plan is derived from the DECLARED list versus the ACTUAL clones, never from a remembered save-time diff, which is what lets the save-time offer, the out-of-date catch-up and the per-agent panel all drive it without being able to disagree (AC21). Per declared repo: not cloned → `cloned` (unchanged #1342 behaviour, incl. `path-occupied`); cloned and matching → `skipped`/`already-present`, untouched; cloned but diverging → `adjusted`, *if and only if* `cloneAdjustSafety(status)` clears it. A clone with no declared entry is `retained` — **never pruned, under any circumstance**, because a checkout may hold unpushed work (the one hard constraint of the ticket).

**D1, the safety predicate** (`cloneAdjustSafety`, its own unit): an adjustment happens only on a clone that is clean and fully pushed. Every other state is a *reported skip* carrying a stable `reason` code plus an actionable `detail` — `uncommitted-changes` · `unpushed-commits` · `no-upstream` · `upstream-unknown` · `detached-head` · `no-remote` · `status-unreadable` · `foreign-remote` — and is marked `blocked: true` so a surface can keep it visually apart from a failure (different resolution: push or commit, versus fix credentials or the URL). Nothing stashes, resets, force-checks-out or cleans anywhere in the path; `adjustRepoClone` is `remote set-url` → `fetch --prune` → `checkout` + `merge --ff-only`, so a diverged local branch REFUSES rather than being rewritten. **`foreign-remote` is the one check that looks like it needs history and does not**: the platform always clones into `repoDirName(url)`, so a clone whose directory name still matches its own remote is one the platform placed there (possibly from an older declared URL — the D1b same-directory case), while a clone whose directory and remote disagree can only have been repointed by hand.

**D1b, a changed URL** is decided by the derived directory name, because that is the only thing the platform keys on: same directory name → the remote is repointed in place; a *different* directory name → treated as an addition **plus** a removal (fresh clone in its own directory, old directory left alone and reported `retained`). This falls out of the diff's own keying rather than needing a special case.

**D4, the out-of-date indicator.** `repoDivergence(repositories, statuses)` is the workspace-derived divergence set (a declared repo with no clone, or a clone whose branch or remote URL differs); a clone with **no** declared entry is deliberately never divergence, since nothing is ever pruned and it could therefore never be brought up to date. Served by `GET /api/environments/:id/repo-divergence` → `POST /internal/environments/:id/repo-divergence` (the console resolves and passes the agent set, because scoping is its job, not the orchestrator's), rendered as a persistent badge on the Agents cell of the environment list with the catch-up behind it. It uses the **minimal** `inspectAgentRepoDivergence` probe — two git commands per repo, no network — deliberately not `inspectAgentRepoStatuses` (~7 per repo), because the badge is rendered for every agent of every environment.

**Credentials** are unchanged and now single-sourced: `repoGitCredentials(repository, envGithubTokenId)` was extracted verbatim out of `cloneRepository` so the adjust path authenticates identically (a second copy would be the one place "the clone worked but the fetch did not" could diverge). It is called after `cloneRepository`'s two early returns, so a clone that will not run no longer fails on a credential it would not have used; the HTTPS-without-a-PAT refusal keeps its original position.

**Authorisation** (AC23): `POST /api/environments/:id/sync-agents` now requires `canManageEnvironment` (it checked only `visibleTeam`, while `PUT`/`DELETE` on the same environment required team-admin), and `POST /api/agents/:id/sync-repositories` — which had **no authorisation at all** — requires `visibleAgent` + `canManageAgent`. Both mutate other people's workspaces. **D6, the trail:** `recordRepoSyncTrail` writes one `audit.record` line **per agent** per run (`action: 'repo-propagation'` + a `repoSync` block naming each repository's outcome, reason and applied operations), because `auditMutations` records one line per *request* and the streamed report vanishes when the dialog closes.

**Client.** The save-time offer is `admin-console/src/components/RepoPropagationDialog.jsx` — a modal that turns into the progress report in place (deliberately not `ConfirmDialog`, which closes on confirm), accept primary + focused so Enter confirms (D5), and it publishes `modal:env-propagation` so its "?" is not opened *behind* it. Its diff is the pure `src/pages/working-env-repo-diff.js` (`classifyRepositoryChanges` → added / changed / removed / unchanged / **collisions**, keyed on the derived directory name; `readOnly` is deliberately not a workspace field, so flipping it raises no offer), computed from **two server-supplied lists** — `previousRepositories`, echoed on the environment `PUT` — so it can never be computed against a draft this browser held since another admin saved. Stream row copy is single-sourced in `src/pages/sync-repos-view.js` (`syncEventRow`/`repoRowTone`), shared with the per-agent `SyncReposPanel`, which **stays** (D2) as the single-agent recovery path after a declined offer or a D1 skip. The environment form now offers **no** manual repository-sync affordance at all (AC1).

**Agent-scoped repositories (#2067).** A repository attached to **one agent**, outside its Working Environment — the per-agent escape hatch host context folders (#1289/#1290) already were, applied to repositories. Persisted as `agentRepositories: [{url, branch, sshKeyId, readOnly}]` on the agent's own `config.json` (the environment record is never touched, D3), edited from two surfaces that render **one** component: the **repositories** button in the Agent Chat Workspace view (D1) and the same panel in the agent's **Settings** tab next to *Sync repositories* (D7). Every rule is the pure `orchestrator/agent-repositories.js`; `orchestrator.js` is its fs half (`agentRepositoriesView`/`attachAgentRepository`/`detachAgentRepository` behind `GET`/`POST`/`DELETE /internal/agents/:id/repositories`). Six things are load-bearing. (1) **The clone convention is shared, not re-derived** — the module re-exports `repoDirName` from `repo-sync.js` rather than adding a third copy, and `cloneAgentScopedRepositories` passes `envId: null` so an agent-scoped repo neither reads from nor seeds the per-env `_repo-cache` (seeding it would let a sibling copy a working tree it was never granted, which is exactly what "changes exactly one agent" forbids). (2) **Persistence survives the wipe**: `deactivateAgent`'s reset-to-stub keeps `config.json` alone, so `agentRepositories` is added to the preserved key set and step 5 of `seedAgentWorkspace` re-clones it on the next activation (D9) — `syncAgentRepositories` heals it too, per agent, so the env fan-out can neither propagate nor drop it (AC11). (3) **The prompt gate widened**: `envHasRepository(env, agentRepositories)` and `workingEnvironmentSection(env, skills, agentRepositories)` now count both scopes, so an attachment on a **repo-less** environment (#1623) no longer arrives with the whole git / `gh-as-agent` block switched off (AC14); an agent with no attachments is byte-identical to pre-#2067. (4) **Authorization is the console's, and it is the first on this family of routes** — `canManageAgent` on both mutations (the `PATCH /api/agents/:id/context-mount` precedent), `visibleAgent` on the read (the Workspace view shows the list to a chat-only user; what that user does not get is the action). The neighbouring `/api/agents/:agentId/workspace/*` routes carried no per-agent check at the time — the pre-existing gap this ticket did not widen, and **#1459 has since closed it** with one gate in front of the whole work plane (see *Work-plane tenant isolation* below). `canManage` is added to the response **by the console**, since the orchestrator's `:4002` API never learns who is asking. (5) **No credential of its own** (D5): the clone uses the SSH-key registry or the env's GitHub token, and `credentialRefusal` refuses before any clone, naming which credential was tried; the attach body is whitelisted to the card's four fields so a client-supplied secret cannot enter the config. **Attach clones first and persists second** (a failed clone writes nothing and leaves no half-cloned dir); **detach persists first and deletes second** (a failed delete leaves a visible directory rather than an entry pointing at deleted files). (6) **Two asymmetries stated in the UI, not decided silently**: attaching during a run is safe by construction (additive, the #1342 mechanism, no respawn — `rebuildAgentPrompt` only, so the agent learns of it on its next run) while **removing** during a run is refused (`agentBusyState`, 409); and when the environment has *since* declared the same directory, detaching drops the attachment but **keeps** the working copy, because deleting it would delete a repository the environment now owns. UI: the console's one repository card is now the shared `admin-console/src/components/RepositoryCard.jsx` (extracted out of `WorkingEnvironments.jsx` (renamed `EnvironmentTemplates.jsx` by #2170), which renders it unchanged — the #1928/#1929 discipline, so D2's "the same fields, no reduced or invented form" cannot drift), the shared panel is `admin-console/src/pages/agent-chat/AgentRepositoriesPanel.jsx` over the pure `agent-repos-view.js`, and `agent-repos-wiring.test.js` is the #651 source-reading guard that both surfaces render **one** panel and that the client's availability rule states the server's four cases in the server's order.

#### System-prompt assembly

The seeded `CLAUDE.md` and a runtime `--append-system-prompt` memory hint are composed by one pure, unit-tested module — **`orchestrator/prompt-assembly.js`** — used by the seed path (`buildAgentClaudeMd`).

- **Ordering.** The authored **Agent Role is hoisted to the top** (after a short identity preamble), ahead of generic blocks (Working Environment → Workspace → Response Formatting → Memory System → Skills → GitHub Actions → Trigger management → Important Notes). Base template read from `/template-workspace/CLAUDE.md` (else `/defaults/CLAUDE.md`), split by H2, re-ordered in code.
- **Conditional gating.** Git/`gh-as-agent` only when the env has a repo; Trigger-management + unattended framing only for **autonomous** agents (`agentIsAutonomous()`: `sessionPolicy: per-invocation`, `orchestrator`/`bp-instance`, or already has triggers); Skills only when the grant is non-empty; the **Context Folder(s)** block (the single-folder *Read-only Context Folder* / *Host Context Folder* wording — #1289 — for a lone `/context` folder, or an enumerated **Context Folders** list for a multi-folder set — #1290) only when the agent carries at least one mount (#1286) — setting or clearing the mounts rebuilds + reseeds CLAUDE.md through `POST /internal/agents/:id/prompt-rebuild` before the respawn, so the prompt never advertises a folder the container lacks. Adding/removing a trigger via the Flow Editor (`PUT/POST/DELETE /api/agents/:id/triggers`) that flips `agentIsAutonomous()` rebuilds + reseeds CLAUDE.md immediately (#962, `reconcileAgentAutonomyPrompt`); a no-op edit that doesn't change the autonomy state never rebuilds.
- **Runtime hint.** `buildMemoryHint(blueprintId)` is **byte-identical** to `_build_memory_hint()` in `agent-entrypoint.py` (test-asserted; change both in one commit).

SSH keys are stored at `/workspace/environments/_keys/<keyId>` (mode 0600), metadata in `_keys/index.json`. The private key is **never** returned by the API — only its fingerprint.

#### Why both an SSH key and a GitHub PAT? (defense-in-depth)

Not redundant. SSH key → git I/O on SSH URLs (via `GIT_SSH_COMMAND`); can be a per-repo read-only deploy key. GitHub PAT → git I/O on HTTPS GitHub URLs, Events API polling, and `gh pr create`/commit/review (`gh-as-agent`, via `GH_TOKEN`/`GITHUB_TOKEN`). **Blast-radius separation:** a leaked SSH key can't merge PRs; a leaked PAT didn't grant the initial clone. For HTTPS-only repos the PAT covers both — prefer SSH URLs + deploy keys.

**Credential rotation propagates to running agents.** Spawn-time credentials (PAT from `env.githubTokenId`, per-agent SSH identity) are baked into each container at `startAgentProcess`, immutable for its lifetime. Repointing an env at a different token → `PUT /api/environments/:id` detects the `githubTokenId` change and best-effort calls `POST /internal/environments/:id/respawn-agents`, recreating every running agent bound to that env. Disabled agents pick up the fresh credential on next enable. **Personal-credential rotation** is a distinct mechanism: `POST /api/{ssh-keys,github-tokens}/:id/rotate` replaces the stored secret in place (keeping id/name/owner), and is personal-tier only and owner-gated — a team/system credential is refused (`rotate-personal-only`). **Rotation now propagates to running agents (#1570, Phase 4):** setting *or* rotating a personal SSH key / GitHub token best-effort calls `notifyOwnerCredentialChange(userId)` → `POST /internal/owners/:ownerId/respawn-agents` → `respawnAgentsForOwner`, the owner-scoped sibling of `respawnAgentsForEnvironment` — it respawns only *that human's* running agents (matched by `credential-resolution.selectAgentIdsForOwner`/`resolveAgentOwnerId`, so a team-member is matched via its parent team's owner), leaving other humans' agents and unrelated environments untouched, and skipping disabled/not-running/tokenless agents like the env path. This also closes the prior gap where an SSH-key change triggered no respawn at all. Because the respawn interrupts in-flight work, the My Credentials rotate panel warns the owner to rotate at a quiet moment. **Offboarding (#1571, Phase 5):** deactivating a human (`DELETE /internal/admin/users/:id`) now calls `orchestrator.offboardOwner(userId)` before `teamsStore.deleteUser` — it **pauses** (drains, never wipes) the departed person's agents (same `selectAgentIdsForOwner` owner match) and **auto-deletes** their personal SSH keys + GitHub tokens (index entries + 0600 secret files, via `credential-resolution.selectPersonalCredentialIds` — team/system credentials are never touched, AC3); pausing first means an in-flight attributed write that outlives the pause refuses rather than acting under a now-deleted identity, and there is deliberately no admin break-glass into the secrets before deletion (D6/D2).

**My Credentials (self-service, #1568 Phase 2).** Every authenticated human — not just a credential admin — manages their own personal SSH key and GitHub token from the **My Credentials** page (`/my-credentials`, no `credAdmin` gate). The page shows only the caller's own personal entries (re-asserted by `personalCredentials(list, myId)` in `admin-console/src/pages/my-credentials-view.js`, which re-checks `userId === me` even though the API already caller-scopes the list). Operations: **create** (paste or server-generate for SSH; paste for token; owner always forced to caller server-side via `resolveCredentialOwnership`), **list** (metadata/fingerprint only; raw secrets never returned after creation), **rename**, **rotate in place** (personal-tier only — see above), **delete**. A caller with no resolved user record cannot own personal credentials (`canOwnPersonalCredentials` fails closed) and sees an explanatory note instead of an empty page — since #2059 that is a fail-closed guard rather than a reachable state, the recordless env single-admin it was written for having stopped authenticating. The admin **SSH Keys** / **GitHub Tokens** pages filter personal credentials out of their tables via `adminManagedCredentials` (also in `my-credentials-view.js`), so the two tiers remain visually and navigationally distinct. `credential-scope-view.js` gains an `isPersonalCredential` helper and a `userId`-aware `canManageCredentialClient` that checks `userId === owner` before the `isSysAdmin` shortcut (mirrors `scope.js`).

**Personal-credential runtime resolution (#1569, Phase 3).** The pure, unit-tested decision core lives in `orchestrator/credential-resolution.js` (`resolveAgentOwnerId` / `findPersonalCredential` / `resolveCredential` / `ATTRIBUTED_OPERATIONS`). The rule is **per operation class**: **attributed writes** (commits, pull requests, reviews, HTTPS pushes — `ATTRIBUTED_OPERATIONS`) carry the human's name and prefer the owner's personal token (personal → team → system order); read-only clones, the repo-check probe, and event polling are shared-eligible and may use a team/system credential. **Refuse-on-missing (AC5):** when an attributed write's owner has no usable personal credential, the run **refuses** with a clear HTTP 424 `personal-credential-required` error — it does not silently drop to a shared identity. **`ownerId`-presence is the switch (AC8 reconciliation):** an agent with no `ownerId` — every agent today, since nothing populates it yet — takes the shared token exactly as before and is never refused; only an agent carrying an `ownerId` is subject to the personal preference + refuse-on-missing. The orchestrator wires the decision into `agentCreatePr` via `resolveAttributedGithubToken` (reads `config.ownerId` from persisted config, never the trigger initiator — AC3), which wraps the pure core with the credential-index read (`index.json` metadata only — raw secrets never loaded) and surfaces refusal as the distinct 424 error. The `_buildAgentGitEnv` signature gains an `overrides` param so the resolved personal token id (or the shared one) is threaded cleanly without altering unrelated paths.

**Re-keyed onto the acting sender for interactive runs (#2175, epic #2166 B3).** The AC3 statement above — the owner comes from persisted config, "never the trigger initiator" — now holds only for an **autonomous** run; for an interactive one it is superseded by the acting human, so a shared agent's commit/PR/push is attributed to whoever is actually chatting, not the team's creator. `resolveEffectiveOwnerId(cfg, loadAgentConfig, actingSenderUserId)` prefers a resolved acting-sender id over `resolveAgentOwnerId` and is a pure fallback — a `null` sender (no B1 identity, or an autonomous trigger's `run_context` carrying no `requestedBy`) reproduces the pre-B3 answer exactly. The sender travels **orchestrator-side only** (I1a — the secret itself never enters the container): `agent-entrypoint.py` exports the chat turn's Matrix sender as `AEE_ACTING_SENDER` (empty for a trigger-fired run), `gh-as-agent push`/`pr` forward it in the POST body (the `AEE_CORRELATION_ID` precedent) as `actingSender`, and `orchestrator.js` resolves it to a platform user id via the same `usageAttribution.userIdForMatrixId` lookup `enrichUsageRecord` (#2137) already uses for cost attribution — so a run's credential and its cost answer to the same identity resolution. `push-ssh` joins `ATTRIBUTED_OPERATIONS` alongside `push-https` now that a resolver exists to prefer a personal credential for it: the new `resolveAttributedSshKey` is the SSH-transport sibling of `resolveAttributedGithubToken`, wired into both `agentGitPush` (previously unwired for any personal-credential preference at all) and the branch-push `agentCreatePr` performs before opening the PR. The in-container `gh` calls the agent runs directly (issue/PR comments, `gh pr review`) are unaffected — still the container's spawn-time baked token — and stay explicitly out of scope, alongside the poller/claim-swap carve-out the ticket names, pending a later block of the epic. **The claim is bounded, not trusted outright (#2187 review finding).** `AEE_ACTING_SENDER` is an ordinary env var for the rest of the run once set, so any Bash call the agent makes (potentially steered by injected content) can overwrite it before `gh-as-agent` forwards whatever value is currently present. `isActingSenderTrusted(cfg, userId, hasMembership)` in `credential-resolution.js` gates every resolved sender before it ever reaches `resolveEffectiveOwnerId`: a team-bound agent trusts only its own team's members (member → parent team, the `scope.js` boundary), a standalone agent trusts only its own `ownerId`. An untrusted sender degrades to `null` — exactly like no sender at all — closing cross-team (and, for a standalone agent, cross-owner) impersonation; it does not make the claim unforgeable *within* that boundary, which would need a signed, per-run claim the container cannot re-mint.

**Team-admin baseline + just-in-time refusal for interactive writes (#2176, epic #2166 B4, depends on B3).** B3 re-keyed WHO the owner is for an interactive run but left the "owner has no personal credential" row of `resolveCredential`'s table falling to shared regardless of whether a human is actually chatting or a trigger is running unattended — B4 splits that row by `interactive`, a new flag both `resolveAttributedGithubToken`/`resolveAttributedSshKey` compute as `actingSenderUserId != null` (a resolved, *trusted* sender — see the #2187 gate above) and thread into `resolveCredential`. An **interactive** attributed write whose owner has no usable personal credential now **refuses** with the existing 424 `personal-credential-required` message, same as the present-but-unusable case always has — a human chatting right now must be told to add one, not silently write under the team's shared identity. An **autonomous** run (no sender) is unaffected by this and keeps falling to shared, because it must always have a working path — refusing a scheduled/GitHub-triggered run over a credential nobody was ever prompted to add would just be an outage with an extra step. The autonomous row's *owner* also changed: `resolveEffectiveOwnerId`'s no-sender fallback is no longer the static, immutable `resolveAgentOwnerId` but the new `resolveBaselineOwnerId` — the ticket's "**baseline identity**" — which for a team-bounded agent (`resolveAgentTeam` resolves a team id) resolves to that team's **current** `team-admin` via `resolveScopeOwner` (`ownership-migration.js`, the same earliest-admin-wins rule the #2077 D6 backfill uses), so a D4 succession is honoured without redeploying the agent; a standalone agent has no team to defer to and keeps resolving to its own static owner, unchanged. `orchestrator.js`'s `loadBaselineDeps()` loads a fresh `{ users, memberships }` snapshot per call (the `loadLiveUserGate` fail-open convention — an unreadable store degrades to the pre-B4 static-owner answer, never a refusal). **Scope note for the reviewer:** this ticket wires the baseline into the two existing ATTRIBUTED-write resolvers only (`resolveAttributedGithubToken`/`SshKey`, the same two B3 built). The read/clone/poll call sites (`env.githubTokenId` read directly at ~40 sites: seeding, the poller's per-repo token pick, `cloneRepository`) are **not** rewired to prefer the baseline credential — `resolveCredential` already implements "reads fall back to baseline" as a pure decision (a non-attributed operation never refuses and prefers personal-if-usable-else-shared, proven in `credential-resolution.test.js`), but wiring it live into every read path is the field-semantics migration B5 ("re-interpret `env.githubTokenId`... to a baseline pointer... migrate existing team/system credential bindings") explicitly owns, and doing it piecemeal here risked regressing the seeding/clone paths every agent depends on. Flagged rather than guessed, per this epic's own B3 precedent (which similarly left the poller/claim-swap credential path out of scope for a later block).

### MCP Servers

Static skills live in the workspace; **MCP servers** are how agents reach runtime data (wikis, vector stores, graph DBs). The pattern mirrors the SSH/PAT split: a *catalog* of servers, a *separate* credential store, an environment that pairs them.
