- `mcp-servers/<id>/server.js` (repo) — reference implementations baked into the image; `make seed-mcp-servers` lays them into `/template-workspace/mcp-servers/<id>/`. The shipped `wiki/` server is a single-file stdio MCP over a REST backend.
- `/workspace/mcp-servers/<id>/server.json` — operator-managed **registry**: `id, name, description, transport (stdio|http|sse)`, plus stdio `command, args, env` or http/sse `url, headers`. `env`/`headers` support `${credential}`, substituted per-agent at seed time.
- `/workspace/mcp-credentials/_keys/<credId>` — raw secret (0600, never returned); `index.json` holds metadata. Credentials are global (one token can back two environments).

**Per-agent isolation.** At seed time the orchestrator reads `env.mcpServers` (or a member's `mcpServersOverride`), substitutes `${credential}`, and writes the merged stanza into **the agent's own** `.claude/settings.json` under `mcpServers[<serverId>]` (Claude Code schema: stdio `{command, args, env}`; http/sse `{type, url, headers}`). No pool-wide MCP config. `mcpServersOverride` on a member replaces the env's MCP set for that member; absent = inherit.

### Plugins (governed capability extensions, #1516)

Agents reach the outside world (container runtimes, test-management systems, …) **only** through registered **platform plugins** (not to be confused with Claude Code "agent plugins"). A plugin pairs a privilege-holding **broker sidecar** with a dependency-free **CLI facade** seeded into the agent workspace — CLI-first (deliberately not MCP), all security server-side, "one contract, two provenances" (`docs/cli-contract.md`). Concept: `docs/plugin-system-concept.md`. Built in phases (#1517 core machinery → #1522); PR #1287's `podman-mcp` broker becomes plugin #1 in Phase 3.

**Phase 1 core machinery (#1517) — pure, unit-tested modules:**
- `admin-console/api/plugin-manifest.js` — validator/normalizer for a `pluginVersion: 1` manifest (the `process-template.js` discipline). `sidecar.capabilities` is a **closed enum** (`podman-socket`, `egress`, `host-mount:<path>` — a plugin cannot request an arbitrary mount); `scopes` declares the granularities the broker honours (`["team"]` or `["team","agent"]`, must include `team`) and bounds a grant's choice (`assertGranularityGrantable`); optional `defaultScopeGranularity` (within `scopes`) is the **per-manifest** grant default (`defaultGranularity()` → the field, else `team`; the §10 decision, #1519); optional `quota: { maxObjects, ttlSeconds? }` is the **per-scope** resource cap (§4.6); `ui.routes[].minRole` is **validated + reserved** in Phase 1, the proxy/role enforcement lands in Phase 5. **#1798 extension:** `commands?: { namespace? }` declares a **command-only plugin** (no `sidecar`/`cli` needed — grants provision `.claude/commands/*` files via #1796's path, not a broker); `fetch?: { source, repo, ref, commandsDir? }` declares install-time content fetching (currently `source: "github"` only — the install route downloads `commands/*.md` files from GitHub Contents API at install time and writes `installed-version.json`); byte-identical mirror in `orchestrator/plugin-manifest.js`.
- `orchestrator/plugin-scope-token.js` — generalises `podman-token.js` to HMAC-signed `{pluginId, scopeId}` tokens (broker verifies on every call).
- `orchestrator/plugin-scope.js` — `resolveScopeId(agentCfg, granularity)` (**both granularities from the start**: `team` = team for members / self for standalone; `agent` = the member's own id) + ownership-label helpers (`aee.plugin.owner=<scopeId>` stamp/filter). Granularity multiplies *tokens*, never backend credentials; teardown keys on `scopeId` so deleting one member at `agent` granularity leaves siblings untouched.
- `orchestrator/plugin-sidecar.js` — pure lifecycle planners: `reconcileSidecars` (start installed / stop orphaned sidecars — `aee.plugin.id` label), `reconcileAttachments` (grant-driven per-agent network connect/disconnect — reachability matches grant truth), `capabilityAttachments` (closed-enum → concrete attachments). The impure libpod calls wrap these in the orchestrator runtime.

**Phase 3 — plugin #1 (#1519): the podman sandbox.** `plugins/podman-sandbox/` reworks PR #1287's `podman-mcp` broker onto the plugin machinery: manifest (`podman-socket` capability, `scopes: ["team","agent"]`, `defaultScopeGranularity: "agent"` — the §10 decision, since a sandbox reads as per-agent — and a per-scope `quota` replacing #1287's global container cap) + the dependency-free `aee-podman` CLI facade (`aee-podman run <image>` — single container, no compose file needed; `aee-podman compose up -f compose.yml` — multi-service stack; both route through `compose_up` so `run` is pure CLI sugar with no new broker tool, inheriting validation / isolation / quota identically; contract §4.5, driven by `orchestrator/plugin-cli-contract.test.js`). The broker sidecar source lives at `podman-mcp/` (kept nearly unchanged from #1287; its `owner-scope.js` stamps `aee.mcp-owner=<scopeId>` on every container so scopes can't see each other's). No podman-specific code sits in orchestrator seeding / prompt-assembly.js / Platform Settings (#1287 never merged there). The live-stack remainder (broker verifying the plugin scope-token + enforcing the manifest quota, orchestrator sidecar lifecycle + socket mount + token mint) is the Phase-3 completion step — same "pure core now, live stack next" split as Phases 1–2.

**Phase 4 — Broker SDK (#1520): `broker-sdk/`.** The obligations every broker owes (§4.6/§4.9), packaged as one dependency-free Node module a broker sidecar imports so each plugin author doesn't re-implement — and re-break — them: `verifyScopeToken(reqOrHeader, secret)` → `{pluginId, scopeId}` (**granularity-agnostic** — the broker never learns whether a `scopeId` is a team or a single agent, and must not branch on it; byte-identical contract to `orchestrator/plugin-scope-token.js`), `scopeFilter(scopeId)` (ownership-label stamp/filter/exact-match on the platform label `aee.plugin.owner`), `auditWriter(dir, {retentionMs})` (JSONL-per-call fs writer over the pure `plugin-audit.js` core), `maskSecrets(output, secrets)` (response redaction — explicit secrets + the `-e/--env KEY=VALUE` pass), `healthEndpoint()` / `teardownScopeEndpoint(reap, {secret})` (the two plain-REST routes the orchestrator calls on every broker; teardown is per `scopeId`, so **one** handler serves both team deletion and single-member deletion, and **resources are reaped before the orchestrator invalidates the token**), and `parkForApproval(op)` / `approvalHoldingPen({requiresApproval})` (the `requiresApproval` holding pen, v1 DM-confirm). **Extracted from plugin #1's broker, not designed on paper** — the retrofit repoints `podman-mcp/server.js` at the SDK and **deletes its own `podman-token.js` / `owner-scope.js` / `mask-env.js` copies**; two generalisations ride along (the 3-field platform scope token, and the platform owner label `aee.plugin.owner` in place of `aee.mcp-owner`), container-isolation behaviour otherwise unchanged. SDK use is **recommended, not enforced** — the platform verifies the wire contract, not the implementation. The broker's Docker build context vendors `broker-sdk/` into the image (its context is the repo root). Pure-module discipline, `node --test` covered (forgery, cross-scope isolation at both granularities, secret echo, teardown ordering).

**Phase 5 — plugin #2 (#1521): test management, on the SDK.** `plugins/test-mgmt/` + broker `plugins/test-mgmt/broker/` — a governed test-management capability (Xray/Zephyr/qTest) proving a credential-backed broker is thin: the broker `require`s `../../../broker-sdk` and adds only the credential-holding `vendor-client.js` (the one vendor token lives here, never reaching the agent — why it is a plugin, not a skill) + `broker-policy.js`. The dependency-free `aee-test` CLI facade drives it (`aee-test create-case …`, `aee-test trigger --plan REGRESSION --wait` submit→poll; contract §4.5, in `plugin-cli-contract.test.js`). Three firsts: (1) **authorisation by granularity** — the new manifest `policy` field (`admin-console/api/plugin-manifest.js`) declares per-subcommand rules keyed on the calling scope, enforced by the SDK `authorizeAction`/`isActionPermitted` (`broker-sdk/authz.js`) — `create-case: { scopes: ["agent"], requiresFlowFinal: true }` means only the flow-final agent whose cross-checks passed may create test cases; a team-scoped token cannot express that. (2) the first real **`requiresApproval`** flow — destructive `data-delete` is parked in the SDK approval pen for DM confirm. (3) the **UI-extension surface** decidable core (`admin-console/api/plugin-ui-proxy.js`): `deriveScopeList` (scope discovery derived from the resolved grant — a plugin enumerates only scopes it was granted to), `enforceUiRoute` (`ui.routes[].minRole` refusal), `signViewerHeaders`/`verifyViewerHeaders` (signed viewer identity + effective role) — reserved + unit-tested at this phase; the live iframe proxy/nav were later wired by the Network Plan epic's Phase 2 (#1562, `admin-console/api/plugin-ui-viewer.js`). The orchestrator live-stack (token mint, scope-registry push to the broker, sidecar lifecycle, DM approval round-trip) is the Phase-5 completion step — same "pure core now, live stack next" split as Phases 1–3.

**Network Plan epic (#1554), Phase 3 — plugin #3 (#1563): netzplan, the planning board.** `plugins/netzplan/` + broker `plugins/netzplan/broker/` — the first **UI-bearing** plugin and the first **privilege-less** plugin (`capabilities: []`, `credentials: []`). Manifest: `scopes: ["team"]` (one board per team), `platforms: ["podman","kubernetes"]`, `ui.routes` `/` @ `team-member` + `/admin` @ `team-admin`, `quota.maxObjects`, `cli: aee-plan`. The dependency-free `aee-plan` CLI drives it with a three-rung autonomy ladder: `show` (autonomy 0 — read-only, always allowed) · `tick <milestoneId>` (≥1) · `edit '<op-json>'` (≥2); the broker re-enforces the level authoritatively and `aee-plan` refuses early from a cached `show`. The broker (`plugins/netzplan/broker/`) is built on the Broker SDK: **`plan-doc.js`** is the pure load-bearing document model — statuses and requirement targets key on **milestone ids, never array indices**, so insert/reorder/delete of milestones are non-destructive; pure `normalize`/`validate`/`migrate`/`mutate` functions with immutable return values (undo/redo is a stack of documents). **`plan-store.js`** is the thin fs adapter: one board per scope on `AEE_PLUGIN_STATE_DIR` (the generic state volume — not the audit mount), atomic writes via rename, autonomy-gated `mutate`, `teardown`, and SDK `count` for quota. **`viewer-headers.js`** copies the §4.8 signed-viewer-header scheme from `admin-console/api/plugin-ui-proxy.js` (not imported — broker build context can't reach it; SDK home deferred). **`server.js`** surfaces `plan_show`/`plan_tick`/`plan_mutate` as MCP tools for `aee-plan`, serves the board UI, and wires the SDK endpoints. **Phase 4 (#1564)** completes the board: `plan-doc.js` now carries all four §5 primitives — `swimlane` plus **`graph`** (typed nodes in bands + dependency edges, with a `graphNeighbors` hover-highlight helper), **`bipartite`** (left/right entries + typed, muteable edges) and **`blocks`** (cover / KPI-tiles / callout / cards as flexible typed blocks) — each with id-keyed normalize/mutate + referential repair, plus panel composition (`add-panel`/`delete-panel`/`reorder-panels`/`set-panel-title`). Every new mutation is structural → `requiredAutonomy` 2, gated by the one existing `plan-store.js` path (no primitive gets its own authorisation). The served board renders all four primitives and its **Edit** mode adds/edits/reorders/removes panels and their contents through a new human-write `/api/mutate` route (role-gated, not autonomy-gated; `set-autonomy` stays the `/admin`-only `/api/settings` route). The direct-manipulation polish (drag reorder, colour pickers, requirement-target-by-click, undo/redo — a stack of the immutable documents `applyMutation` returns) is a UI refinement over the same mutations.

**Registry:** reference plugins ship under `plugins/<id>/` (`plugin.json` + `bin/`); `make seed-plugins` (chained from `seed-pool`) lays them into `/pool/plugins/`. **Installing** (`POST /api/plugins/:id/install`, System-Admin-only, from the **Plugins** page `/plugins`) copies one into the operator registry `/workspace/plugins/<id>/` — the same two-volume move as skill promote-to-global — and that is what makes the orchestrator start its broker sidecar. Install is a deliberate human action, not something seeding does: a sidecar holds real privilege (plugin #1 mounts the host Podman socket), so the declared capabilities are reviewed in plain language before the confirm. Installing still **grants nothing** to any agent. `plugins/dummy-echo/` is a no-op reference plugin — a safe smoke test for plugin install, sidecar lifecycle and grant-driven network wiring, driven with the dependency-free `aee-echo` CLI; `plugins/podman-sandbox/` is plugin #1 (Phase 3, running on the Phase 4 Broker SDK); `plugins/test-mgmt/` is plugin #2 (Phase 5); `plugins/netzplan/` is plugin #3 (Network Plan epic, #1563 Phase 3 + #1564 Phase 4 — all four §5 primitives editable + #1566 Phase 6 — content-migration import/export); `plugins/patterns-catalog/` is the UI-only, privilege-less **reference-catalog** plugin (`capabilities: []`, `credentials: []`, `scopes: ["team"]`, one `ui.route` `/` @ `team-member`) — the former built-in **Patterns Catalog** console page (SDLC + Mainframe Modernization catalogs) re-shipped as a plugin so it is off by default and re-activated per install by install + team grant; its broker `plugins/patterns-catalog/broker/` just serves static pages (no MCP, no state), and the dependency-free `aee-patterns` CLI lists the catalogs. **Global UI visibility (#1599):** its manifest sets the opt-in `ui.global: true` flag, so once installed its UI nav entry + general route are visible/usable to **every** authenticated user without a team grant (a human-UI concern only — agent CLI access still comes solely from a team grant). See `plugin-ui-viewer.js` in Key Files below and §4.8 of `docs/plugin-system-concept.md`.

**Phase 7 — live stack (#1547).** Phases 1–6 shipped pure cores and deferred their live half; this collects it and proves it on plugin #1. **Registry** `orchestrator/plugin-registry.js` reads + re-validates `/workspace/plugins/<id>/plugin.json` (the validator is duplicated as `orchestrator/plugin-manifest.js`, byte-identical to the admin-console canonical, two-sided-guard tested) and owns the shared HMAC signing secret at `/workspace/plugins/_secret` (0600). **Sidecar lifecycle** `orchestrator/plugin-runtime.js` is the impure adapter over the Phase-1 planners: `reconcilePluginSidecars` (boot + `POST /internal/plugins/reconcile`), privileges derived *only* from `capabilityAttachments` (`podman-socket` → a bind of the **host** path `PODMAN_HOST_SOCKET`, now passed into the orchestrator service; `host-mount:` → read-only), a **plugin-neutral** env contract (`AEE_PLUGIN_ID`/`_PORT`/`_SIGNING_SECRET`/`_QUOTA_*`/`_AUDIT_DIR`, read by brokers through the new `broker-sdk/broker-config.js`, so no plugin-specific variable name reaches the generic orchestrator; a manifest's own `sidecar.env` can never override them), and grant-driven `reconcilePluginAttachments`. **Grant → workspace** `setTeamPluginActive`/`setAgentPluginActive` + `POST /internal/{teams,agents}/:id/plugins/{activate,deactivate}` (console proxy `POST /api/agents/:id/plugins/{activate,deactivate}`) run `planWorkspaceSync` as an **additive file sync, no respawn** — exactly the skill-activation pattern — writing `.agent-studio/bin/<cli>` (0755) + `.agent-studio/plugins/<id>.json` (0600). **Making a granted CLI runnable** takes two things the image cannot bake in, because a plugin CLI is seeded per agent at grant time, not at build time. (1) **PATH**: the image puts a fixed dir `/opt/aee/plugin-bin` on `PATH` and the entrypoint symlinks it at the per-agent `<workspace>/.agent-studio/bin` — PATH resolves through the link on every lookup, so a plugin granted minutes later is instantly runnable with no respawn. Deliberately a symlink, not a mutated `os.environ["PATH"]`: an env the entrypoint changes reaches its children (the Claude CLI and its shell calls) but is **invisible to `podman exec`**, so an operator debugging a granted plugin would get "command not found" for a CLI that is installed. (2) **`AEE_WORKSPACE`** — which every CLI facade reads to locate its own `.agent-studio/plugins/<id>.json` — is set on the **container** by `startAgentProcess` for the same reason (unset, a granted CLI looks in `/root` and reports itself ungranted). **Prompt**: `buildAgentClaudeMd` now resolves the grant (`agentPluginDisplays`) and passes it to the already-existing `plugins` param of `prompt-assembly.js`; preview and seed stay byte-identical. **Teardown** runs before the config is deleted (§4.9), and at `team` granularity only when no sibling still holds the same `scopeId`. **UI**: `/plugins` registry page (`Plugins.jsx` over the pure `plugin-registry-view.js`) + a `PluginGrantPicker` on the agent edit form (over the Phase-2 `plugin-grant-view.js`) that applies **immediately** rather than on Save — that form was the Agents page's then; since #1929/#1930 it is `agent-lifecycle/AgentEditForm.jsx`, hosted by Agent Chat's **Settings** tab. **Team assignment from the Plugins page (#1691):** each *installed* plugin card also carries a `PluginTeamAssign` control (over the pure `plugin-team-assign-view.js`) that assigns the plugin to one or more **teams** directly — team scope only, closing the discoverability gap where granting used to live solely on the Agents page. It reuses the **same** activation path (`POST /api/agents/:teamId/plugins/{activate,deactivate}`, team construct — no new grant mechanism/data model) and applies immediately; grantable teams are the `role: 'team'` agents from the team-scoped `GET /api/agents` (no team → an explanatory hint, not an error); a grant reuses the manifest's `defaultScopeGranularity` and honours the platform-mismatch gate. The card shows which teams currently carry the persisted team-level `plugins[]` grant (member `pluginsOverride` is untouched). The per-agent `PluginGrantPicker` is unchanged — it rode the edit form out of the Agents page and is reached from Agent Chat's **Settings** tab since #1930. The concept/help material that a standalone `/plugin-system` page used to carry (#1620/#1638) now lives in the Plugins help topic — see the context-help E5 note below. **Kubernetes (#1565).** Plugin sidecars now run on Kubernetes for **UI-bearing plugins**: `spawn-kubernetes-plugin.js` (a sibling behind the `container-runtime.js` seam, same raw-HTTPS `k8sRequest` style as `spawn-kubernetes-pod.js`) creates a **Deployment + Service + state PVC** per installed plugin, and `plugin-runtime.js` delegates `reconcilePluginSidecars`/`reconcilePluginAttachments`/`listLiveSidecars` to it when `AEE_CONTAINER_RUNTIME=kubernetes` (the rest of the surface — `mintScopeToken`/`brokerBaseUrl`/`teardownScope` — is runtime-agnostic because the Service is named identically to the Podman container, so `http://aee-plugin-<id>:<port>` resolves on both). The state volume is a **PVC not `emptyDir`** (an `emptyDir` would silently lose the board on pod restart); the audit trail is an `emptyDir` here (a per-plugin audit PVC on k8s is a separable follow-up). RBAC (`deploy/k8s/base/core/10-rbac.yaml`) gains namespaced `apps/deployments` + `services` + `persistentvolumeclaims` (+ `networkpolicies`, reserved). **State-PVC reinstall race (#1698):** because the per-plugin ReadWriteOnce state PVC deletes asynchronously on GKE (PD detach ~1–2 min), a quick uninstall→reinstall used to 409-skip the create against the still-`Terminating` PVC and then wedge the new pod on a PVC that finished deleting (FailedScheduling → Pending forever). `ensureStatePvc` now treats a `Terminating` PVC as *absent* — it polls until the old PVC is fully gone (404) before creating a fresh one — and `reconcilePluginSidecars` self-heals an already-live broker whose state PVC went missing (recreates it), so a wedged sidecar recovers on the next reconcile instead of needing a manual uninstall. **Still Podman-only** — grant-driven per-agent-network reachability that a **broker** plugin needs becomes per-grant `NetworkPolicy` objects, a larger separable mechanism: a UI-only plugin needs none (the console reaches its Service in-namespace, no agent talks to it), so `reconcilePluginAttachments` is a no-op on k8s and a broker plugin's CLI stays at exit 3 there. `capabilities: ["podman-socket"]` has no k8s equivalent, so `plugins/podman-sandbox/` stays `platforms: ["podman"]` permanently (the `platforms` field gates it). `make plugin-images` builds the broker images (chained from `make build`, deliberately **not** from `make up`).

**Phase 2 agent surface (#1518) — pure, unit-tested modules:**
- `orchestrator/plugin-grants.js` — the plugin GRANT, sibling of `skill-grants.js` (same skill-grant doctrine: the grant lives on the agent construct, never on the env). `plugins[]` on team agents (inherited), `pluginsOverride` on members, own list on standalone/orchestrator, inherit on bp-instance. `effectivePlugins(cfg, loadCfg)` resolves it; each entry normalises to **`{ id, scopeGranularity }`** (`"team"` default / `"agent"`; a bare string is the team shorthand). `planAgentPluginGrantEdit` upserts keyed on id — activating an already-granted plugin at a **different** granularity is a granularity flip (entry replaced in place); `planPluginSync` fans a team grant out per member (`skip-override`/`sync-override`/`stub` like `planSkillSync`).
- `orchestrator/plugin-sync.js` — the workspace seed/sync **planner**: grant → `.agent-studio/bin/<cli>` (0755) + `.agent-studio/plugins/<id>.json` (0600 — endpoint + minted scope token, the shape the CLI reads) + network connect; revoke → remove both files + network disconnect. A **granularity flip** re-mints the token for the new `scopeId` and rewrites ONLY the config — additive, no orphan-removal, network kept (resources under the old `scopeId` keep their owner label and are reaped by TTL/teardown; §4.6). Impure fs/net wraps this in the runtime (skill-sync pattern, no respawn). **#1798:** command-only plugins (no `manifest.cli`) are silently skipped by the planner — their command file projection happens in `_syncPluginIntoWorkspace` (orchestrator.js) using `projectSkillCommands`.
- `orchestrator/plugin-prompt.js` — the gated **Plugin Capabilities** CLAUDE.md section, built by `pluginCapabilitiesSection(plugins)` from the resolved grant (never env contents), one `promptLine` per plugin (fail-soft generic line otherwise). Wired into `prompt-assembly.js` right after Skills (new `plugins` param), so a grant flip that reruns prompt-rebuild adds/drops exactly the changed lines.
- `orchestrator/plugin-audit.js` — the broker **audit obligation** shape: `buildPluginAuditLine({ts,pluginId,scopeId,agentId,subcommand,outcome})` (one JSONL line per call) + parse/retention. At `agent` granularity `scopeId` and `agentId` coincide — both kept, not deduped, so a line reads the same at either granularity.
- `plugins/CONTRACT.md` + `orchestrator/plugin-cli-contract.test.js` — the **CLI contract** (§4.5: dependency-free single file, `--json` per subcommand, exit 0/1/2/3, `--help`, submit→poll) enforced end-to-end by driving the `dummy-echo` python CLI.
- `admin-console/src/pages/plugin-grant-view.js` — pure **grant-UI** decisions (admin-console-src CI job): `granularitySelectorState` (disabled + honest hint when the manifest offers only `["team"]`), `quotaCeiling` (per-scope footprint stated before the toggle — `agent` × member count), `platformMismatch` (`platforms` vs stack runtime), `pluginGrantRow`. The JSX grant panel is a thin renderer over these; server re-enforces `assertGranularityGrantable`.

### Agent Roles

- `agent` — standalone. Responds to DMs in its 1:1 room; with cron/GitHub triggers it can run autonomously.
- `team` — a reusable system-prompt template + shared Working Environment; hosts no agent itself. **Add Member** (or the wizard) spawns `team-member` agents.
- `team-member` — spawned from a team. Inherits the team's `systemPromptTemplate`, the team agent's `skills[]`, and the env's `mcpServers`; records `parentTeamId`. May override `skillsOverride` / `mcpServersOverride` — absence = inherit. **System-prompt mode (#1511)** has three states (`orchestrator/prompt-modes.js` ⇄ byte-identical `admin-console/api/prompt-modes.js`, browser copy `admin-console/src/pages/prompt-mode-view.js`; `resolvePromptMode`/`composeMemberPrompt`): **Inherit from team** (template only); **Additional input** (the team template followed by the member's own text in a delimited `## Agent-specific instructions` section — stored as the addition alone in `config.systemPrompt` + `promptMode: "additional"`); **Exclusive overriding input** (the renamed "Custom" — the member's own text fully replaces the template, legacy config shape kept so existing custom members are byte-identical). Editing the team's `systemPromptTemplate` eagerly rewrites every inheriting **and** every additional member (the latter re-composed team + addition, `updateTeamPrompt`); an exclusive member is untouched. Skill/MCP changes apply at the next member rebuild. **Team deletion cascades (#1960).** Deleting the `team` agent deletes every member first — materialized members **and** stubs — and also does so when the parent's own config is unreadable, so an interrupted delete finishes on the retry rather than stranding the members it never reached (the DELETE route 404s on a missing dir, so that state is otherwise unreachable). Which children is the pure `cascadeChildIds` in `agent-delete-plan.js`; a non-team parent never cascades siblings, because a member naming one is a config error the **orphan scan** reports rather than a licence to delete. A member the cascade could not remove is now **reported**, not just logged: `cascadeWarnings: [{ id, kind, error }]` (the team fan-out's `rollbackWarnings` shape) rides the orchestrator's 200, the console relays it on a 200 body (a clean delete is still a bare 204, so existing callers are byte-identical), and both delete surfaces render it through the shared pure `cascadeResidueNotice`. The **one-off cleanup** for pre-existing orphans is System-Admin-only and operator-invoked: `GET /api/agents/orphans` is the dry run, `POST /api/agents/orphans/cleanup` acts (re-plans, logs the identical listing *before* the first deletion, reports `planned`/`deleted`/`warnings`, idempotent, slow reaping backgrounded like the DELETE route). Report-and-**delete**, not adopt — a member's prompt, skills and grants all came from the team. One class is deliberately report-only: `parent-not-a-team` (the named parent exists but is not a `team`) lands in `reportOnly` and is never swept, because its parent is still there to point at, so correcting the id is a human call; only `parent-missing` / `no-parent-id` are `removable`. Consequently the Agents page's "Orphaned agents" block no longer listed team members and got no replacement surface (the bp-instance arm stayed — that model was removed separately); #1930 then took the block with the whole page, so the operator cleanup above is the only orphan surface there is. The reason is recorded in `help-topics/agent-chat.md` (the *Where the Agents page went* chapter — `help-topics/agents.md` was folded into it by #1930) + `docs/agent-roles.md` and guarded by `src/pages/team-member-orphans-wiring.test.js` and `api/orphan-routes.test.js` (the latter pins `/api/agents/orphans` above the parameterised `/api/agents/:id`, which Express would otherwise swallow).
- `orchestrator` — bound to a BriefingPack dir (`briefingsPath`). On creation and each ~30s claim tick it pulls main (60s-throttled), scans the dir, writes one stub `bp-instance` per BP. On a BP → `status: dispatched` it DMs a suggestion; on user confirmation it `curl`s `/internal/agents/:id/claim` (patches `brief.md`, commits+pushes, materialises the bp-instance). Manual Activate / Reset to stub bypasses the suggest flow.
- `bp-instance` — one per Briefing Pack under an orchestrator. A stub (no Matrix user, no workspace) until claimed/activated; bound to one BP, uses the ACE engineering skill to execute its LoopScript.

> **Legacy roles.** `expert`/`expert-group`/`expert-instance` → `agent`/`team`/`team-member` (and `parentGroupId` → `parentTeamId`) on startup and on read.

### Autonomous Agent Triggers

Triggers live in a per-agent `/workspace/agents/<id>/triggers.json`; the scheduler syncs every 60s (`node-cron`). **Self-managed** (the agent can edit its own), **file-based sync**, **per-trigger overlap protection** (a still-running trigger skips the next tick), and **agent-wide serialization** (#1157): an agent has one workspace, so at most one workspace-touching run — a loop iteration, any trigger, or a chat/DM turn — executes at a time. All three paths pass through one `WorkspaceGate` (`agent-entrypoint.py`); while a loop runs it holds the workspace across **all** its iterations and every other trigger and chat message queues (FIFO) until the whole loop ends (completion or user Stop). The pending-drain pauses while a loop is active so `http`/`github-event` work stays durably queued; chat backlog is bounded (`AEE_MAX_QUEUED_CHAT`, default 8); Stop stays out-of-band.

- `cron` (`cron`, 5-field) — recurring. `once` (`at`, ISO) — one-shot, auto-removed after execution.
- `http` (`secret`, auto-gen) — fired by external HTTP POST (see Webhook Server).
- `github-event` (`repo` `owner/name`, `events` e.g. `pull_request.opened`, optional exact-match `labelFilter`, optional `claim: {remove, add, postcondition?}`) — fired by the GitHub poller. **No source/origin filtering** — fires on any matching event regardless of cause. Agent gets the `prompt` + full event payload as JSON. **`claim` field (#957/#958):** when set, the runner mechanically swaps the wake label for a claim label (`remove` → `add`, e.g. `"ready"` → `"ready:wip"`) after the preCheck gate and before the Claude invoke — making work-in-progress visible on GitHub. On a **successful** completion the claim is swapped forward `<wake>:wip` → `<wake>:completed` (#1163), so a finished run stops looking in-progress — the three states (`ready` / `ready:wip` / `ready:completed`) stay visibly distinct. **`postcondition` (#2113):** an optional claim field (`merged` | `review-submitted` | `pr-exists`) that gates the forward swap on an **observable** GitHub fact — a `merged` label needs the linked PR merged *and* its issue closed, `review-submitted` a submitted review, `pr-exists` a PR to exist. When declared, a run that finishes but whose condition does not hold keeps `:wip` rather than completing; verification fails **closed** (an unreadable GitHub or an unrecognised kind also stays `:wip`), and a run blocked this way is measured a failure, not a success. Omitting it is fully backwards-compatible and costs no GitHub call. The channel port carries it as `channels[].postcondition` (the strictest wins when several consolidate onto one label); it is deliberately absent from the generated `## Outcomes` prose (a claim the agent is *asked* to verify just moves "the agent forgot" one step earlier). Spec: `docs/process-template-format.md`. On final double-failure the swap is instead rolled back (`add` → `remove`), which re-emits a fresh `issues.labeled` event and restarts the chain automatically; a user-aborted run keeps its `:wip` (no completed swap, no rollback). A claim label missing in the repo (`gh` doesn't auto-create labels) is created on the fly and the swap retried once (the `:completed` label auto-creates green, distinct from the `:wip` amber). Best-effort — a failed swap is logged and never gates the run. **Convention enforced:** `add` must equal `<labelFilter>:wip` (the bundle validator rejects anything else so Layer-3 reconciliation can always derive the claim label). Channel-derived label listeners get their claim auto-stamped by `expandGithubChannels`; set `autoClaim: false` on the channel port to opt out. **`transport: 'internal'` (#1173):** the listener is excluded from the poller plan (`pollerSubscribes`) and woken directly by `POST /internal/flow-emit` (producer CLI `aee-emit`) — enqueue first, then the label is mirrored onto GitHub as decoration-only status; the plan exclusion is the single dedup mechanism. Payload is synthesized in poller shape, so prompts/claim-swap/anomaly detector work unchanged; throttle/burst layers don't apply. Human gates and PR-anchored triggers stay poller-delivered. Authored via the **Delivery** selector on label-edge dialogs (Flow Editor) and — on an existing listener — the shared `TriggerForm` (Flow-Editor Inputs-row edit + Agent-Chat trigger editor, #1189, gated to label listeners, hidden for human gates); `transport` round-trips through the bundle (export/materialise/Edit-as-draft), rides on graph edges (dotted stroke), and flips the producer's Outcomes-block instruction to `aee-emit`. **Labelled PR/issue channels (#1194):** a labelled, non-human channel port may also carry `transport: 'internal'` (Delivery control on `EditChannelDialog` + the Routing → Inputs channel row) — its derived `<artifact>.labeled` listener is stamped internal by `expandGithubChannels` when **every** channel consolidating onto that `(artifact,label)` listener opted in (all-or-nothing, so a lone poller producer isn't dropped by the plan exclusion). Unlabelled channels (`*.opened` — artifact creation) and human gates stay poller-delivered. **Defaults (#1175):** the Flow-Weaver weaves every generated intra-stage label listener internal (stage-gate channels stay poller-delivered); the curated SDLC templates ship their agent→agent listeners internal; the advisory `internal-channel-candidate` info lint suggests the toggle on remaining poller-delivered agent→agent hand-offs; the anomaly detector's `wake-not-claimed` message is transport-aware (internal = durable queue, so it points at the consumer, not a lost event). Concept: `docs/internal-channels-concept.md`.
- `wake-agent` (`prompt`) — **direct agent-to-agent hand-off (#2030):** carries the consumer's wake instruction for a `wakeAgent` outcome action (see `docs/process-template-format.md`). Like `transport: 'internal'` it is queue-fired and never poller-subscribed — but it is a distinct mechanism, not a label at all: no issue/PR, no GitHub trace. The producer runs `aee-wake --to <roleId> [--note "…"]` → `POST /internal/wake-agent`, where the orchestrator resolves + authorizes a **same-team** target and enqueues a `kind: 'wake-agent'` entry in that target's `pending-triggers.json` (`orchestrator/wake-agent.js`, pure sibling of `flow-emit.js`; unknown role → `404`, cross-team/self → `403` — an integrity constraint on the flow graph, since the producer id is self-asserted in the body and — even with the #1456 gate authenticating the *caller* — is still not cross-checked against it, **not** authentication). The consumer runs its **own** configured wake instruction as the prompt; the producer's `note` (≤1024 code points, truncated not rejected) rides in as untrusted context inside an `<external-agent-handoff>` envelope, never as the instruction. Nothing schedules this type and `pollerSubscribes` excludes it — it fires only from the queue (`_execute_wake_agent`, through the ordinary `_execute` chokepoint so it inherits the #1157 `WorkspaceGate` and the #611 backstop; #506 marker makes a crash between dequeue and completion detectable). One `source: 'internal'` event-log row per hand-off is the whole audit trail. **Authored in the Flow Editor (#2031):** the hand-off is a producer→target edge with **no label token** (`on: 'wake'`, indigo + solid, an `↪ <Target>` chip — `⇥` was unavailable, being the claim marker already on that canvas), drawn from `wakeAgent` outcome actions and drag-authored via the *Hand off directly* tile; its Edit dialog carries the target role and the note and deliberately **no** label field and **no** Delivery selector (a direct hand-off is internal by construction). The consumer's listener is **derived, never authored**: `expandWakeHandoffs` (+ `reconcileTeamWakeHandoffs` for a live team) stamps one `wakeHandoff: true` trigger per CONSUMER — one per agent, not per producer, because the queue entry carries no `triggerId` and the runner takes the first enabled one — and a hand-authored listener suppresses the derivation. **Readable and linted (#2032):** the event-log row was GitHub-event-shaped and rendered as dashes with the distinguishing `source` never shown, so three cells now carry it — the Event column reads *"direct hand-off"* through the shared `eventLabel` (`handoff.wake` lives in a separate `INTERNAL_EVENT_LABELS` map, **not** in `GITHUB_EVENT_OPTIONS`, which is the trigger editor's `<select>` and must never offer an unsubscribable key), the single `delivered → <target>` outcome chip carries the target, and the otherwise-empty **Title** column carries the producer's note. That last one needed a *writer* change too: `buildEventEntry` extracts only GitHub's own vocabulary (`label`/`ref`/`workItem`), so the note never reached the log — it now rides in a `handoff: {from, to, note?}` block written alongside `source`/`emittedBy` (`buildWakeLogFields`), flattened onto the row's `workItemTitle` key so DataTable's key-based filter and sort reach it (pure decisions in `admin-console/src/pages/github-event-handoff-view.js`). Both halves of the pending-queue projection learned the kind (`summarizeEntry` in `orchestrator/pending-queue.js` → `from` + a `QUEUE_NOTE_PREVIEW_MAX`-bounded note preview, the full entry being one `GET /internal/agents/:id/pending/:entryId` away; `pending-queue-view.js` → *Direct hand-off* + `entryDetail`), or a queued hand-off read as *"wake-agent — An unrecognised queue entry"*. **A statement about work, not a debugger line (#2101):** `summarizeEntry` now also stamps `workItem` (which PR/issue is waiting, always present and `null` for a work-item-less event, via the *same* `extractWorkItem` the Processed events tab uses — single-sourced out of `github-event-log.js` into the new pure fs-free `orchestrator/github-work-item.js`, which the log writer now re-exports so the two projections cannot disagree), and the list gains **Work item** + **Title** columns reusing the Processed tab's own `TypeCell`/`WorkItemCell`/`TitleCell` (columns are now **Kind · Work item · Title · Details · Received**). The repository and the GitHub event id left the Details string for its hover (`entryHoverDetail`, spelling the event id out deliberately *without* a `#` so an internal id no longer reads as a ticket number), the event key now reads in plain language through the shared `eventLabel`, and the carried label renders as a chip (`labelChip` decoding the mechanical `:wip`/`:completed` claim suffix into *in progress*/*done* beside the bare token). Two more live kinds finally got labels — `handover-dm` → *Hand-over message*, `loop-verify` → *Loop verification* — with `pending-queue-wiring.test.js` ratcheting `KIND_LABELS` against the kinds the runtime actually drains (`bp-event-batch` stays deliberately unlabelled, #1962/#1964). The LLM authoring paths learned the action type: `flow-copilot-prompt.md` gains a hand-off bullet + few-shot, and the outcome suggester offers `wakeAgent` **only** when the caller threads a peer list (`wakePeerList`, filtered on `(p.kind || 'agent') === 'agent'` so the actor pseudo-nodes and human roles are dropped) — the same list becomes `parseOutcomeSuggestion`'s `agents` source, so an invented target sinks the whole suggestion back to the heuristic instead of persisting an edge into nothing. Four advisory lints (`wake-target-disabled`, `wake-target-unresolved`, `wake-without-note`, `direct-handoff-candidate` — see concept §12.1 for why each is scoped as it is; the disabled one is deliberately narrower than "has no listener", since that listener is derived). Both open questions are settled: **no pair-level hand-off throttle** (the #611 consumer-side backstop stays the only limiter — the pair-level one was wanted for diagnosis quality, which the readable row now supplies; §8 carries the re-open condition) and **`note` stays optional**, nudged per edge by the info lint rather than required. **Read as a consumer on both wiring surfaces (#2057):** the two read-only consumer summaries — the Agent-Chat right rail's **Outcomes** tab and the Flow Editor's **Routing** tab — knew only `setLabel`/`openPr`/`openIssue`, so the one case where the *platform itself* wakes the next agent fell through to their "nothing reacts to this outcome's signal" branch and a correctly wired hand-off read as a broken flow (with the raw type string `wakeAgent` next to the `Ban` icon the panel uses for *set no signal* — the semantic opposite). Both now derive it from the shared pure `outcome-consumers.js`: `outcomeHandoffs()` returns one `{to, name, state}` per hand-off and `handoffStatement()` returns the surface-agnostic `{tone, text}` (#963's extraction is why a fix on one surface only would be a regression). Three things worth knowing. (1) The healthy wording is **uniform with a label hand-off** — `wakes <target> (direct hand-off)` — and the parenthetical is `WAKE_EDGE_LEGEND`, the constant the canvas edge legend and the event log's Event column already carry, so no fourth phrasing entered the product. (2) The three states are the graph lints' own three, **by the same predicates**, so the panel and the lint list cannot disagree about one hand-off: `unresolved` covers a stale id, a person, an actor pseudo-node **and** a self-hand-off (each draws no edge and is refused on delivery); `disabled` is the `wake-target-disabled` rule — the target's `wake-agent` listeners all exist and are all switched off — deliberately narrower than "has no listener", since that listener is derived. Both are **warning-coloured on both surfaces**, the Outcomes tab being the one surface with no lint list at all. (3) The consumer statement carries **no `✕`**: the neighbouring `✕ Stop waking <name>` unwires a *listener* and applies immediately, while a hand-off has no listener to unwire and is removed by editing the save-gated action two lines above — so `unwireConsumerSpec` deliberately still returns `null` for a hand-off, pinned by a guard in `outcome-routing.test.js`. A hand-off row is also no longer offered `mark terminal` / `mark external…` (declaring it an open end would be false), and a hand-off next to a label, a marker or a person hand-off renders **both** relationships — a hand-off that also declares itself the end product is a contradiction the operator needs to see. Concept: `docs/direct-agent-handoff-concept.md`.
- `loop` — **re-arms on completion** (chains, not wall-clock): fires an iteration, then arms the next when it returns (`_loop_runner`, sibling of `_cron_runner`). Fresh context per iteration (defaults to `sessionPolicy: per-invocation`). **Stop contract**: `maxIterations` (**required**, positive int) → `exhausted`; `until.command` exit `0` → `done`; optional `maxTokens` → `exhausted`; the #611 runaway backstop → `paused`. Optional `verify` is a *separate* gate — one of `{command}` / `{agentId}` / `{flow:true}` / `{selfGraded:true}` — that must pass for an iteration to count; `{flow:true}` skips the inline gate and delegates to the flow's wired downstream QS chain (use when the loop starts a review pipeline — same reviewer won't check twice); empty/self-graded `verify` on a team agent is re-filled with a sibling reviewer role (`_resolve_loop_verify`), so an agent never silently grades its own work. Validated by `_validate_loop_trigger`; concept: `docs/loop-triggers-concept.md` §4. Agent-facing docs are injected via the Trigger-management block (`orchestrator/prompt-assembly.js`). A user **Stop** ends the loop as `stopped`; on any terminal outcome the terminal-outcome message carries a structured `io.aee.loop-record` payload (config + outcome), rendered in Agent Chat as a persistent, re-runnable finished-loop card with a "Start again" button (#1231).

Results post to `trigger.roomId` if set, else the agent's DM room. If no room exists yet the run is **skipped**. `[PASS]` suppresses output.

**Session policy.** With `sessionPolicy: per-invocation`, *triggered* runs start fresh (no `--resume`) and don't write the session pointer — DM continuity is untouched; the trigger prompt gets a learn-hint to extract lessons into `memory/log.md` first. Absent = `persistent` (legacy).

### Process Canvas / Flows (orchestration graph)

Agents + their triggers form an **orchestration graph**, authored in the **Flows** view (`/flows`, `Briefings.jsx`). **One wiring vocabulary everywhere** (#1009, concept §4.6): Input · Outcome · Action · Artifact · Consumer · Reviewer · Gate · Brake; the trigger prompt has exactly one user-facing name — **"Wake instruction — what the consumer is told"** — across edge dialogs, the chat-rail TriggersPanel and lint texts (retired: "Listener prompt", "Wake prompt override", bare "Prompt", "Connection", emit/listen phrasing). The graph/editor is the `ProcessGraphPanel` export in `ProcessCanvas.jsx`, driven by a `scope` prop (`'<teamId>' | 'draft:<id>' | 'import'`). The Flows left list selects scope: **Teams** (live graphs), **Drafts**, legacy **BriefingPack flows**. `/process` routes redirect to `/flows`.

**Process Canvas lives inside Flows.** The Process Canvas is not a standalone page anymore — it is embedded in the **Flows** view (`/flows`, `admin-console/src/pages/Briefings.jsx`). The old scope dropdown is gone; scope selection moved to the Flows left list, which lists **Teams** (live process graphs), **Drafts**, the read-only **Role-templates** blueprint, and the legacy **BriefingPack flows** (Orchestrator agents → `BPGraph`). A **New flow** button creates a draft; it and the **Describe flow…** / **Import** create actions now live in the unified top-bar header (#1847/#1912), not the left list, so they stay reachable while the flows list is collapsed. The graph/editor itself is the named export `ProcessGraphPanel` in `admin-console/src/pages/ProcessCanvas.jsx` — a controlled component driven by a `scope` prop (`'all' | '<teamId>' | 'templates' | 'draft:<id>'`) with `onNavigate` / `onDraftsChanged` callbacks. Flows tracks the selected process scope in the `?proc=` query param and BriefingPack flows in `?flow=`. The last-selected flow is persisted per user in `localStorage` under `flows.lastSelected.<username>` and restored automatically on return — stale or deleted flows fall back silently to empty selection; the transient `import` scope is never persisted. Mirrors the `agentChat.lastAgentId` pattern in Agent Chat. The `/process` and `/process/:teamId` routes now redirect to `/flows` (the latter with `?proc=<teamId>`); there is no longer a "Process Canvas" nav entry.

**Declarative ports are the single source of truth** for edges (siblings on a role/agent config, edited via `ConnectionsSection.jsx`, persisted via `PUT /api/agents/:id`): `emits: string[]` (label tokens the work sets — declarative only, the agent still sets the label at runtime; draws "agent → label → listening trigger"); `dataFlow.{dependsOn,inputs,outputs}` (live) ≡ role `dependsOn/inputs/outputs` (draft) — dependency/data-flow edges; `github: {producesPr?, producesIssue?}` (what a role opens — drives badges/ports + an "open a PR/issue" prompt block); `channels: [{artifact: 'pr'|'issue', from, label?, gate?, autoClaim?}]` on the *consumer* (which producer's PR/issue it reacts to; `gate: 'human'` = inline approval gate; labelled channels auto-derive a `claim` for their listener — set `autoClaim: false` on a port to opt out); `parentMemberId` ≡ role `parent` (cycle-guarded); `stage`.

**Functional triggers are derived from ports, not authored.** A draft derives them at materialise (`expandDataFlowSteps`, `expandGithubChannels`); a live team re-derives on every port write (`reconcileTeamDataFlow`, `reconcileTeamGithubChannels`) — regenerating `<consumer>-on-push-<upstream>` / `<consumer>-on-<artifact>-<producer>` triggers (marked `dataFlow`/`channel: true`), leaving hand-authored ones untouched. **A human-approval gate** is a label listener on a downstream step whose label is flagged `humanGate` in the bundle's `labels[]` registry (or gated by a channel's `gate: 'human'`) — the red gate node; never trips `gate-violation` since no agent emits it. Gate-ness is **configuration, not a naming convention** (#928): the old `human:*` prefix and the hardcoded `hold`/`needs:human` brakes carry no meaning of their own.

**Graph endpoint.** `GET /api/process/graph` (or `POST {processTemplate}`) → `{ level, team, nodes, edges, gates, labels, humanTouchpoints, lints }`. Edge `S→T` labelled `L` exists when `S` emits `L` and a `T` trigger listens via `labelFilter`. Raw GitHub-event triggers (no `labelFilter`) are `external: true`; gate/brake labels and `cron`/`once` self-loop `timer` edges originate from `null`. **Gate/brake classification reads config flags, never the label name** (#928): `labels[].humanGate` / `labels[].brake` from the bundle registry (a live team keeps it on the team agent, like `stages`) plus `channels[].gate: 'human'` — threaded into the builders as `{ labels }`. A `humanGate` label may not be agent-set (`gate-violation`); a `brake` label pauses the loop and *may* be agent-set. **Named human roles (#987, supersedes the #981 `@human` hub):** the generic `@human` actor is GONE — a person is never an anonymous hub, only a **named** `kind: 'human'` flow node. An optional `humans: [{ id, name, description?, stage?, emits?, consumes? }]` registry (persisted on the team agent like `stages`/`labels`, threaded into the builders as `{ humans }`, round-tripped through export/materialise/Edit-as-draft) declares them — purely declarative (no agent, no trigger; `triggers.json` stays byte-identical). A human's `emits` label is *implicitly a gate* (an agent emitting it → `gate-violation`); its `consumes` (`{label}` brake **or** plain delivery (notification when the label is set, no blocking — flag-based: `brake: true` in registry = brake, else delivery) · `{artifact:'pr'|'issue',from}` PR/issue review · `{artifact:'file',name}` output read) bind the gate/brake/delivery edges to that named node (first match in `humans[]` order wins; two emitters draw both edges), suppressing `producer-unconsumed`/`unconsumed-output`. A gate/brake/delivery with **no** human role wired stays UNBOUND — its edge endpoint is `null` (the renderer drops it, no hub box) and it raises an advisory `info` lint (`unbound-gate`/`unbound-brake`/`unbound-delivery`); it still lists as an unassigned `humanTouchpoint`. Bound touchpoints carry `role: <humanId>`. Wired by drag&drop (agent → human box = delivery to `consumes`; human box → agent = gate to `emits` + a listener trigger; the human-aware `CreateEdgeDialog` branches on node `kind` so a person is never mis-wired as a GitHub channel on a role) or via the Routing tab's **human-role delivery picker** (replaced the #981 "a person consumes this" audience checkbox); roles are created in the Flow-Editor **Add human** panel + the human NodeInspector (`PUT /api/agents/:teamId {humans}`, team-agent only). `lints` (advisory, never blocks): `orphan-listener`, `dead-end-emitter`, `competing-stage`, `producer-unconsumed`, `channel-from-non-producer`, `unbound-gate`/`unbound-brake`/`unbound-delivery` (info — a human touchpoint with no named role wired), `unclaimed-listener` (info — label listener has no `claim`; stall indistinguishable from a missed wake), `gate-violation` (error). Levels: **live** (`?team=<id>`) reads persisted `emits`; **template** (`?templates=...`) reads role-template `emits` (raw `{{var}}`). Pure builders: `process-graph.js` + `unified-graph.js`.

**Flow-anomaly endpoint.** `GET /api/teams/:teamId/flow-anomalies` (proxy to `GET /internal/teams/:teamId/flow-anomalies`) sweeps open GitHub label/timeline state + control-plane session state and feeds them to the pure detector (`orchestrator/flow-anomaly-detector.js`). Returns `{ teamId, generatedAt, thresholds, repos, model, anomalies, errors? }` — `anomalies` is a deduplicated, ranked list of `{ class, severity, objectId, agentId, ageMs, message, evidence }`. **Observe-only; skipped during maintenance mode.** Four anomaly classes (concept `docs/flow-reconciliation-concept.md` §4.1): `wake-not-claimed` (warn), `claim-stalled` (warn), `output-missing` (warn), `gate-idle` (info). A fetch/read failure degrades to a partial result with `errors[]`, never a 500. **Per-team threshold overrides:** `anomalyThresholds: { wakeNotClaimedMs?, claimStalledMs?, gateIdleMs? }` on the team-agent config; defaults T₁ = 15 min, T₂ = 2 h, T₃ = 24 h. **UI:** the **Anomalies** toolbar button (live team scope only) opens `FlowAnomaliesPanel.jsx` (side panel; Refresh re-sweeps); affected canvas nodes carry a count badge. No repair — that is Phase 4. **Scheduled persistence (#1221, hill-climbing loop Phase 1):** the on-demand endpoint computes and discards; a separate hourly orchestrator timer (`orchestrator/anomaly-history.js`, maintenance-gated, best-effort) re-runs the same sweep per Agent Team and appends one JSONL line (`ts`, `count`, `anomalies`, `errors`) to `/workspace/teams/<teamId>/hill-climb/anomaly-history.jsonl` — a shared substrate for recurrence detection and the flow-reconciliation Phase-4 responder's "third sighting" ladder. Line-based retention (default 90 days, `HILL_CLIMB_ANOMALY_RETENTION_DAYS`); a team with no repos/tokens writes no file.

**Admission control — a declarative per-flow WIP limit, enforced at dispatch (#2114, epic #2112 Flow Control; design of record `docs/flow-control-concept.md` §4.0).** A stalled epic's root cause was upstream of the merge: an unbounded dispatcher fed several tickets into one flow at once, their PRs raced a single shared `main`, and the conflicts were manufactured **by construction**. Phase 0 fixed it in the *dispatcher agent's prompt* — advisory, per-team, re-derivable by an LLM; this makes it a platform invariant. A bundle may carry a top-level **`wipLimit`** (a positive integer; `0` is REFUSED rather than tolerated, since it reads like "no limit" but means "admit nothing"), round-tripped like `stages`/`labels`/`hillClimb` and persisted on the team agent — the orchestrator's dispatch path reads the team config, never a draft. **Absent = today's unbounded behaviour**, with no lane file and no extra request anywhere. Pure core `orchestrator/admission-control.js` (see Key Files); the fs/GitHub half is `orchestrator.js`. **The gate sits inside `enqueuePending`**, not at each of the five producers (poller fan-out, `flow-emit`, the label fast-path, `wake-agent`, the hook server), because that function is the pending queue's single writer — one seam a producer added later cannot bypass; the promotion path re-enters it with `{admissionChecked: true}`. Only `github`-kind entries are gated: a session reset, a handover DM, a loop-verify, a digest and a direct `wake-agent` hand-off carry no GitHub object to attribute and are control traffic, not flow dispatch. **A held dispatch returns `true`, not `false`** — it is durably recorded and will fire, and a `false` means "nothing anywhere has this event" (#2030), which would make an `aee-emit` producer report a delivery failure for merely queued work. **The lane is per team** (`admission-lane.json` beside the team agent's other control files): `inFlight` is a set of **tickets** — not runs, not GitHub objects — each carrying the `objects` (its issue and its PR(s)) that make it one ticket, which is what stops `wipLimit: 1` deadlocking on its own first hand-off; `held` holds the verbatim queue entries, deduped on `(agentId, triggerId, eventId)` and deliberately **uncapped**, because the queue it defers into has no cap either (#1528 — bound execution, not the queue) and a cap on a hold queue can only be implemented by dropping work. **A 60 s reconcile tick** (`admissionSweepTeam`, `AEE_ADMISSION_TICK_INTERVAL_MS`) sweeps open issues + PRs per repo, releases finished tickets and promotes held dispatches — a periodic tick and **not** a completion event, because §4.0 is explicit that encoding seriality in the wake event is what froze the epic in the first place. It returns before its first request for a team with no limit or an empty lane, **fails closed** on an unreadable repo (the ticket keeps its slot, marked `unverified` — a GitHub outage must not open the lane, the #2113 discipline), and **enqueues before persisting** the lane (concept §4.2 step 3: a crash then re-delivers rather than dropping). It is deliberately **not** maintenance-gated — it starts no work, only moves entries between two durable queues, and the agent-side drain is already gated. Two attribution rules with opposite safe sides, both stated in the code: an incoming PR event is keyed on the issue it **closes** (the poller's own strict `closesIssues` enrichment, #1833), because a false link there would merge two separate tickets into one lane slot; the reconcile instead uses the **loose** `prsForIssue` linkage, because a false link there only keeps a ticket in flight longer. A ticket is released when GitHub shows nothing open for it — including an issue left open with only `:completed` labels and no PR, which is a flow that dropped the ticket and would otherwise freeze the lane forever. Wiring is source-guarded by `orchestrator/admission-control-wiring.test.js` (14 named checks, each proven by a mutation that fires exactly it). No Flow-Editor control yet — the limit is set through the bundle or `PUT /api/agents/:teamId {wipLimit}`.

**Ticket reconciler — the ledger, deriving true ticket state from GitHub (#2115, epic #2112 Flow Control; design of record `docs/flow-control-concept.md` §4.1 + Amendment 1).** `GET /api/teams/:teamId/tickets` (proxy to `GET /internal/teams/:teamId/tickets`, maintenance-gated exactly like `flow-anomalies`) returns a per-team **ticket ledger**: one row per open work item carrying a canonical **lifecycle state** (`backlog · refining · ready · in-dev · in-review · rework · doc · mergeable · done`) plus an orthogonal **blocker** with a stable reason code (`superseded · claimed-done-not-merged · conflict · stale-base · ci-red · approved-not-merged · orphaned · unclaimed · stalled · needs-human`), a `lane`, an `epicId`, an `ownerAgentId`, `ageInStateMs`, `prNumber`/`prState`, `needsHuman`, `recurrence` and `evidence[]`. **Observe-only — it computes and discards, and mutates nothing** (a guard asserts the sweep makes no write call and issues no GitHub POST). The pure core is `orchestrator/ticket-reconciler.js`, the GitHub-JSON shaping `orchestrator/ticket-reconcile-sweep.js`, and `computeTeamTicketLedger` in `orchestrator.js` is the thin I/O adapter — the `flow-anomaly-detector.js`/`flow-anomaly-sweep.js` split, for the same reason. **The point of §4.1 is that state comes from EVIDENCE, not labels**, so wherever a label and a GitHub fact disagree the fact wins and the disagreement is itself reported as `claimed-done-not-merged`. Six things are load-bearing. (1) **Nothing positional is name-matched**: an entry lane is a wake label no consumer emits, a terminal consumer is one that emits nothing and produces no PR, `mergeable` is "the deepest active label reaches a terminal consumer" and `doc` is "one hop before that" — so `ready-2`/`ready-to-merge` are never in the code and a team with a different vocabulary gets the same lifecycle. (2) `buildFlowModel` **drops** human-gate labels and every member whose only listener is one, which in the observed flow is the Developer and the whole dispatch-lane edge set — so `buildTicketLedger` optionally takes the raw `members` and recovers those edges itself (`gateWakeEdges`/`mergedConsumers`) rather than changing a builder three other features read; `dispatchGates` then keeps "a person must act" (a gate nobody listens on) apart from "a person acted and an agent was woken". (3) `claimed-done-not-merged` is **not** "a `:completed` label with an open PR" — that flags every healthy hand-off; it fires when the completing stage set **none of its declared outgoing labels** and the PR is still open past a grace window, which is vacuously true for a terminal consumer and is exactly the §1.1 blind spot. (4) `superseded` counts only files a PR **adds**, requires **all** of them to be in the base, and **refuses on a truncated base tree**, because a truncated recursive tree reports "absent" for paths it never listed; `stale-base` needs both halves (text-merges **and** `main` moved since the PR branched), since red checks on an unmoved base is a code fix (`ci-red`) while red checks after `main` advanced is a rebase; `mergeable` stays three-valued, GitHub answering `null` while it is still computing. (5) A ticket's labels live on **both** its objects — the gate on the issue, every hand-off on the PR — so the ledger reads the **union**. (6) The four shipped anomaly classes fold in as **one input signal** (`output-missing` → `stalled`, which §4.1's table leaves unnamed) and `anomaly-history.jsonl` supplies `recurrence` via the new `readEntries` — both reused, both non-fatal, and both deliberately outranked by a hard GitHub fact. Per-sweep cost is bounded by `TICKET_LEDGER_PR_LIMIT` (default 60 enriched PRs; the rest are named in `errors[]`, never silently dropped) and one recursive base tree **per repo**, not per PR; every fetch is individually try/caught into `errors[]`, so a partial outage degrades precision and never 500s. No board and no UI — §4.3/§4.4/§4.5 own those. Wiring is source-guarded by `orchestrator/ticket-ledger-wiring.test.js` (15 named checks, each proven by a mutation that fires exactly it).

**The Flow Control board (#2116, epic #2112; design of record `docs/flow-control-concept.md` §4.4 + Amendment 2).** The operator-facing half of the ledger: a `/flow-control` page rendering one team's `GET /api/teams/:id/tickets` as a **swimlane per lifecycle stage**. **Nav entry removed (#2147, step 1 of 2):** the page no longer has a sidebar entry — it sat in **Agent Setup**, directly under Flows, until #2147 took it off the main menu at the requester's insistence (a role gate would not have helped, since the requester is themself a System Admin). The route, its help and its backend are unchanged and the page is still reachable by navigating to `/flow-control` directly; #2145 (step 2) re-ships the board as an installable, team-grantable plugin, at which point it regains a nav entry of its own. Each card carries `#`/title, the derived **state**, the **owning agent**, **age-in-state**, a **blocker badge** with the reason detail on hover, and **deep links to the issue and the PR**; above the board, a cross-lane **epic roll-up** (`Epic #2112: 3 done · 1 blocked · 2 in progress · 4 waiting`). Decisions are the pure `admin-console/src/pages/process/flow-control-view.js` (the path §4.4 names); `admin-console/src/pages/FlowControl.jsx` is a thin renderer. **Read-only by construction** — the ledger observes and mutates nothing, so the page has no repair action and its guard asserts it issues no mutating `apiCall`; §4.3's supervisor and §4.5's inbox own acting on what it shows. Four things are load-bearing. (1) **AC2 ("never derives state from raw labels") is proven behaviourally, not by grepping for label vocabulary** — which is a false-positive generator for ordinary words and blind to a label read through a variable: the test **deletes `ticket.labels` from a ledger row** and asserts the card is `deepEqual` to the card built with it, so the labels array is demonstrably not an input, and a fixture whose labels all say `:completed` while the reconciler says `in-review` lands in **In review with a `Claimed done` badge**, never in Done. (2) `TICKET_STATES` and `BLOCKER_CODES` are **duplicated** here because the browser bundle cannot `require` from `orchestrator/` — the drift guard reads both `Object.freeze([…])` arrays **out of `orchestrator/ticket-reconciler.js`** and fails on a reorder, an addition or a badge table missing a code (the `working-env-repo-diff.js` two-sided arrangement). (3) **Empty lanes are kept**: where work is *not* is information, and a board hiding its empty columns is a filtered list; an unknown state lands in a trailing `Other` lane rather than being dropped, and lane order is the ledger's own `compareTickets` ranking (most-actionable first), never re-sorted. (4) The **epic roll-up's four buckets are mutually exclusive and exhaustive** by construction, `done` outranking `blocked` (a blocker on merged work is history), asserted over the whole state × blocker cross-product. `age` and the severity stripe reuse `flow-anomalies-view.js`'s `formatAge`/`severityColor` rather than a third copy. A real route costs the full registration surface — `App.jsx`, `Shell.jsx` nav, `breadcrumbs.js`, `route-title.js`, `PAGE_ROUTES` — plus an authored `help-topics/flow-control.md`, since `help-coverage.test.js` fails CI on a page route with no topic and no named owner; the new `/flow-control` grounding context measures **231 256**, well under the unmoved `/quickstart` worst case of **312 384** of 340 000. **The §4.4 netzplan stopgap is deliberately NOT built** (Amendment 2 A2.1): its specified actor is *the supervisor*, which is Phase 4 and has zero code in the repository, and there is no orchestrator→netzplan write path to substitute for it — `aee-plan` is an agent-workspace CLI seeded by a plugin grant. Two §4.4 factual corrections are recorded there for whoever does build it: a netzplan **swimlane lane has no `status` field** (only a graph node does, so a lifecycle mirror must use the graph primitive), and `ownerId` is referentially validated against `doc.owners[]`, so a mirror must `add-owner` first.

**Hill-climb metrics collector (#1222, hill-climbing loop Phase 1).** A per-team run-metrics pass that folds the team's log surfaces (member session JSONLs, usage spool, #1221's `anomaly-history.jsonl`, the event-transparency log, audit / `.agent-studio/prompt-history.json` version boundaries) into the §4 observational catalog — run success rate (excl. `[PASS]`), rework ratio, time-to-done p50/p90, anomaly recurrence, waste (failed/aborted/`[PASS]` tokens, throttle drops, digest folds), dead config (zero-fire triggers, unconsumed emits) — keyed by `(agentId, triggerId)` and rolled up per config version so a later phase can measure a change across the boundary. The deterministic core is pure + golden-fixture tested (`orchestrator/hill-climb-metrics.js` + the I/O-shaping adapter `orchestrator/hill-climb-sweep.js`, siblings of `flow-anomaly-detector.js`/`flow-anomaly-sweep.js`); `computeTeamHillClimbMetrics` in `orchestrator.js` is the thin fs adapter. **Observational only** (no LLM, no quality scores); below `minRunsPerWindow` the snapshot is honestly flagged `insufficientData` and the ratio metrics are nulled. A **daily** orchestrator timer (`HILL_CLIMB_METRICS_INTERVAL_MS`, maintenance-gated, overlap-guarded, best-effort) persists a day-keyed snapshot `/workspace/teams/<teamId>/hill-climb/metrics/<date>.json` (same-day re-run overwrites). **Endpoints:** `GET /api/teams/:teamId/hill-climb/metrics` (latest snapshot, read-only) + `POST .../hill-climb/run` (manual pass — same code path as the timer, behind a per-team single-flight lock → `409 pass-running`), both proxying `/internal/teams/:teamId/hill-climb/*` behind team scoping + audit. **Config:** `hillClimb: { cron?, windowDays? (default 7), minRunsPerWindow? (default 20), excludeAgents? }` on the team agent, round-tripped through export/materialise/Edit-as-draft like `stages`/`labels` (`normalizeHillClimb` strips everything but the config keys, so applied-card/snapshot state never travels). Concept: `docs/hill-climbing-agent-concept.md` §3.1/§4/§7.

**Improvement cards — analyst + store (#1224, hill-climbing loop Phase 2).** The pass now also raises **improvement cards** (proposals only — nothing in this phase mutates config). The pure card core is `orchestrator/hill-climb-cards.js` (in the `process-template.js` discipline): `validateCard` (schema-enforces a mandatory `targetMetric` + non-empty `evidence` — the closed-loop invariant), `heuristicFindings` (deterministic cards from the snapshot: zero-fire triggers, unconsumed emits, recurring anomalies), `mergeCards` (budget `maxCardsPerPass` default 5 + one-open-card-per-`(object,class)` dedup-bump + one-window cooldown after an applied change + dismissed-suppression), and `buildAnalystDigest`/`redactSecrets` (the secret-free digest handed to the analyst). `runHillClimbPass` folds the heuristic cards into `/workspace/teams/<teamId>/hill-climb/cards.json` on **every** pass (concept's honest fallback) and returns the digest. The **analyst** is a server-side one-shot LLM call in the console (`admin-console/api/hill-climb-analyst.js` + `resolveAssistTarget`, skips oauth-token providers, mirrors `port-suggestion.js`): the `POST /api/teams/:id/hill-climb/run` route runs it over the digest when `hillClimb.autonomy >= 1` and a provider resolves, then POSTs the parsed cards to `POST /internal/teams/:id/hill-climb/cards/merge`, which **re-validates** every one through `validateCard` (the store is the authority — the analyst is untrusted) and merges under the budget rules; no provider / LLM failure leaves the heuristic cards standing. **Endpoints:** `GET /api/teams/:id/hill-climb/cards` + `POST .../cards/:cardId/dismiss` (proxy `/internal/...`). **Config adds** `hillClimb: { autonomy? (0–2; default 2, 0 = collector-only no cards), maxCardsPerPass? (default 5) }`, round-tripped like the Phase-1 keys. No apply path runs yet (that is #1225). Concept: `docs/hill-climbing-agent-concept.md` §3.2/§5/§7.

**Improvements panel (#1223, hill-climbing loop Phase 1).** The **Improvements** toolbar button (`TrendingUp` icon, live team scope only, inside the `isTeamScope && !imported` block alongside **Anomalies**) opens `ImprovementsPanel.jsx` (side panel; mirrors `FlowAnomaliesPanel.jsx` structurally). Renders the §4 metric-catalog tiles from the latest snapshot (`GET /api/teams/:teamId/hill-climb/metrics`) as a 2-column grid: three ratio tiles (run success rate, rework ratio, time-to-done p50/p90) render an explicit **Insufficient data** label below `minRunsPerWindow`; three counting tiles (anomaly recurrence, waste, dead config) always render. **Run now** (`POST .../hill-climb/run`) updates the panel from the response without a reload; a 409 pass-running or `{ maintenance: true }` response is surfaced as a distinct inline notice rather than an error. Clean empty state when no snapshot exists yet. Pure view helpers in `admin-console/src/pages/process/hill-climb-view.js` (dependency-free, `node --test` covered; mirrors `flow-anomalies-view.js`). **Card list (Phase 2–3 frontend):** below the tiles the panel renders the improvement-card store (`GET .../hill-climb/cards` → `{ autonomy, cards }`) as an actionable list (regressed cards first — a pending rollback beats a fresh proposal — then open cards in store order) plus a collapsed **Change log** ledger (everything terminal + `applied` still measuring, newest first). Per card: status badge, class/risk/source chips, `seen ×n`, object + `targetMetric`, collapsible evidence citations, and the gated actions — **Apply** (open, `POST .../cards/:id/apply`), **Roll back** (regressed, `POST .../cards/:id/rollback`), **Dismiss**. `cardAction` pre-resolves the autonomy (≥2) and wired-class gates (`EXECUTABLE_CARD_CLASSES`/`EXECUTABLE_ROLLBACK_KINDS` mirror `hill-climb-apply-guard.js` — trigger-hygiene / restore-trigger only) into a disabled button + honest hint; the server re-enforces everything. Actions re-read the store afterwards (never optimistic); **Run now** also re-reads cards since the pass merges new ones. Autonomy 0 → "collector-only" banner; autonomy 1 → read-only banner. Concept: `docs/hill-climbing-agent-concept.md` §4/§7.

**Gated applier (#1225, hill-climbing loop Phase 2b).** A card is applied through its declared existing **audited choke-point** as the acting human — cards never write config directly (concept §3.3). `POST /api/teams/:id/hill-climb/cards/:cardId/apply` gates all server-side (bypass-proof): autonomy **2** required (0/1 deny, not just hide); only an `open` card; only an executable class (**trigger-hygiene** is wired first → `DELETE /api/agents/:id/triggers/:tid`; prompt/flow-wiring/threshold/knowledge paths follow on the same engine); the **trigger guard** rejects `intrinsic`/`dataFlow`/`channel`/human-gate-label triggers; a card whose target trigger changed since detection → **`409 stale-card`**. The mutation runs through the shared `deleteAgentTrigger` choke-point (the apply call is itself an audited `/api` mutation under `req.user`), then the orchestrator records the applied card (apply timestamp + `configVersionRef` + baseline metrics snapshot + undo payload = the deleted trigger JSON + one-window cooldown) for #1226 to measure. Split across containers like #1224: console-side guard/staleness/executability in `admin-console/api/hill-climb-apply-guard.js` (holds `req.user` + the mutation), autonomy + applied-card patch in `orchestrator/hill-climb-apply.js` (owns the store) — disjoint, no shared logic (the admin-console image copies only `api/*.js`). `hillClimb.autonomy` already round-trips (from #1224). Concept: `docs/hill-climbing-agent-concept.md` §3.3.

**Verdicts + rollback cards (#1226, hill-climbing loop Phase 3).** The daily pass now closes the loop: `runVerdictStep` measures every `applied` card across the config boundary it created and transitions it. The pure core is `orchestrator/hill-climb-verdict.js` (golden-fixture tested, `hill-climb-metrics.js` discipline): `computeVerdict` compares the card's declared `targetMetric` before (the baseline snapshot #1225 stamped) vs after (the fresh snapshot), **only** once the after-window lies entirely past the boundary (`window.fromMs >= configVersionRef.at` — never a window that spans two config versions of the target object); `metricDirection`/`resolveMetricValue` resolve the metric (success-rate is higher-is-better, everything else lower; arrays → length, `timeToDone` → p50). Outcomes: improved → `validated`; regressed → a **rollback card** (`buildRollbackCard`, citing both windows + a class-specific `buildRollbackPlan` undo — trigger-hygiene restores the retained trigger JSON, prompt-improvement names the exact prior `#990` prompt-history version); neutral → `neutral`, finalised only after **two** windows so a flat-looking change stays measured; below `minRunsPerWindow` or an unresolved metric → `insufficient-data` (deferred, never guessed). **Rollback is a human click** (`POST /api/teams/:id/hill-climb/cards/:cardId/rollback`): it re-runs the undo through the same audited choke-point as the apply (trigger-hygiene → the shared `restoreAgentTrigger`; other classes refused as not-yet-wired, mirroring the applier), then the orchestrator marks the card `rolled-back` with a **fresh one-window cooldown** so the object re-enters detection only after a full window (`mergeCards` defers a `regressed` card and a `rolled-back` card still in cooldown). New card statuses: `rolled-back` (+ `verdict`/`rollback` fields). The Improvements panel surfaces these: verdict badges + before/after line (`verdictLine`), regressed cards at the top of the card list with the rollback reason and a **Roll back** button. Concept: `docs/hill-climbing-agent-concept.md` §6.

**Artifacts** are matched by **logical name** (`{name, hint?, description?}`, `artifactKey` in `process-template.js`), not physical path, since a re-instantiable agent writes to a varying path. Real location is resolved at runtime from a git-committed manifest `.agent-studio/artifacts.jsonl`: the producer records with `aee-artifact add "<name>" <path>` (CLI `agent-image/scripts/aee-artifact`) + commits/pushes; the derived trigger tells the consumer to `git pull` and `aee-artifact path "<name>"`. A **wake, not a start-gate** — nothing blocks a downstream agent (agents have no "completed" state; that gating is BP-only).

**Drafts** are editable bundles that exist before materialisation. CRUD `/api/process/drafts[/:id]`, persisted at `/workspace/process-drafts/<id>.json` (empty `roles` allowed). **Private to their creator, shared with equal rights (#2068, refines #2041 AC4 for the one object that exists *before* a team does).** A draft record carries `createdBy` (immutable provenance, display only) and a flat `accessUserIds` set, and authorization is **one predicate** — `canAccessDraft(scope, draft)`, *is the caller in the set?* — with a `scope.all` shortcut, i.e. the `visibleAgent` pattern, **not** the personal-credential tier (which deliberately has no admin bypass). It is called from all six draft routes plus three new ones (`GET`/`POST /api/process/drafts/:id/access`, `DELETE .../access/:userId`); a caller outside the set gets **the same 404 as a non-existent draft**, so the endpoint is not an existence oracle. **`createdBy` is never an authorization input** (DD2 — the creator holds no lasting privilege), asserted by a guard that reads the enforcement path. **The access fields are server-owned:** `PUT` spreads the whole request body and `validateProcessTemplate` is not a whitelist, so the incoming pair is stripped and the stored one carried over the way `createdAt` is — which is also what makes sharing survive a rename, and what keeps Export/Import from becoming a way to grant yourself access (`downloadBundle` strips them on the way out, the create route on the way in). Everyone in the set is fully equal — edit, rename, export, create a team, share on, remove anyone including you, delete — with two invariants falling out of that: the set can **never be emptied** (the last holder is refused with a 409 naming *Delete draft*), and deleting a **user record** strips them from every set without deleting a draft (a set left empty keeps the draft, System-Admin-reachable). Migration is literal #2041 D6: `backfillDraftAccess()` in the `app.listen` callback stamps every pre-#2068 draft with the first System Admin and **all current users**, and re-runs are no-ops. The **Agent Team Wizard's draft gallery is correct for free** — it consumes the same list endpoint, which is why the filter is server-side. UI: a **Share…** entry on the draft's Flows-rail row opening a `SidePanel` over the open directory `GET /api/users/directory` (**built here**, projecting `{id, displayName, email}` only — never the `systemAdmin` flag, never the GitHub handle); the rail row marks each draft *private* / *shared with N people* and names a creator who is not you (resolved server-side, so no user id reaches the client); the delete confirmation names the other people who will lose it. Pure decision core `admin-console/api/draft-access.js` (whose `draft-access.test.js` carries the first integration coverage any draft route has ever had — the two-user loop), wiring guard `draft-routes-access.test.js`, client decisions `src/pages/process/draft-share-view.js`. Draft mode edits in place via a write-target adapter (`edit-adapters.js`) routing shared dialogs to `liveOps` (HTTP to running agents) or `makeDraftOps` (mutate bundle + PUT); `liveOps` writes only ports, so it's shape-compatible — the editor never branches on mode.
