**Templates & round-trip.** `GET /api/process/template?team=<id>` exports a portable `processTemplateVersion: 1` bundle (roles, `emits`, triggers, gates, labels, `stages` — no repos/secrets); materialising feeds it to `POST /api/teams`. **Edit as draft** seeds a draft with the team's bundle + `sourceTeamId` (defaults `mode: extend`). Format: `docs/process-template-format.md`.

**Stages** partition the flow axis: each node has an optional `stage` (inherits from parent via `effectiveStage`); an ordered `stages: string[]` on the bundle/team-agent defines column blocks. Authored via `StageManager.jsx`; carried through materialisation and round-tripped by Edit-as-draft. **Layout** is a layered DAG (`flow-layout.js`: `computeFlowLayout` + `assignCrossCoords`) — breaks rework cycles, layers by longest-path, reduces crossings, aligns via isotonic regression; parent-child hierarchy forms contiguous **sibling groups** drawn as a bezier fan. Canvas edges are clickable (`flow-graph-render.js`) with type-aware dialogs.

**LLM-assisted authoring.** The **Add role** dialog's **Generate agent prompt** button → `POST /api/agent-prompt/generate` (`resolveAssistTarget` enforces the caller's header selection against team visibility — 403/400 when the provider is not permitted / unselected — else the `.env` fallback in bootstrap installs; one stateless `/v1/messages` call). Wiring suggestions via `POST /api/agent-ports/suggest` (`port-suggestion.js`, LLM + keyword fallback, user-confirmed). Nothing persisted server-side. **Autonomous synthesis** (curator auto-promotion `autoPromoteStep`, skill distillation `autoExtendStep`) runs with no user session, so the per-user header selection cannot apply; the optional team-agent field `assistLlmEndpointId` names the provider these calls bill against (`orchestrator/assist-binding.js` — `pickAssistEndpoint`, non-oauth, team-scoped; `resolveAssistBindingForTeam` resolves `{baseUrl,token,model}`; unset → `.env` as before). Configured in the **Knowledge page's ConfigurePanel** (`KnowledgeFlow.jsx`), Team-Admin-gated. **Knowledge-domain bootstrap** (`POST /api/knowledge/domains {op:'bootstrap'}`) is user-triggered, so it uses the caller's header selection instead: ids forwarded through the console proxy (`resolveAssistTarget` — same 403/400 enforcement as the nine Family-A routes), token resolved locally in the orchestrator (`resolveAssistBindingFromSelection`); unset/unusable falls back to `.env`. Concept: `docs/assist-model-selection-concept.md` §4.6.

**Assist Model Selector (#1212, moved to Settings → Model in #1805).** A **`<Provider> · <model>`** picker in **Settings → Model** (the account-menu Settings item, `SettingsModal.jsx` Model section, `AssistModelSelector.jsx`) lets the logged-in user pick which Model Provider every assist call bills against. The control was removed from the Shell header right-cluster in #1805. `ModelSelectionProvider` (`src/context/ModelSelectionContext.jsx`, inside `AuthProvider`) loads `GET /api/assist/model-options` after auth, validates any stored selection against it, auto-selects when exactly one provider is available, and persists the choice per user at `localStorage assistModel.<username>`. States: attention styling when ≥ 2 providers but none picked; auto-selected (no attention) with exactly one; read-only "Built-in (.env)" chip when zero providers + env fallback active; "No assist model available" chip otherwise. `apiCall()` (`src/hooks/useApi.js`) injects `X-Assist-Model: <endpointId>:<modelId>` on every request via `assistHeaders()` from `src/context/assist-transport.js`; the copilot NDJSON stream (owned by `FlowCopilotContext.jsx` since #2087, formerly `FlowCopilotPanel.jsx`; the wizard `StepProcess.jsx` dropped its inline copilot branch in #1703) adds the header explicitly; `KnowledgeFlow.jsx` / `SkillsRegistry.jsx` raw-fetch call sites go through an `assistFetch()` wrapper — covering all nine Family-A assist routes. Error nudge: 400 `assist-model-not-selected` / 403 `assist-endpoint-not-permitted` from any assist call opens **Settings → Model** (via a `registerOpenSettings` opener `Shell.jsx` registers into `ModelSelectionContext`, deep-linking `SettingsModal`'s Model section) and then the picker — since #1805 the selector lives inside the modal, which is unmounted while closed, so opening the modal first is what makes the picker visible (403 also drops the stored selection and refetches options). Server-side enforcement (replacing `resolveGenerationTarget` with `resolveAssistTarget` on the nine routes) ships in #1211. Concept: `docs/assist-model-selection-concept.md` §4.3–§4.5. **Shared selector component (#1670).** The popover markup is now the shared `admin-console/src/components/ModelSelectorPopover.jsx` — one provider-grouped popover (scope badge per group, "default" badge, checkmark, plain model label with **no** family/kind suffix) used by *both* this global assist selector (now in Settings → Model) and the Agent-Chat runtime selector, so they read with one visual/interaction language while staying functionally separate. `AssistModelSelector.jsx` is now a thin wrapper feeding it groups from `assistGroups(providers)` (in `assist-model-selection.js`), its billing hint ("Model AI helpers bill against"), attention tone and the env-fallback / no-provider chips; pure row/label/clamp helpers live in `admin-console/src/components/model-selector-view.js` (unit-tested, `admin-console-src` CI job).

**Admin console sidebar navigation IA (#1595).** The left sidebar is organised into two tiers by `Shell.jsx`: **NAV_TOP** (ungrouped, always-visible — Agent Chat with its unread/busy badges) and **NAV_GROUPS** (collapsible accordion groups in workflow order — **Agent Setup** · **Connections** · **Administration** · **Help**). **The NAV_TOP tier is PINNED, not merely first (#2035).** The `<aside>` is a fixed-height (`100vh`, `overflow: hidden`) flex column; `topItems` renders in its own `flexShrink: 0` block **between** the `+ Create` block and the `<nav>`, so only the accordion groups scroll — it used to be the first child *inside* the `flex: 1; overflowY: auto` `<nav>` and scrolled out of view with them while `+ Create`, which has always sat outside, stayed put. It is a render-tree position only: `nav-model.js`, the role gating and the NAV_TOP/NAV_GROUPS data model are untouched, and the row is still rendered by the shared `renderNavItem` so the badge, the collapsed-rail dot and the active-route highlight are the same code. The whole tier is pinned rather than Agent Chat specifically, so a second NAV_TOP entry inherits the behaviour. Because a JSX position cannot be asserted by `nav-model.js`'s pure tests, the source-reading drift guard `admin-console/src/components/nav-pinned-top.test.js` pins it (#651). The former Insights section is gone: GitHub Event Log moved into **Connections**, Token Usage into **Administration**. **Connections** also holds My Credentials (personal credential self-service, ungated); **Administration** holds the admin **Plugins** registry entry. There is **no Help nav group**: `/getting-started` and its sidebar entry were retired in #1874 and the big-picture introduction now lives in the header Help panel's **Overview** face (the path itself stays as a redirect to `/chats` that opens that face, so old bookmarks degrade gracefully rather than landing on a blank route). The previous flat sections (INFRASTRUCTURE / CONFIGURATION) are gone. Role gating (`adminOnly` → System Admin only; `credAdmin` → System Admin or Team Admin) is unchanged; a group left empty for the current role renders no header. Per-user collapse state persists in `localStorage` under `nav.collapsedGroups.<username>` (distinct from the Agent-Chat sidebar's `agentChat.*` keys — different sidebar, different namespace). The group containing the active route auto-expands on load and deep-link; first-ever load defaults to **Agent Setup** expanded. Dynamic UI-plugin entries append as an additional collapsible **Plugins** accordion group (runtime key `plugins`, collapsed by default; participates in `nav.collapsedGroups.<username>` via `ALL_GROUP_KEYS = [...NAV_GROUP_KEYS, 'plugins']`) — appended **last**, at the tail of the accordion list (it used to be inserted before the now-removed Help group) — so a long plugin list folds away and never overflows the sidebar. **Active-route resolution is specificity-aware** (`mostSpecificActivePath`): the longest matching nav path wins, so a `/plugins/:id/view` plugin-viewer route lights up (and auto-expands) only the dynamic Plugins group — never also the Administration group's `/plugins` registry item that it merely prefixes. Pure gating, active-route matching (`isPathActive`/`mostSpecificActivePath`), active-group lookup (`activeGroupKey`), and collapse-state reconciliation (`mergeCollapsed`/`defaultCollapsed`) live in `admin-console/src/components/nav-model.js` (unit-tested, `admin-console-src` CI job); `Shell.jsx` JSX stays uncovered per the #651 convention.

### Webhook Server (`http` triggers)

Second HTTP server on `HOOK_PORT` (default `4003`). External systems trigger with:

```
POST /hooks/:agentId/:hookId
X-Hook-Secret: <trigger.secret>
```

Path must match an existing agent + an `http` trigger with matching `id`. The event is enqueued in `pending-triggers.json` (drained every 5s). **Security:** the secret is validated against `X-Hook-Secret`; secretless triggers are rejected 401 (no global "open" mode). The POST body is ignored — the trigger's `prompt` is what Claude receives.

*Known limitation:* the queue only protects against the **Maintenance Mode flag**. An event delivered while the container/webhook server is genuinely down (crash, redeploy) is **not auto-retried** — but the scheduler writes durable `in-flight-triggers.json` markers before each execution; orphaned markers at next startup trigger a "⚠️ Trigger possibly lost" DM (the human decides). Prefer `github-event` over `http` for anything that must not drop during downtime.

### Maintenance Mode and triggers

While platform-wide Maintenance Mode is active, each scheduler checks `GET /internal/maintenance-mode` before firing: `cron`/`once` inside the window don't fire; `http`/`github-event` keep queuing but stay undrained until maintenance ends; for `cron`, exactly **one** catch-up run executes after the window (never a backlog flood); `once` self-heals on the next 60s tick, `github-event` on poller restart.

### GitHub poller (`github-event` triggers)

`orchestrator/github-poller.js` polls the GitHub Events API every 30s for every repo in some env with a `githubTokenId` — for local stacks with no public ingress (polling replaces webhooks).

- **Repo discovery**: walks all envs, dedupes, picks one token per repo (first env wins); tokenless repos skipped.
- **Bandwidth**: page-1 uses `If-None-Match`; 304s cost no rate budget (~120 conditional GETs/h/repo). **Pagination**: reads further pages on a >100-event burst until an already-seen id / partial page / `MAX_PAGES`, so bursts don't drop events.
- **Cursor**: a per-repo sliding **seen-id set** (`state.seen[]`, `state.json`), not a `created_at` high-water-mark — an event is fresh iff its id is unseen. First poll only baselines. Fan-out oldest-first.
- **History horizon**: each subscriber gated by `effectiveHorizon = activatedAt ?? createdAt ?? triggers.json mtime` + 5-min grace, so a `triggers.json` rewrite doesn't shove it to "now". Derived triggers preserve prior same-id `activatedAt` (`stampDerivedActivation`).
- **Fan-out**: matched events → `pending-triggers.json` (drained every 5s), appended as a fenced JSON block. Event keys `<lowercase_type_without_event>.<action>` (e.g. `pull_request.opened`); bare `push`. The Events API omits some actions (no `pull_request.synchronize`).
- **Identity**: agents share one PAT (same actor for everyone). `gh-as-agent` stamps human-readable attribution (`commit` → author email `<agentId>@agents.local`; `stamp <file>` → `<!-- agent: <id> -->`) but this is **vestigial for delivery** — the poller doesn't read origin.
- **Resilience** (#1572): a hard wall-clock deadline (`AEE_GITHUB_REQUEST_DEADLINE_MS`, default 30 s, above the 15 s socket-inactivity timer so the more specific message wins) settles every request regardless of which phase stalls; a stall watchdog force-opens the re-entrancy guard after 5 minutes with no progress and logs a warning, so a future stuck await (e.g. mid-body socket kill) self-heals in seconds rather than requiring an orchestrator restart. Ops verification: if the poller goes silent, `state.json` mtime stops advancing — a stale mtime past 5 min with the orchestrator alive means the watchdog should have already triggered.

**Loop protection — frequency only, no source filtering.** A trigger fires on **any** matching event, including self-caused. One frequency backstop: the **conversation throttle** (`_checkAndIncThrottle`) — per `(agent, conversationKey)` sliding window, default 5 events/h (`conversationKey` groups by `pr:42`/`issue:99`/`push:refs/heads/x`). First over-threshold drop enqueues a `kind: "throttle-warning"` (plain DM), then drops silently until reset. Event-type matching, `labelFilter`, horizon are content/recency routing, not source filters. **Burst-cap digest** (`_fanOutBatch`, `planBurstHandoffs`): a group exceeding `BURST_CAP` (default 3) in one poll collapses into a single **lossless** `kind: "github-digest"` entry (plain DM) — the poll-scoped sibling of the hour-scoped throttle.

### BP-event router

Central ingestion endpoint any signaler (today ACE `bp-transition.py`) calls to announce a Briefing Pack change; the orchestrator owns routing. **Endpoint:** `POST /internal/bp-events` (port 4002, internal-only, no auth).

```json
{ "source": "<id>", "events": [ { "type": "bp:revised|bp:completed|bp:input-changed", "bp": "<12-char hex>", "details": {} } ] }
```

**Routing** (`routeBpEvents` + `bp-event-router.js`): build a reverse index `bpId → {stubId, parentOrchestratorId}`; group by hosting orchestrator (unknown BP → `unknown-bp`); force-pull the briefings clone, build the dependency graph via `loadBriefingsGraphFromExplorer()` (bp-explorer's `/api/briefings`); compute affected BPs (**`depends-on` dependents** + **input-artifact dependents** matched output→input path); apply each agent's `bp-trigger-policy`, `enableAgent` (idempotent), append a `bp-event-batch` entry to `pending-triggers.json` (a `kind`-tagged queue entry handled inline by `executeBpEvent`, not a `triggers.json` type). Dedup on `(type, bp)`; best-effort (per-target failures surface in `routed[].error`/`skipped[]`).

**Situation classification** tags each entry from the affected BP's status — each requires an explicit closing question so the chat shows the agent waiting for input:
- `kickoff` — BP `ready`, all `depends-on` `completed`/`reviewed`. Agent posts a 2–3-sentence mission summary + approach, asks for greenlight. No claim/LoopScript/commit before user OK.
- `cascade-stale` — BP already `completed`/`reviewed`/`stale`, possibly invalidated. Agent posts a summary, asks whether to `/confirm-valid` or `/revise`.
- `other` — defensive fallback → cascade-stale prompt.

**`bp-trigger-policy`** (optional, `config.json`): `mode` = `off` / `notify-only` / `notify-on-activate` (default) — `off` ignores; `notify-only` enqueues only when already running; `notify-on-activate` materializes stubs + respawns disabled agents first. `respect-manual-disable` (default `false`) — when `true`, skip activation for a manually-disabled agent.

### BP-instance hand-off (manual)

Every bp-instance chat has a hand-off action to activate downstream agents at end-of-session. Because each agent has its own workspace + repo clones, **git is the only durable bridge** — the dialog enforces this. Opening it fetches candidates (briefings graph) + per-repo git-status; submit is disabled while any repo is dirty/unpushed unless the user opts into an override (checkbox + reason quoted into every target's note). Pre-flight nudge buttons (`POST .../handover/nudge`) post a canned commit/push prompt into the source agent's own DM; **Re-check** refreshes. On submit, `POST .../handover` re-fetches git-status server-side and returns `409 git-not-ready` unless ready or the override carries a reason. For each target it materialize/enables the stub, ensures a DM room, and posts a structured body (source-of-work per repo, input-artifact paths, user note, override reason, consistency warnings, and a "Before you start" checklist).

### Internal-API authentication (`:4002`, #1456 / GOV-PLATFORM-2)

`:4002` is never published to the host, but it *is* reachable by everything on the container network — agent containers included — so until this landed every console-side authorization check was bypassable by an agent calling the orchestrator directly. There is now **one gate as the first statement of the `internalApi` request handler** (`orchestrator/orchestrator.js`); that server is a 4 400-line `if`-ladder with no middleware chain, so "at the mount" means the top of the dispatcher, and every route — including the `/internal/admin/*` delegation and the inverted-guard tail route that doubles as the 404 — is behind it by construction rather than by review.

**Two token classes, both bearer.** `INTERNAL_API_TOKEN` is the **control-plane** credential: the admin console sends it on every `:4002` call and gets the full `/internal/*` surface, precisely because it has already applied its own per-user authorization (`scope.js`) before proxying. Each **agent container** instead receives `AEE_INTERNAL_TOKEN`, a *derived* per-agent token minted in `startAgentProcess` and baked into the env like the PAT — deterministic (an HMAC over the agent id), so no state is kept, a respawn re-mints the identical value, and rotation is free: all three respawn paths funnel through `startAgentProcess`, and changing the secret invalidates every outstanding token at once. §3.5's third class (a **signaler** token for `POST /internal/bp-events`) is deliberately **not** implemented — that route was deleted by #1961/#2005 and `bp-removal-guard.test.js` asserts it stays gone, so the class has no caller.

**The agent allowlist** (`AGENT_ROUTES` in the pure `orchestrator/internal-auth.js`) was derived by reading every caller that runs inside an agent container, not from the concept's prose list: own-`:id` telemetry / usage / knowledge / triggers / ticket / git-push-and-PR, plus the id-less routes that carry their own integrity check (`flow-emit`, `wake-agent`, `loop-verify/verdict`, `maintenance-mode`). **One route is legitimately cross-agent** — `POST /internal/agents/:id/enqueue-pending`, the #933 loop-verify hand-off into a *sibling's* queue — marked `scope: 'peer'` and pinned as the only one in both directions.

**The console side is one choke point, not 126 edits.** `admin-console/api/internal-auth-client.js` wraps the global `fetch` once, before the express app is built, scoped to the orchestrator's own origin **and** the `/internal/` prefix — so the GitHub / Matrix / LLM calls the same process makes never see the token, and a call site cannot opt out. That scoping predicate is pure and has its own leak test.

**Rollout is the §3.2 ladder**, `INTERNAL_AUTH_MODE=observe|warn|enforce`, **default `observe`**: all three rungs run the same predicate and only `denied` differs, so the observe-week census (`grep '[internal-auth]' | grep would-deny`) is a truthful preview of what enforce will reject rather than a second, drifting implementation. With no `INTERNAL_API_TOKEN` set the gate is inert and the platform behaves exactly as before; `enforce` without one is refused at **boot**. **The subtle part:** `startAgentProcess` builds the container env from `...process.env`, so the orchestrator's own control-plane token would otherwise be inherited by every agent — both it and the agent-token signing secret are explicitly deleted, mutation-proved by `orchestrator/internal-auth-wiring.test.js`. Concept: `docs/governance-concept.md` §3.5 + Amendment 1; operator guide: `docs/deployment.md` §2a.

### Admin Console authentication

The console is gated by a login screen; every `/api/*` data endpoint requires a valid session cookie (`admin-console/api/auth.js` mounts `auth.gate` over `/api`). HMAC-signed HttpOnly cookie (`aee_session`); static assets + SPA fallback stay open, as do the four public auth routes (`/auth/{login,logout,session,ready}`). **IAP SSO bridge (hosted GKE stack):** when `IAP_AUDIENCE` is set, a session-less request may instead authenticate via its Google-IAP-signed JWT (`x-goog-iap-jwt-assertion` — ES256-verified against Google's JWKS and audience-pinned in `admin-console/api/iap-auth.js`; the spoofable plain e-mail header is never trusted); a normal session is issued for the JWT's `email` **only when it matches a registered user-store subject** (#1415) — a registered user never sees the login form behind IAP; an unregistered IAP identity falls through to the normal login form instead of getting a silent empty-scope session. The registration lookup fails closed (unreadable users index → login form). Unset (compose/kind) → bridge inert, password login only.

**One login path — the configured initial user is a SEED, not a login (#2059, concept `docs/decentralised-access-concept.md` §13 / #2041 D13).** `ADMIN_USERNAME`/`ADMIN_PASSWORD` (default `admin`/`admin`) used to be a credential the console string-compared against, issuing a session with **no user record behind it** — so the only principal on a fresh stack had no id, which made `PUT /api/me/preferences` a silent no-op, personal credentials unownable by construction, and the audit actor the bare string `admin`. They are now consumed **once**, at first boot, on a stack with **zero** user records, to create a real System-Admin **user record** — and never read again. The seeding lives in the orchestrator, which owns the store: the pure `orchestrator/initial-admin-seed.js` decides the input (`BOOTSTRAP_SYSTEM_ADMIN_SUBJECT`/`_EMAIL`/`_NAME` wins when set and seeds a **password-less** record for SSO stacks; else the `ADMIN_*` pair — a present-but-blank value is a deliberate opt-out, an absent one gets the shipped default), and `teamsStore.seedInitialSystemAdmin` owns the zero-users gate and writes one audit entry (`method: 'BOOTSTRAP'`, the same JSONL shape the console writes for every mutating request, pinned by a key-set diff against `audit.js`'s own `buildEvent`). It runs in `bootstrapTeams()` **before** `internalApi.listen(4002)`, which is what closes the start-order race from the inside — no login can be verified against an unseeded store. Consequences, all deliberate: **`verifyCredentials()` and the env-admin `all: true` branch of `scope.js` are deleted** (with the `adminUsername` parameter and the `envAdmin` session flag — the latter mattered because it was computed *independently* of `computeScope`, and `isKnownPrincipal` admitted an IAP identity on that flag alone, so leaving it would have re-opened the #1415 silent-empty-scope hole for anyone whose e-mail equalled `ADMIN_USERNAME`); `userId: null` is no longer a state an authenticated caller can be in; **while the user store is unreachable nobody can sign in**, so recovering an identity is a **host-level** action on `/workspace/users/index.json` (`docs/governance-concept.md` GOV-ACCESS-7, restated) and the login route answers **503 `user_store_unavailable`** rather than blaming the password, behind a 5 s timeout (Node's `fetch` has none, and the old fallback used to absorb the stall); and the seeded password **lives on the record**, so editing `.env` and restarting no longer revokes it — only the Users page does. Public `GET /api/auth/ready` (`{ready}` 200/503) reads the users index off the shared volume so a bring-up can wait for the seed before signing in (`e2e/scripts/post-up-config.sh`); the console starts without waiting for the orchestrator, so a premature login would otherwise look like bad credentials. **The session-cookie signing key is no longer derived from the credential pair** (`admin-console/api/session-secret.js`): `AUTH_SESSION_SECRET`, else a random 32-byte key persisted 0600 at `/workspace/auth/session-secret`, else process-random (sessions then do not survive a restart — failing safe). That derivation was defensible while the pair WAS the login; once the password lives in a record, an operator with a strong record password and a default `.env` would have had a *guessable* HMAC key, and a forged cookie is a login by another route. **The last System Admin cannot be deleted or demoted** (#2041 AC18): refused in the store with a 409 whose sentence the Users page mirrors on a disabled Delete and a locked System-Admin checkbox (`users-view.js` `lastSystemAdminGuard`, two-sided-tested against the store's own constants).

**Per-user local passwords (interim).** Users-page users (Users page, `/users`, Edit → Login password; #1844) can get a local scrypt password (never returned). Login checks the user store (`POST /internal/admin/users/verify`) and nothing else since #2059. A session's `sub` is the user's `subject`, so audit names the person.

### Admin Console audit trail

Every mutating `/api` request is one append-only JSONL line at `/workspace/audit/audit-<date>.jsonl` (override `AUDIT_DIR`). `admin-console/api/audit.js` `auditMutations` is mounted **before** the auth gate (so it sees denied attempts), logging from `res.on('finish')` (actor from `req.user`): `ts, actor, action, method, resource, teamId, status, outcome, ip`. Best-effort. Read via `GET /api/audit?limit=<n>`. Matrix chat history is intentionally **not** the config-mutation audit log.

### Admin Console error containment (#1974)

An uncaught throw during render unmounts the tree up to the nearest boundary — with none in the path, up to `#root`, i.e. a **blank white page**. The console previously had only two boundaries (`SafeMarkdown` in `Message.jsx`, `SkillMarkdown`), both wrapping only a chat message's markdown body, so every point-fix for an individual trigger (#1857, #506, #481) left the next unknown payload fatal. Three layers now contain it; they are **defense-in-depth, not a fix for any one trigger**:

1. **App/route level** — `RouteErrorBoundary` (`components/ErrorBoundary.js`) wraps `Suspense`/`Routes` **inside** `Shell` in `App.jsx`, so a page throw shows a recoverable card (Try again · Go back · Reload page) in the content area while the **navigation stays usable**. It resets on route change via a `resetKey` on `location.pathname` — deliberately *not* a React `key`, which would remount the page subtree on every navigation and discard Agent Chat's state on each `/chats/:agentId` change.
2. **Per event** — `EventErrorBoundary` wraps each transcript row (`ChatPanel.jsx`, via the extracted `renderTranscriptRow` so no future message-type branch can escape containment) and each session-log row (`SessionLogPanel.jsx`), degrading one malformed event to an inline placeholder while the rest keeps streaming. No `resetKey`: a malformed event is still malformed next render; it recovers when the virtualized row remounts.
3. **Markdown** — `SafeMarkdown` / `SkillMarkdown` stay the innermost layer, now built on the shared `components/markdown-safe.js` (`MarkdownSafeBoundary` + `plainTextFallback`). Both nest three tiers — **full pipeline → GFM-only → plain text** — where each tier is a boundary whose fallback is the next tier and the innermost fallback (`plainTextFallback`) **cannot throw** (it coerces the body with `String()` and touches no remark/rehype code). This fixed the actual reason the page kept dying (not a per-trigger patch): the old boundary's `fallback:true` degraded to a **GFM-only render that shares the same remark/rehype parse path** as the full pipeline, so a body that threw in *both* tiers (a `mdast-util-to-hast` "Cannot use 'in' operator to search for 'children' in undefined" on one message) made the boundary re-render the very fallback it had just seen throw — looping into React **#185** (max update depth), which escaped the per-row `EventErrorBoundary` and blanked the whole Agent Chat page. With every fallback infallible, no boundary re-renders a child it already saw throw, so a single unparseable message degrades to plain text in place regardless of content or dependency version. The primitive is authored JSX-free and unit-tested (`markdown-safe.test.js`); `error-boundary-wiring.test.js` AC7 is the #651 drift guard that both surfaces wire it down to the plain-text floor.

**Telemetry.** On catch, a PII-safe report (error name/message, component stack, route, agent/session/event ids — never message bodies, agent output, query strings or hashes) is posted with a bare `fetch` (never `apiCall`, whose 401 path would bounce the user to the login screen because a row failed to render) to `POST /api/client-errors` → one JSONL line at `/workspace/client-errors/client-errors-<date>.jsonl` (override `CLIENT_ERROR_DIR`) plus a `[client-error]` container-log line, which the existing Logs page already streams. Reports are **deduped by fingerprint and rate-limited per window** (one gate shared by every boundary in the tab) so a re-render loop cannot flood the endpoint; the server re-caps every field independently, since the client is by definition misbehaving when it calls. Purely observational — nothing reads it to make a decision, and there is no console UI for it.

`ErrorBoundary.js` is deliberately authored **JSX-free** (`React.createElement`) so the `admin-console-src` CI job can import and exercise the real class — this is the one component whose own behaviour must be tested rather than only source-guarded. Deterministic logic lives in the pure `components/error-boundary-view.js`; `ErrorBoundary.test.js` drives the class through React's boundary contract and `error-boundary-wiring.test.js` is the #651 source-reading guard that it stays mounted in all three places.

### GitHub poller event-transparency log

Separate from the audit trail: answers "what did the poller see and what happened to it". Every **fresh** event (survived seen-id dedup) is recorded — delivered, dropped by a protection layer, folded into a digest, or matched no subscriber. Purely observational. **Writer** `orchestrator/github-event-log.js` (from `_fanOutBatch`): `ts, eventId, repo, event, actor, createdAt, label`, `ref` (#1833 — the git ref a `push`/`create`/`delete` carries, `payload.ref`; `null` otherwise), `workItem` (#1827 — the PR/issue the event happened on: `{kind: 'pr'|'issue', number, title, url}`, extracted null-safe from the enriched `payload.pull_request`/`payload.issue`; an `issue_comment` on a PR arrives as `payload.issue` with a nested `pull_request` marker and is classified `kind: 'pr'` (#1848), so a PR comment reads `PR` not `Issue`; `null` for events with no work item, e.g. `push`; #1833 adds `closesIssues: [n]` on a PR work item — the issue(s) it resolves, parsed from the enriched PR body + head branch, omitted when none) + one `outcomes[]` entry per subscriber whose event-type filter matched (`{agentId, triggerId, status: delivered|dropped|digest, reason?, wakeLabel?}`); `wakeLabel` (#1846) is the label that satisfied the subscriber's `labelFilter` — set on outcomes that survived both the history-horizon and label-filter gates; `null` for any-label listeners (no `labelFilter`); absent on label-filter-dropped and history-horizon-dropped outcomes, and on rows written before #1846; `outcomes.length === 0` → `noMatchingTrigger: true`. **Internal label fast-path (#1858):** the poller is not the only writer — when a human applies a flow label through the GitHub Issues plugin (`writeLabel(...,'add')`), the Admin Console notifies the orchestrator (`POST /internal/github-label-applied`), which wakes the poller-subscribed listener immediately (bypassing the flaky Events API) and records its own row here tagged `source: 'internal-plugin'` with `status: delivered` outcomes. It also arms a time-bounded, consume-once dedup marker (`orchestrator/label-dedup-markers.js`), so when the poller later sees the same `*.labeled` event it drops the duplicate with `status: dropped`, `reason: 'internal-dedup'` (a legitimate re-label after the ±5-min window still fires normally). Poller-written rows carry no `source` field; the target trigger stays `transport: 'github'` (no config change) and labels set directly in the GitHub web UI stay poller-only. **PR enrichment (#1833 item 2):** every `pull_request*` event is enriched with title/`html_url`/`closesIssues` before it is logged — regardless of trigger outcome (delivered / throttled / digested / no-subscriber) — via `_enrichPullRequestPayload`, cached per event id in the batch's `enrichedPayloads` Map so a PR is fetched at most once. JSONL at `/workspace/github-event-log/`, best-effort, 7-day retention. **Reader** → `GET /api/github-events` (merges day files newest-first, cap 5000). **UI**: the **GitHub Event Log** page (`/github-events`) — the Processed-events table columns are **Time · Repository · Type · Work item · Title · Event · Actor · Outcomes** (Repository restored to an early, plainly-visible short-name column in #1833 — full `owner/name` on hover; **Type** (#1848) shows `PR`/`Issue` from `workItem.kind` — always-visible and sort/filterable via its own flat `workItemType` key, `—` for a work-item-less/legacy row, via the pure `admin-console/src/pages/github-event-log-type.js`; a work-item-less push/create/delete row appends its branch to the Event cell; a PR work item trails a linked `→ closes #<issue>`). **Third writer — direct agent-to-agent hand-offs (#2030/#2032):** `POST /internal/wake-agent` records one `source: 'internal'`, `event: 'handoff.wake'` row per hand-off, which is the whole audit trail for a mechanism that leaves nothing on GitHub. Such a row has no repo, label or work item and honestly shows `—` there; what makes it readable is the Event column's plain-language *"direct hand-off"*, the `delivered → <target>` outcome chip, and the **Title** column carrying the producer's note out of the row's own `handoff: {from, to, note?}` block — see the `wake-agent` trigger bullet above.

### Agent Team Wizard

`POST /api/teams` (wizard at `/team-setup`) materialises a team-shaped agent group in one atomic-with-rollback transaction on top of the `team`/`team-member` machinery. **Create-only via wizard** — growing an existing team happens in the Flow Editor, or via **Add member…** on the team's group header in the Agent Chat rail (the **Add Member** button on the Agents page card until #1930 removed that page; #1375's **Add role** button for a single missing loop-closing role is gone — #1959, see below). Steps: **Process → Working Environment → Team → Knowledge (optional) → Review**. It picks an existing env and sends `environmentId` (backend resolves repo/branch/SSH/PAT/MCP); also accepts legacy direct inputs (`repository` + `githubTokenId` + `mcpServers`, auto-creating env id `<teamId>`). Always ships a `processTemplateVersion: 1` bundle; `mode: "extend"` only on an id collision. Accepts optional `labelSuffix` (1–8 chars of `[a-z0-9]` with single inner hyphens, e.g. `k3f9` / `team-1` — #1934) — stamps every label token (trigger `labelFilter`, channel `label`, `setLabel` actions, bundle label registry, prompt text) before agents are created, so parallel teams sharing one GitHub repo don't cross-trigger each other. `GET /api/teams/suggest-suffix` returns a random 4-char suggestion. Extend-mode enforces that the suffix matches the stored one.

**Process step** (`StepProcess.jsx`) lands **directly on the template/draft gallery** — the sole entry (#1703). It lists the curated registry (`orchestrator/skill-pool/process-templates/` → `make seed-process-templates` → `GET /api/process-templates`; ships `core-sdlc` + `extended-sdlc`) plus the user's saved drafts. The former three-card chooser is gone: **Describe the flow** (plain-language → Flow Copilot `POST /api/process/copilot`) and **Start blank in the canvas** (empty draft → Flows editor) moved to the unified **"+ Create" launcher** (#1665, `/create`) — the wizard keeps only a lightweight "need a custom flow? use + Create" pointer (and an empty-state link to `/create` when no templates/drafts exist). Selecting a template renders the inline preview + authoritative lints (`POST /api/flows/graph`); error lints block submission. Exits: **Create team** (`POST /api/teams/stream`, full parity) or **Save as draft**.

**Role templates** live under `/template-workspace/agent-templates/` (from `orchestrator/skill-pool/agent-templates/` via `make seed-templates`). **Deliberately rudimentary**: parameter-free prompts (only weave-time `{{stageName}}`/`{{stagePrefix}}` remain), one role-specific *wake* (split into one trigger **per event** — #1008 wiring conventions: single-event triggers, claims on label listeners, `terminal`/`external` markers on flow-ending outcomes, CI-guarded in `process-template-registry.test.js`) + a daily self-curation cron (staggered in the 03:00 hour, `intrinsic: true`) pruning `memory/`. 24 roles; sample:

- `business-analyst` (persistent) — `issues.labeled` `req:refine` (intake pair with `issue-triage`)
- `developer` (per-invocation) — wiring from the flow; `code-reviewer`/`security-`/`test-`/`performance-reviewer` — `pull_request.opened/reopened`
- `reworker` — `pull_request.labeled` `revisions-required`; `documentation-sync` — `pull_request.closed` (merged-guard)
- `issue-triage` — `issues.opened`, emits `req:refine`; `dispatcher` — `issues.labeled` `ready`, releases via `dispatch`
- `generic` (persistent) — blank slate, no curation cron

**Materialisation:** one shared env (reused or auto-created id `<teamId>`); one `team` agent id `<teamId>` (`skipFirstMember: true`); one `team-member` per role id `<teamId>-<roleId>` carrying `systemPrompt` (role override), `sessionPolicy: per-invocation`, and a full `triggers.json` (`{{repoFullName}}`/`{{agentId}}` substituted); one DM room per member (best-effort, materialize-now mode only — see below). `skillsOverride` omitted — members inherit the team `skills[]`. On any failure, roll back members → team → env; a failure card (`progressError && !result && !collisionInfo`) surfaces this with a **Retry** button that re-calls `submit('create')` (which resets all error/progress state at its top). **Disabled-button affordance & reason (#2157):** the submit/Retry/*Add missing agents* buttons in `LaunchStep.jsx` share one `primaryStyle(disabled)` helper — `opacity`/`cursor`, the same house pattern used ~15 places elsewhere — so a disabled button is never pixel-identical to a live one; the disabled condition and its explanation are single-sourced through `launchBlockReason` (settling the team id first, so a trailing dash mid-edit never flickers the reason) instead of a separate `envBlocking` boolean deciding one thing and `validateLaunch` explaining another, and the reason renders as persistent text next to the button, not only a hover title. `MaterializeDialog`'s `/api/environments` fetch no longer folds a failed load into an empty list (the inline `.catch(() => [])` is gone), so a 403/500/network failure surfaces `envError` instead of the false *"No working environment exists yet."* Members are later editable with the three-mode prompt-mode selector (#1511 — Inherit from team / Additional input / Exclusive overriding input) and two-state Inherit ↔ Custom toggles for Skills and MCP servers. **Orphan adoption (#1525):** config-less / `_provisioning`-stub leftover dirs from a prior aborted create are cleaned through `DELETE /internal/agents/:id` (Phase 0, before env creation) — a same-id retry now recovers without picking a new team id. A member id held by an unrelated agent yields a `409 conflict-members` (never mis-routed to `extend`). A rollback that cannot delete an agent surfaces `rollbackWarnings[{id, kind, error}]` in the 500 JSON (non-streaming) or a `type:'warning'` SSE event (streaming) naming the leftover, instead of swallowing the error silently. Shared pure decision: `admin-console/api/team-residue.js` (`classifyMemberResidue` / `decideTeamCreate`). **Deferred materialization (#1955):** the request body's `materializeMembers` (default `true`, read as `!== false`) decides how far each member is taken, and is honoured by **both** `POST /api/teams` and `POST /api/teams/stream` — it used to be read only on the non-streaming handler, which made the launch step's *Materialize agents now* checkbox inert, since both UI surfaces stream. When `false`, `buildMemberFromTemplate` passes `materialize: false` and the orchestrator writes the member as a **STUB** (config + `triggers.json`, `enabled: false`, no Matrix user / workspace seed / clone / process; `enableAgent` does the full provisioning on first activation) — so the stream labels the step `Create member (stub): …`, **neither plans nor runs** the `dms` phase (a stub has no Matrix user; the two `toCreate.length > 0 && materializeMembers` gates must agree or the wizard renders a step that never completes), and echoes `materialized` in `complete.result`. The clone-warning-as-fatal check stays in place but is inert for a stub (no workspace is seeded, so no warnings come back). The result card's wording is the shared pure `launchResultMessage` (`admin-console/src/pages/process/launch-team.js`) — an **absent** `materialized` key reads as the backend default, never as a stub, so a pre-#1955 payload is not relabelled. Defaults differ per surface: the Team-Setup wizard sends an explicit `materializeNow: true`, the canvas' `MaterializeDialog` defaults it **off**. Source-reading drift guard: `admin-console/api/team-stream-materialize.test.js`. **Repo-less materialization (#1623):** when the reused-env path (`environmentId` provided) targets a repo-less environment, a `repoLess` flag is set — repo URL / SSH-key validation and `deriveRepoIdentity` are skipped; `{{repoFullName}}`/`{{repoOwner}}`/`{{repoName}}`/`{{repoUrl}}` all resolve to empty strings (via `repoVars(null,'')` in `team-repo-resolve.js`); `defaultTeamPrompt` drops the repository sentence and the `gh-as-agent` line. The legacy auto-create-env path (no `environmentId`) still requires a repo. `resolveTeamMemberVars` (used by `POST /api/agents/:teamId/members`) is equally repo-less-safe so roles can be added to a repo-less team post-materialization.

**Missing-role suggestion — REMOVED (#1959, was #1375/#1556).** The platform used to infer which loop-closing role an Agent Team was missing relative to a reference process template and offer a one-click **Add `<role>` role** (a warning block on the expanded Agents-page team card, extra entries in the Agent-Chat team menu). **Gone — retired by requester decision, not re-homed.** The inference was unfalsifiable in practice: a team never persists which template it came from (`POST /api/teams` flattens the bundle and discards the template identity), so the source had to be guessed from the label registry + member ids; deliberately conservative, it stayed silent on many teams with a real gap, and where it fired "missing" was a claim about a template the team may have diverged from on purpose. Deleted with it: `admin-console/src/pages/missing-roles-view.js` (`inferMissingRoles`/`fulfilledRoleIds`/`presentRoleIds`), the `missingRoles` parameter of `buildTeamMenu` and its `add-role:*` entries, `parseMenuKey` in `agent-chat/rail-lifecycle.js` (no menu key carries an argument any more), and the `{ processTemplateId, roleId }` branch of `POST /api/agents/:teamId/members` — that body shape is now **refused** (`400`, code `process-template-role-removed`) rather than falling through to the free-form branch, which would have created a member named after the role but without the template's prompt, session policy or triggers. Growing a team is **Add member** (the `templateId` / free-form branches of the same route) or the Flow editor. Two removal guards keep it gone: `admin-console/api/process-role-member-removed.test.js` and `admin-console/src/pages/missing-roles-removed.test.js`.

**Deferred materialization of a single member (#1958).** The two surviving branches of `POST /api/agents/:teamId/members` were "unchanged" only until this ticket: the route never passed a materialization preference, so `addTeamMember`'s materializing default always won even though `opts.materialize === false` (stub: config + `triggers.json`, `enabled: false`, no Matrix user / clone / container) had been supported since #1955. The pure `admin-console/api/member-materialize.js` (`parseMemberMaterialize`) now reads it: **absent ⇒ materialize now** (so every pre-#1958 caller, including a body-less POST, is byte-identically unaffected), present-but-not-a-boolean ⇒ **400** rather than a coercion — a caller that sends `"false"` believes it deferred, and cloning a repository behind its back is the exact failure this closes. The resolved value is written onto the outgoing body **explicitly** (`let outBody = { ...inBody, materialize }`, and handed to `buildMemberFromTemplate` on the `templateId` branch, which emits the key only when defined) so the two branches cannot drift in what they forward. Client-side the rail's **Add member** stops being a bare POST and opens a dialog carrying a **Materialize now** checkbox, default **on** — pure copy/body/label/notice decisions in `admin-console/src/pages/agent-chat/add-member-view.js`, rendered through the generic `ConfirmDialog` (its `message` takes a ReactNode, so one control needs no bespoke dialog). Two things worth knowing: (1) the success notice reads the mode the **server** reports, and an **absent** `materialized` key is the documented backend default (the materializing branch of `addTeamMember` returns no such key) rather than a guessed stub — same rule as `launchResultMessage`; (2) the "a dialog follows" ellipsis is added by the chat-only `withChatDialogLabels` in `agent-chat/rail-lifecycle.js`, **not** in the shared `buildTeamMenu` descriptor, because the Agents page's own **Add member** was still a bare materializing POST and pushing the ellipsis down would have made that page promise a dialog it did not have (the one-key/two-wordings technique #1957 used for `toggle-enabled`). #1930 removed that page, so the chat is the descriptor's only renderer now: the code is unchanged, but the reason to keep the wording chat-side is the technique itself rather than a second surface it would misdescribe. The create-then-disable workaround this replaces — create the member, then `PUT {enabled:false}`, yielding a *paused* agent whose clone and container had already happened — was removed in #1959; `rail-lifecycle-wiring.test.js` now carries a guard that keeps that shape out, since the checkbox is what it was standing in for. AC3 needs no new code: the orchestrator's stub config is `role: 'team-member'` with a `parentTeamId`, which is exactly `isStubMember`, so #1957's *Not materialized yet* section picks it up on the ordinary post-mutation refetch.

**Save as template (#1509).** A proven live agent — standalone chat agent or individual team member — can be snapshotted into a reusable, team-neutral **user-tier Agent Template**. It shipped as the **Save as template** (BookmarkPlus) icon on the Agents page's `LeafAgentCard` (standalone agents) and `AgentRow` (team members), with whole-team and orchestrator cards excluded; since #1928 it is the **Save as template…** entry of the Agent Chat rail's row context menu — the `extract-template` key of the pure `agent-lifecycle/agent-lifecycle-menu.js` `buildAgentMenu`, offered for exactly those two roles (`CLONEABLE_ROLES` = `agent` + `team-member`, a `team` row getting `buildTeamMenu` instead) and inert on a stub — dispatched by `RailLifecycleHost.jsx` into the same `ExtractTemplateForm` review dialog. #1930 deleted the icon's two host components with the page; the menu entry is the only surface left. The review dialog pre-fills a unique template id (deduped across both tiers), a display name, an editable description, an inferred+editable role type / pattern role, and an **Include triggers** checkbox (default off). On confirm, `POST /api/agents/:id/extract-template` resolves the source's **effective** prompt (`readAgentSystemPrompt(id)` falling back to `cfg.systemPrompt` — the same source as the export path), effective skill grant (member `skillsOverride` → own `skills[]` → parent team's `skills[]`), and `sessionPolicy`, then builds a team-neutral template through the pure `admin-console/api/extract-template.js` module (`buildExtractedTemplate` whitelist builder) and validates via `agentTemplateStore.validateTemplate`. Derived flow-wiring triggers (`dataFlow`/`channel`) and unsupported types (e.g. `loop`) are dropped; runtime/identity and team-composition fields (`userId`/`token`/`teamId`/`emits`/`channels`/`outcomes`) are never carried — the builder is a whitelist, not a stripper. Saved to `USER_AGENT_TEMPLATES_DIR` with `origin: 'user'` (appears with a **Custom** badge on the Agent Templates page); 409 on id conflict — the UI re-suggests. The source agent is left completely unchanged.

### Direct Messages & Session Log

Each agent runs:

```
claude [--resume <sessionId>] --print \
  --output-format stream-json --verbose --permission-mode acceptEdits
```

The user prompt is written to `claude` on **stdin** (via a daemon writer thread), not passed as an argv argument — so a message that *begins* with an option-like character (`-`, en-dash `–`, German quote `„`, a `--help`-style token) is never mis-parsed by the CLI as an unknown flag (#1727); this is why the invocation uses the bare boolean `--print` rather than `-p <prompt>`. NDJSON stdout is parsed and written with wrapper events (`message.received`, `claude.invoke`, `message.posted`, `error`, `trigger.fired`) to `/workspace/agents/<agentId>/sessions/<roomId>.jsonl` (schema: `docs/agent-chat-plan.md` §4.1). Every entry carries a `correlationId` (Matrix `event_id`, or `trigger:<id>:<ts>`) tying log rows to chat bubbles.

**Per-room session pinning.** After each successful run the runner writes `session_id` to `<roomId>.current.json`; the next message in that room uses `--resume` (on failure it clears the pointer and retries once). `GET`/`DELETE /api/agents/:agentId/session/:roomId/current` read/drop it; the chat header toolbar shows *Reset Session* directly, with *Clear History* under a `…` overflow menu (#1810, re-pointed in #1922 — the manual *Save & Learn* button was removed as redundant with the automatic per-invocation memory hint, and *Reset Session* took the primary slot). Snapshots live under `~/.claude/projects/<encoded-cwd>/` (persisted via the `claude-home` volume so `--resume` survives restarts).

**Parent-ticket binding (#1383).** A chat room can be bound to a parent GitHub issue for traceability (issue ⇄ conversation). The binding is **room-scoped** (persists across *Reset Session* — it is a separate `<roomId>.ticket.json`, not the session pointer). `GET`/`PUT`/`DELETE /api/agents/:id/session/:roomId/ticket` read/bind/unbind it; the console proxies the GitHub work to the orchestrator (`POST`/`GET`/`DELETE /internal/agents/:id/ticket`), which creates a new issue (`gh api`) or resolves an existing one (issue number or URL) with the env PAT and materialises `ticket.json` at the agent workspace root so a running agent reads it on its next invocation. Agents register child issues on the parent via `POST /internal/agents/:id/ticket/children`. Each bind/unbind appends a `ticket.bound`/`ticket.unbound` event to `<roomId>.jsonl`. Pure parsing/shapes in `orchestrator/ticket-store.js` (unit-tested). The backend endpoints remain; the Agent-Chat UI surfaces (toolbar chip, bind dialog, Sessions-panel banner, transcript rows, and the `ticket-view.js` display helpers) were removed in #1607.

**Chat-turn gate** (`agent-entrypoint.py`, `is_human_sender` — #2173, replaces the old exactly-two-members DM heuristic): act iff the sender is a joined member of the room (cached 30s) AND the sender isn't this agent — so the one shared per-agent room can hold this agent plus any number of humans, each posting under their own Matrix identity (`human-matrix-store`, provisioned since #1458) rather than the shared admin account.

### Agent Chat UI

The Admin Console embeds a Matrix client at `/chats` (+ `/chats/:agentId`). Under `admin-console/src/pages/`: `AgentChat.jsx` (top-level) + `agent-chat/` (`AgentList` — left-rail list with drag&drop reordering (`Alt+↑`/`Alt+↓` keyboard path); each row shows its Working Environment name below the agent name, except inside a group where all rows share one environment — in that case the env is hoisted to the group header and member rows go single-line (#1451); a deviating member always keeps its own line; `agent-list-dnd.js` — pure `computeReorder`/`dropPosition` helpers (unit-tested, `admin-console-src` CI job); `agent-list-env.js` — pure `groupEnvironmentName`/`rowEnvironmentName` helpers for the environment-hoist logic (unit-tested, `admin-console-src` CI job); `agent-list-model.js` — pure `buildGroups`/`orderModel`/`filterModel`/`groupKey` list-model helpers that `AgentList` runs in three layered `useMemo`s (grouping keyed on `[agents]` alone) so drag/render churn never re-groups (#1682) (unit-tested, `admin-console-src` CI job); **lifecycle quick actions (#1928, epic #1925 Phase 1)** — the rail hosts the agent/team management actions that used to live only on the Agents page. `AgentList` stays presentational: it reports `onRequestMenu(target, anchor)` from a hover kebab, a right-click and the `Menu`/`Shift+F10` keys (open question 1: *both* triggers) and `RailLifecycleHost.jsx` owns the menu, every dialog and every request behind it — the dialogs/forms are the Agents page's own, extracted unchanged into `pages/agent-lifecycle/` (`AgentLifecycleDialogs.jsx`, `AgentLifecycleForms.jsx`, `form-kit.jsx`) and rendered by both surfaces while both existed, so a quick action could not drift into a second, differently-warned copy of an irreversible operation — which is what made #1930 a deletion rather than a rewrite. Which entries a row/header gets — and when each is inert — is the pure `agent-lifecycle/agent-lifecycle-menu.js` (`buildAgentMenu`/`buildTeamMenu`/`agentState`/`isChatOpenable`/`deleteDialogCopy`); the rail-side decisions (menu placement, the `(agent, agents)` lookups the thin chat payload can't answer alone) are the pure `agent-chat/rail-lifecycle.js` — both unit-tested, `admin-console-src` CI job, with `rail-lifecycle-wiring.test.js` as the source-reading drift guard over the JSX the pure tests cannot load (#651). The **disabled-agents opt-in** (epic decision D2) is off by default and persisted per user under `agentChat.showDisabled` (the rail's own namespace, sibling of `collapsedGroups`/`sidebarOrder`); it is the only *preference* that widens the list URL, adding `includeDisabled=1` to `GET /api/matrix/agents` (#1957 added a second, unconditional `includeStubs=1` — see below — so the URL is no longer byte-for-byte the pre-#1928 one, but `includeDisabled` is still present only when the opt-in is on, never as `=0`). That route ships two extra per-entry fields for the menus: `hasToken` (never the token itself, so the shared `agentState()` can tell *paused* from *stub*) and `canManage` (the same `canManageAgent` boundary the mutating routes enforce, so the menu disables what the server would refuse — epic decision D1; server-side authorisation is unchanged). A listed disabled agent is marked disabled and is not chat-openable — clicking its row opens its own menu, where **Enable** sits. **Not-yet-materialized members (#1957, epic #1925).** The list route dropped tokenless agents *unconditionally*, so a team created with `materializeMembers: false` (#1955) read as empty in the chat; `?includeStubs=1` now admits **stub team members** (config only — no Matrix user, no workspace, no container), and the chat always sends it, because a stub is not a paused agent someone chose to hide. The admission rule is the pure `admin-console/api/matrix-agent-list.js` (`isStubMember` — deliberately narrower than the client `agentState()`: `role: 'team-member'` **with** a `parentTeamId`, so a stub bp-instance stays out and every stub in the payload has a group to sit in; `admitsToChatList` — the two opt-ins are independent, so the default response is unchanged for every other consumer). Client-side, `buildGroups` splits each team group's `stubs[]` out of `children[]` — keeping the stored member order, the member drag scope and the group count describing exactly the rows they used to — `passesDisabledFilter` exempts stubs (folding them into the disabled opt-in would make that filter silently mean two states), and `AgentList` renders them under a locally-collapsed **"Not materialized yet (N)"** sub-header (`stubSectionLabel`; never persisted — the section empties itself as members are materialized). A stub row is badged **Not initialised** with its own muted dot, is not chat-openable (so no DM room is ever requested and `/chats/<stub-id>` cannot resolve), and its menu's `toggle-enabled` entry is relabelled **Materialize** — the same key, hence the same `PUT /api/agents/:id {enabled:true}` the Agents page's *Activate* used to send, hence `enableAgent`'s full provisioning. The team lock (`isTeamLocked`) and `canManage` gate it exactly as they gate Enable, and the orchestrator's `team-disabled` 409 already relays verbatim. `RailLifecycleHost` reports its `busyIds` up through `onBusyIdsChange` so the row spins while a clone-bearing materialize runs, and the ordinary post-mutation refetch is what moves the row up into the active members. **Mark as unread (#1938)** is that menu's first entry, separated from the lifecycle group (`separated: true` on the following item, so the menu keeps a flat list of real, focusable entries) — the epic's D3 "context menu, not icon rows" applied to the rail's last hover icon, which is why `AgentRow`'s trailing cluster is now kebab/grip/dot only and its grip never shifts on hover. It is chat-only, so it is prepended in `rail-lifecycle.js` rather than in the `buildAgentMenu` descriptor the Agents page then shared (that page had no unread concept); it is a **local view action** — `RailLifecycleHost` dispatches it straight to the chat's own `handleMarkAsUnread` with no request — so it is enabled purely from the row's unread + chat-openable state (`canMarkUnread`) and never from `canManage`, and when it does not apply it is greyed out with no hint rather than omitted. **Agent configuration (#1929, epic #1925 Phase 2)** — the rail's **Settings** tab (`AgentSettingsPanel.jsx`) hosts the agent's own edit form, which followed the same extraction route: `AgentEditForm` and the field controls the create form shares with it moved unchanged into `pages/agent-lifecycle/` (`AgentEditForm.jsx`, `agent-form-fields.jsx`), so the Agents page's edit panel and the chat tab rendered **one** component (a field added later appeared in both) and saved through the same `PUT /api/agents/:id`; since #1930 the chat tab is that component's only host. Its shape logic is the pure `agent-lifecycle/agent-edit-form-model.js` (`buildInitialForm`/`buildEditSubmit`/`isFormDirty`) — the save shape is the contract the two surfaces must produce identically, and `isFormDirty` is what makes a draft worth keeping. The chat-only decisions are the pure `agent-chat/agent-settings-view.js`: `settingsSubject` (the tab follows the open chat, is **hidden** when the list payload's `canManage` is false — parity with what the server would refuse — and can be pointed at another agent by a rail menu, which is how a **team**'s settings and a **disabled** agent's are reachable at all), the save/restart notices, and the per-agent **draft store** (`putDraft`/`getDraft`/`dropDraft`). Unsaved edits are preserved rather than confirmed away: `agentId` changing does not unmount the panel, so switching agents and back re-mounts the form from its draft behind an "unsaved changes" banner with a Discard — a list click is never blocked by a modal. Plugin grants and context mounts keep their own immediate-apply endpoints; the chat additionally passes `confirmRestart` so a context-folder change asks before the re-provision (which ends the run the user is watching) and `onImmediateApply` so the panel can say what happened. `AgentEditForm`'s host hooks (`initialForm`/`onFormChange`/`confirmRestart`/`onImmediateApply`/`cancelLabel`) are all optional and were unused by the Agents page, which #1929 left byte-for-byte unchanged (and #1930 deleted). Phase 1's `edit-team` jump to `/agents` is replaced by **Team settings…**, which opens this tab; `agent-settings-wiring.test.js` is the source-reading drift guard (#651) over the shared-form imports, the tab registration and those wirings. **List actions in the page header (#1956, epic #1925).** `/chats` was the only Shell route that portalled nothing into the header's page-action slot (`PageActionsContext`), which is *why* its create/import had to be invented locally as a rail `+` menu; it now portals a secondary **Import Agent** and a primary **New Agent** like the twelve other pages, and the `+` (`RailHeaderButton`) plus `buildCreateMenu` are **gone** — AC2 is that neither action is reachable from two interactive places, so they are removed, not merely unrendered (guarded in `rail-lifecycle-wiring.test.js`). The rail header row itself stays (filter · disabled opt-in · the list's "?"), and its empty state becomes a **text pointer** at the header button rather than a competing CTA, matching `WorkingEnvironments.jsx` (renamed `EnvironmentTemplates.jsx` by #2170)/`Briefings.jsx` (pure `agent-chat/empty-state.js`: `emptyStatePointer()` replaced `emptyStateCta()`). **Import** stays one implementation: the file input and the shared `ImportAgentForm` remain in `RailLifecycleHost`, which is now a `forwardRef` exposing a single `openImportPicker()` handle the header button calls — one click to the picker, the id/team dialog after a file is chosen, still a refetch and no reload. Because the header knows nothing about the rail's filter, the success notice runs the pure `importedAgentFilterNote(listFilter, …)` and says so when a narrowed filter would swallow the new agent. **Role gating** is the pure `agent-lifecycle/agent-create-access.js` (`describeAgentCreateAccess(user)`), the client mirror of `canManageAgent`: full-visibility admin, or Team Admin of *some* team. Both buttons are **disabled with the reason in `title`**, deliberately not hidden the way `Teams.jsx` hides its admin actions — the header slot is the only home these two actions have left, and an empty header answers "where did New Agent go?" with silence. **The create form followed the same extraction route** as the dialogs (#1928) and the edit form (#1929): `AgentCreateForm` (with the `RoleCard` picker only it used) moved out of `Agents.jsx` into `agent-lifecycle/AgentCreateForm.jsx`, and the `/create` launcher gained a fifth entry — *Create a single agent*, renamed **Configure an agent manually** and moved behind the *Advanced options* disclosure by #2027 — that hosts it in a side panel keyed on `?form=agent` (pure `isSingleAgentFormOpen` / `createdAgentDestination` in `create-launcher-view.js`; the destination is data because a `team` agent hosts no chat and deep-linking to one would be a dead end). Same fields, same `describeAgentSubmitState` gate, same `POST /api/agents` — the Agents page and `/create` rendered **one** component, which was the precondition for retiring the Agents page — met by #1930, leaving `/create` its only host. `agent-create-form-shared.test.js` is the #651 guard over that. That entry is also the one launcher entry that names the artifact: #1665 AC7's jargon-free-title rule is narrowed to the *intent* entries rather than dropped, because the direct path's whole value is that it says what it makes. **Ranked into three tiers (#2027).** The five entries stopped being the flat equally-ranked grid of #1661's D1 — which #2027 deliberately supersedes, so the `hasForcedDefault()` helper that asserted it in `create-launcher-view.js` is **deleted** rather than left asserting a rule the product no longer follows — and are now ordered simplest → most advanced across a full-width **hero** (`chat` → `/quickstart`, badge *Fastest start*, the only entry carrying its own button label *Start chatting*), one bordered **flow** group of three cards under a heading that finally names the concept and says all three end in the same flow (`template` → `/team-setup` badged *Recommended*, `describe` → `/flows?new=describe`, `build` → `/flows?new=blank` badged *Advanced*), and a collapsed **advanced** disclosure holding the specialist form (`ADVANCED_DEFAULT_OPEN = false`, collapsed on every load for every user and deliberately **not** persisted — a specialist re-opening one disclosure is cheaper than a newcomer meeting five equal options again). Why: five flat tiles answered "what can I do" but not "where do I begin", the two single-agent paths read as near-duplicates with the *expert* one sounding simplest, the three flow paths were never named as a flow, and no tile said whether you land in a chat, an editor or an empty canvas. The tier is data on each entry (`CREATE_TIERS`/`entriesByTier`/`heroEntry`) and the array order IS the display order (no separate rank field to drift); page/group copy is single-sourced in `CREATE_COPY`. **Nothing is rewired** — every entry hands off to the route it always did, so each target's own empty-state guidance is inherited rather than re-implemented. Every entry also carries an `outcome` line (`→ …`, what kind of screen it opens), and a `preview` (`{schematic, caption}`) whose schematic is referenced **by name** into `create-previews.jsx` — the same indirection as the lucide icon map, keeping the pure module React/lucide-free; the reveal fires on hover **and** keyboard focus plus an eye `<button>` that is a SIBLING of the card's `<Link>` (a `<button>` inside an `<a>` is invalid HTML and would swallow the card's tap), and it is a plain mount/unmount with **no** animation, which is why `prefers-reduced-motion` needs no separate branch. **The preview is the flow tier's only, since #2034**: #2027 gave one to all five entries, but on the hero it was a large drawing of a chat window (self-evident, and the biggest thing on the page) and on the disclosure entry a drawing of a form for the one reader who least needs it — so `preview` is now an **optional** field, present on `template`/`describe`/`build` and absent on `chat`/`single-agent`, and the pure `hasPreview(entry)` (a NAMED schematic, not merely a `preview` object — an unnamed one would put an eye control in front of an empty panel) is the renderer's one branch. Three consequences worth knowing: (1) the reveal path is **absent, not hidden** on those two cards, per the ticket's own edge case — `EntryCard` early-returns a bare `EntryLink` (the card markup, extracted so it can be rendered without the reveal) while `PreviewableEntryCard` owns the hover/pinned state, so a preview-less card has no eye `<button>`, no schematic and no focus/hover handler to leave an orphan focus target or screen-reader label behind; (2) the two now-unclaimed schematics (`chat`, `form`) were **deleted** from `CREATE_PREVIEWS` rather than left behind their removed entries, because `create-launcher-wiring.test.js` asserts the map and the entries correspond in *both* directions (a schematic no entry names is dead weight that reads as coverage); (3) the entries' own copy is untouched — both cards keep their title, body, badge and `outcome` line, which is what the copy-inventory test still asserts verbatim. Two strings deviate from #2027's otherwise-binding copy inventory, both mandated by its own edge case (the guided empty-canvas state is out of scope): `build`'s outcome is `→ Opens the canvas`, not "…with a guided first step", and its caption promises only a prompt to add the first agent — verified against `EmptyCanvasState` in `ProcessCanvas.jsx` and `flow-graph-render.js`'s zero-visible-node early return. `ChatPanel` — backfill/pagination/optimistic-send/SSE tail/session toolbar; the transcript list is **windowed** (#1685 — only the on-screen bubbles plus a small overscan mount, via the shared `VirtualList` glue over the pure `virtual-window.js`; callers keep owning the scroller so load-older / atBottom / unread logic is unchanged). Because off-screen rows are estimated until measured, a one-shot `scrollTop = scrollHeight` lands short of the true bottom, so **stick-to-bottom** is owned by `VirtualList`'s `stickToBottom` prop — driven by ChatPanel's `atBottom` intent, it re-pins after each measuring pass and converges on the newest message (opening/switching a chat, sending, the "↓ new messages" pill, and a streaming reply) while a deliberate scroll-up stops the re-pin (#1710); the 100 px bottom/near-top threshold decisions are the pure `chat-scroll.js` (`isAtBottom`/`isNearTop`/`distanceFromBottom`, unit-tested, `admin-console-src` CI job); a per-user transcript **font-size** −/+ stepper moved to **Settings → Display** (#1437/#1806 — default 12px, range 10–18px; the message scroll container sets a scoped `--chat-font-size` CSS var the message bubble reads; persisted server-side on the user record via `PUT /api/me/preferences` → session `chatFontSize`, with a per-username `agentChat.fontSize.<username>` localStorage fallback when the server cannot be reached — before #2059 it was also what the recordless env single-admin fell back to permanently; `notifyFontSizeChanged` dispatches `FONT_SIZE_CHANGED_EVENT` so the active ChatPanel picks up changes immediately without a remount; pure clamp/step/persist/notify logic in `chat-font-size.js`, unit-tested, `admin-console-src` CI job); `Message` — markdown via `react-markdown` + `remark-gfm` + `rehype-pretty-code` + `rehype-sanitize`; **quick-reply buttons (#1663)** — a run that paused on Claude's `AskUserQuestion` tool posts the terminal message with a structured `io.aee.ask-question` payload (single-choice only — `multiSelect` degrades to the text body, D1). Because the *built-in* `AskUserQuestion` is interactive-TTY-only and the CLI removes it from the model's tool set in headless `-p` mode, the agent runtime re-supplies an identically-shaped `AskUserQuestion` from a bundled, dependency-free stdio MCP server (`agent-image/scripts/ask-user-mcp.py`, registered via `--mcp-config` in `agent-entrypoint.py`) so agents can actually emit it (#1712); it surfaces as `mcp__aee-ask__AskUserQuestion` and the runner intercepts either the namespaced or the bare name. The payload is emitted *alongside* the human-readable Markdown body (so non-console clients still see the question + options, AC8); `AskQuestionCard.jsx` renders each option as a clickable control (label + optional always-visible description); for a **single-question** payload clicking one sends the option's **label** as the reply plus a structured `io.aee.ask-answer` identifier referencing the question message (AC2). A **multi-question** payload (#1799) is answered *sequentially* — one question at a time behind a "Question X of N" progress footer, each intermediate choice held client-side (`AskQuestionCard`'s local `openIdx`/`pendingAnswers`) with **no** message sent, and a single **consolidated** reply (a text body of the chosen labels + one `io.aee.ask-answer` carrying an `answers[]` array) sent only after the last question so the agent resumes once with every choice (`ChatPanel`'s `handleAnswerQuestion` detects the consolidated `{ isMulti, answers }` shape; `multiSelect` *within* one question still degrades to the text body, D1). A question is answerable only while it is the newest message and unanswered — a later message (the optimistic answer, a free-text reply, or a newer agent turn) makes it inert (AC5/AC6, superseded/duplicate-click cases); the chosen option is marked on reload. Pure decisions (`normalizeAskQuestion`/`buildAnswerContent`/`buildMultiAnswerContent`/`multiAnswerText`/`questionProgressLabel`/`indexAskAnswers`/`questionState` — the latter now also returns `multiAnswers` for a consolidated answer) in `ask-question-view.js` (unit-tested, `admin-console-src` CI job). `POST /api/matrix/rooms/:roomId/messages` accepts an optional `extraContent` whose `io.aee.*` keys it merges onto the sent message content; `MessageInput` — textarea composer; user-configurable send keybinding (`agentChat.sendKeybinding`: `ide` = Cmd/Ctrl+Enter sends / Enter newline, default; `chat` = Enter sends / Shift+Enter newline) with OS-aware (`⌘`/`Ctrl`) placeholder + Send-tooltip hints. The keybinding *selector* moved out of the composer footer into **Settings → Behavior** (`SettingsModal.jsx`, #1804); the composer is now a read-only consumer that re-reads the preset on the same-tab `SEND_KEYBINDING_CHANGED_EVENT` (dispatched by the Settings control on change) and the cross-tab `storage` event, so a change takes effect in the active chat immediately without a reload; `message-send-keybinding.js` — pure `decideSendKey`/`placeholderSendHint`/`sendKeybindingOptions` + the change-signal helpers (unit-tested, `admin-console-src` CI job); per-agent composer draft persistence (`agentChat.draft.<agentId>`) so a typed draft survives the agent-switch remount, pure logic in `message-draft.js` (unit-tested, `admin-console-src` CI job); a **slash-command palette** (#1797 — typing `/` as the composer's first character opens a filterable, keyboard-navigable (`↑`/`↓`/Enter/Esc/Tab) dropdown of the agent's provisioned slash commands (name + description) fetched per-agent from `GET /api/agents/:id/commands`; selecting one inserts `/<command> ` with the caret positioned for arguments; `/` typed mid-message never opens it, an agent with no commands never flashes an empty palette, and the palette owns Enter while open so it never conflicts with the send keybinding; pure decisions in `command-palette.js` — `commandQuery`/`filterCommands`/`commandInsertion`/`nextIndex`, unit-tested, `admin-console-src` CI job); a **unified Stop control** (#1427 — while the agent is busy on a chat turn *or* a launched loop the primary-action button flips from Send to a single Stop ⏹ that reuses the session-stop path `POST /api/agents/:id/session/:roomId/stop`; the loop button becomes launch-only, never a second stop; a turn stop drops a distinct client-side "Stopped by user" transcript marker; Send/Stop mode decision in pure `composer-action.js`, unit-tested, `admin-console-src` CI job); a per-agent **model/provider selector** in that toolbar (#1670 — `ChatModelSelector.jsx`, the runtime selector that changes which provider+model *the agent runs on*, permission-gated + server-persisted; its closed trigger button shows the effective selection (per-agent override → environment default) as `<model> · <provider>` — model name first so the running model stays identifiable when width is tight, the trailing provider being what truncates (#1845); the trigger is also sized to `--eb-text-xs` and widened to match the adjacent session toolbar buttons, #1704/#1845). It renders the shared `components/ModelSelectorPopover.jsx` (same popover as the global assist selector): one provider-grouped popover with a System/Team scope badge, "default" badge, checkmark, per-group auth/endpoint detail rows, plain model labels (**no** family/kind suffix), an override indicator, reset-to-environment-default, disabled/read-only reasons, and a **confirmation** before a provider switch (which restarts the agent container) — the old separate provider chip + native model `<select>` are retired; pure decisions in `chat-model-selector.js` (`buildChatGroups`/`chatSelection`/`resolveChatSelectAction`/`chatResetAction`/`chatTrigger`/`effectiveModelLabel`/…, unit-tested, `admin-console-src` CI job); each selector row also shows the model's **context window** (e.g. "1M context", resolved via `resolveContextWindowTokens`; omitted when unknown, #1718). **Header info popover (#1718)** — the always-visible header keeps only a **compact context-usage indicator** (`used / real window · %`, or the used count alone when the window is unknown — `headerContextIndicator`) plus the model selector; a small **(i)** icon (now labelled **"About this chat"**, `SessionInfoPopover.jsx`, portaled + viewport-clamped like the model popover, #1807) opens the full detail in sections — **About** (Working Environment name + each repo:branch chip with a dirty-state dot and uncommitted-change count tooltip, #1807), **Session** (copyable session id), **Usage** (single total price + four-class token breakdown Input / Output / Cache-write / Cache-read, fed by `SessionLogPanel`'s `onCostUpdate` breakdown), **Model** (selected context window + max-output tokens when discovery captured it); every value is omitted rather than guessed or zero-padded. Pure formatting/derivation in `agent-chat/session-info.js` (`formatContextWindow`/`selectorWindowLabel`/`headerContextIndicator`/`sessionPriceLabel`/`tokenBreakdownRows`/`repoBranchRows`, unit-tested, `admin-console-src` CI job). `RightRail` — tabbed, toggle `Cmd/Ctrl+Shift+L`; its Sessions-tab badge, the `SessionsPanel` list and the `DiffView` running-probe all subscribe to **one** shared per-agent poll of `/api/agents/:id/sessions/active` (`SessionsActiveContext` + pure `sessions-active.js` — `sessionsFromResponse`/`activeSessionCount`/`isAgentRunning`/`removeSession`/`hasActiveTriggerSessionForRoom`, unit-tested, `admin-console-src` CI job; #1678) mounted in `AgentChat.jsx` around **both** `ChatPanel` and `RightRail` (widened from RightRail-only in #1852), instead of each running its own timer — `ChatPanel` reuses this same poll via `hasActiveTriggerSessionForRoom(sessions, roomId)` to keep the animated "working…" thinking indicator lit during **trigger-initiated** runs (cron/once/http/github-event/loop), whose last transcript entry is the agent's own trigger notice so the "last message is human" heuristic never fires (#1852); `SessionLogPanel` — structured timeline (its event timeline is windowed by the same `VirtualList`/`virtual-window.js`, #1685); its live **TODO surfaces** (inline rich checklist + collapsed `<done>/<total> · <step>` summary in `ToolBlock`, plus the bottom-docked `PinnedTodoPanel`) are folded from the agent's incremental task-tracking tools (`TaskCreate`/`TaskUpdate`, with `TaskList`/`TaskGet` reads and legacy `TodoWrite` full-replace) by the pure `task-todo.js` (`isTaskTool`/`applyTaskOp`/`annotateTaskRows`/`formatTodoSummary`, unit-tested, `admin-console-src` CI job; #1662, supersedes the #255 `TodoWrite`-only handling); a `TaskUpdate` landing on an id whose `TaskCreate` fell outside the current fold window (per-turn scoping, or the `?limit=500` backfill truncation) gets a stub row instead of being dropped, marked `contentUnknown` so `snapshotTasks` renders `(task created earlier in this session)` in place of a blank line until a later update supplies a real `subject` (#2203 — a status-only update, the realistic shape, never fills it in on its own; widening the fold window itself is a larger structural change the ticket left undone); an `Edit`/`Write` tool call renders a line-level red/green diff instead of the raw input JSON (#2189) — the same `--eb-status-success-bg`/`--eb-status-error-bg` row language `DiffView.jsx` uses, plus an old/new line-number gutter neither component had before, computed by the pure `edit-diff.js` (`computeLineDiff`, a Myers shortest-edit-script over `old_string`/`new_string` with common prefix/suffix trimmed off first;
