`Write` diffs empty content against the new file, i.e. all-additions) and never truncated — the existing `ToolBlock` collapse/expand affordance is what keeps a long one out of view by default (`MultiEdit` is out of scope: it carries an array of edits, not one before/after pair); a `system_event` of subtype `task_updated`/`task_notification` (#2191 — background sub-agent task tracking) similarly gets a purpose-built render ahead of the generic scalar-only `system_event` handling every other subtype still falls through to: the collapsed row surfaces the task id plus its status (`task_updated`) or a report snippet (`task_notification`), and expanding it renders the `patch` object's fields as key/value rows, the `task_notification` markdown `summary` via the same `SafeMarkdown` component chat messages use (instead of an escaped JSON string), and shortens `output_file` to a workspace-relative reference — computed by the pure `system-event.js` (`isTaskEvent`/`taskEventSummary`/`taskUpdatedPatchRows`/`taskNotificationSummary`/`formatOutputFileRef`); every other tool call's generic Input/Result fallback (#2197 — `Bash`, `Read`, `Grep`, `Glob`, `Write`, and any future tool with no bespoke branch) renders the same way instead of a `JSON.stringify` dump: unquoted `key:` rows one field per line, nested objects/arrays indented recursively, a multi-line string as real line breaks, and a markdown-like string leaf in a **Result** (e.g. a `Bash` result's stdout) through the same `SafeMarkdown` component — an **Input** leaf is always rendered `multiline`/preformatted instead, even one that happens to match the same heuristic (a shell `#` comment, a `**glob**`-shaped token), since it is the literal text the agent supplied, not prose to reinterpret (`renderableRows(value, { isInput: true })`, #2198 review finding) — computed by the pure `tool-value-view.js` (`renderableRows`); a `rate_limit_event` stream-json line from the Claude CLI (#2202) similarly gets a purpose-built render instead of falling into that generic `unknown` bucket: `agent-entrypoint.py`'s `_map_stream_json_line` gives it its own `type`, carrying the parsed `rate_limit_info` as `info` and the whole parsed line as `raw`; `LogEvent.jsx` renders a plain-language collapsed summary (status/overage/reset time) and, on expand, every `info` field as its own labelled row plus the full raw payload through `ToolBlock.jsx`'s now-exported `ValueRows` — computed by the pure `rate-limit-event.js` (`rateLimitEventSummary`/`formatEpochSeconds`/`rateLimitEventDetailRows`); `isUsingOverage` and `overageInUse` are surfaced verbatim side by side rather than collapsed into one inferred relationship, since a sample has been observed where they disagree and which field is authoritative is still unconfirmed; `WorkspaceBrowser`) + `hooks/{useSSE,useColorScheme}.js`.
**Agents page — REMOVED (#1930, epic #1925 Phase 3 of 3 and the final phase of epic #1961).** `admin-console/src/pages/Agents.jsx` (2 099 lines) and its pure `agents-view.js` are deleted, together with the sidebar entry that led the **Agent Setup** group (now Flows · Agent Templates · Skills · Knowledge) and the mirrored `breadcrumbs.js` / `route-title.js` pair. `/agents` — and the older `/blueprints` alias that used to redirect *to* it — are now `<Navigate to="/chats" replace />` in `App.jsx`, so no bookmark dead-ends; `help-routes.js` and its client mirror `page-help-view.js` carry both as `REDIRECTS` + `ALLOWLIST` entries so an old link keeps its help. Nothing was re-homed by this ticket: every capability had already moved (rail quick actions #1928 · **Settings** tab #1929 · **New Agent**/**Import Agent** in the page-action header #1956 · the *Not materialized yet* stub section #1957 · the **Add member** dialog #1958) or was deliberately retired (the missing-role suggestion #1959, the *Orphaned agents* block #1960 — the cause was fixed instead — and the Orchestrator/BriefingPack half with the model itself, #1961). Help side: `help-topics/agents.md` is gone, folded into `help-topics/agent-chat.md` as the `## The agent roles` and `## Where the Agents page went` chapters — the latter is the "where did this go?" table for every affordance above.

**Convert a single agent into a team of one, in place (#2052).** The launcher and the one-assistant fast lane have said since #1667 that *a single agent is simply a team of one*; nothing implemented it, and the `create` help topic carried a whole sub-chapter explaining the dead end (growing built a **new** team and abandoned the assistant you had been chatting with). **Convert into a team…** — a standalone-only entry in the Agent Chat row menu (`buildAgentMenu`, offered for `role: 'agent'`/legacy `expert` only; a `team`, a `team-member` and a stub are refused) — creates a `role: 'team'` record beside the agent and makes the agent its only member. Nothing is copied and no identity is re-minted: the agent keeps its **id**, its Matrix user, its DM room and history, its triggers and its Working Environment, which is what makes the whole thing cheap — a team record is pure metadata (no token, no workspace, no container), and a one-member team was already a supported state (`POST /api/teams` sets no minimum of two, the loop verifier degrades to `team-no-sibling`, and both zero-member reconcilers test `=== 0`). **Every decision is the pure `orchestrator/team-conversion.js`** (`assessConversionEligibility` — one closed set of codes shared by the menu predicate, the API refusal and the orchestrator's own re-check; `deriveTeamId`/`deriveTeamDisplayName` — `<agent-id>-team` / `<Display Name> Team`, derived rather than asked for, refused with a specific message when taken; `planTeamConversion` — the team record, the member patch, the fields to unset, and the four things that re-key). `Orchestrator.convertAgentToTeam()` is the fs/broker/respawn half behind `POST /internal/agents/:id/convert-to-team`, proxied by `POST /api/agents/:id/convert-to-team` (thin on purpose — the console gates on **both** `canManageAgent(agent)` and `canManageAgent({teamId})`, since the action edits the agent *and* creates a team in its tenant, and re-implements no part of the rule). **The four re-keyings are the whole difficulty**, all keyed on `agentKnowledgeTeamId` flipping from the human tenant to the new squad: (1) the **prompt** — the team's `systemPromptTemplate` is left EMPTY and the agent's prompt stays its own in the platform's legacy *exclusive* shape (own `systemPrompt`, **no** `promptMode` key, which is what `PUT /api/agents/:id` persists for exclusive and what `updateTeamPrompt` skips), read out of `AGENT-PROMPT.md` when the config carries none — exactly what `importAgent` compensates for — so the resolved prompt is byte-identical and the user is not handed an inheritance relationship they never asked for; (2) **skills** — the *grant* is names but the *content* resolves per tier, so a skill served by `teams/<tenant>/skills` would keep a grant with nothing behind it: the plan names those (global- and agent-pool skills resolve identically) and the copy into `teams/<newTeamId>/skills` is the only content duplication in the feature; (3) **knowledge** — the tenant's per-team knowledge config (#570) is carried onto the new team, which keeps the KB repo ref and branch identical, so the agent's existing `knowledge/` checkout is still the right one (a tenant with no overrides has nothing to carry); (4) **plugin scopes** — a `team`-granularity scope id is the agent's own id while standalone and the team's afterwards, so `teardownAgentPluginScopes` runs BEFORE the flip, while the old scope's token is still mintable. Also written explicitly: `roleId` (Token Usage recovers it by stripping a `<teamId>-` prefix the converted id does not have, so every usage row would otherwise read the literal `team-member`) and the grant in **member shape** (`skillsOverride`/`pluginsOverride` on the member, the same list on the team so a second member inherits it). The two config writes are ordered so a failure after the first undoes it (AC9 — no partial state), then `saveAgentPrompt` rebuilds `AGENT-PROMPT.md` + `CLAUDE.md` for the new scope, `reseedAgent` mirrors them into an isolated volume and `respawnAgent` brings the agent back on its own. **A mid-run conversion is refused, not decided for the user**: `agentBusyState` gates it and the route answers `409 { warning, busy, activeTriggers }`, the same two-phase contract `disableAgent` uses — the console re-sends with `force` only after the user answers *Convert anyway*. UI: the pure `agent-lifecycle/convert-to-team-view.js` owns the derived preview and every line of the confirmation (with `convert-to-team-view.test.js` reading `orchestrator/team-conversion.js` so the id the dialog promises and the id the orchestrator mints cannot diverge), `ConvertToTeamDialog` renders it, `RailLifecycleHost` routes it, and `rail-lifecycle-wiring.test.js` guards the wiring. **Two deliberate non-goals:** there is **no reverse conversion** (deleting the team cascades to its member, which is the existing behaviour), and the QuickStart repository fast lane is **not** extended to find a converted assistant — `findStandaloneAgentForEnv` still accepts `role: 'agent'` only, so submitting the same repository again creates a second standalone assistant rather than silently adding to a team the user built on purpose; the `create` topic says so. **AC12 read against the tree, not the ticket:** the *Grow into a full team →* link the ticket asks to replace was already **removed by #2045**, and `onboarding-help-copy.test.js` forbids putting a grow/convert affordance back on `/quickstart` — so the correction is the help topics, where chapter 2's `### Growing creates a new team, it does not convert this one` becomes `### Two ways to grow: convert this assistant, or build a new team` and `agent-chat.md`'s row-menu chapter documents the action where it lives.

**DM rooms.** First open lazily POSTs `/api/matrix/agents/:id/dm`; server creates/reuses a 1:1 room, invites the admin, persists `dm-room.json`; subsequent loads short-circuit on that file. **Session log rendering**: SSE `/api/agents/:id/session/:roomId/stream` (`since=<byte-offset>` cursor); `correlationId` pairs log rows with bubbles (hover dims non-matching — CSS-driven via the pure `hover-highlight.js` `hoverBaseCss`/`hoverDimCss` (unit-tested, `admin-console-src` CI job) keyed off each row's `data-cid` + the scroll container's `data-hover-scope`, so a hover mutates one shared `<style>` node instead of threading a `dimmed` prop through every row and re-rendering the transcript / re-running Shiki; #1676); adjacent `tool_use`/`tool_result` with the same `toolId` fold into one block.

**Workspace browser** (read-only over `/workspace/agents/<agentId>/`): `GET .../workspace/{tree,file,download,image}?path=<rel>` — all reject paths outside the agent root. Reads capped at 512 KB; binary files (NUL in first 8 KB) flagged; raster images (PNG/JPEG/GIF/WebP by magic bytes, SVG excluded) rendered inline via `/image` (no cap). **Reference a workspace file in the composer (#1699):** a hover-revealed per-file `@` (Reference in chat) button splices `@<workspace-relative-path>` into the selected agent's draft, reusing `MessageInput.insertRefs` (the same caret-aware `@<ref>` path an upload/attach produces, so the agent resolves it from its cwd without a re-upload). Files only (never directories) and only for real workspace keys — a `/context` host-mount key (leading slash, not agent-resolvable) is hidden. Cross-panel wiring: `WorkspaceBrowser` (RightRail) → `AgentChat` lifts an `{ref, ts}` request-object → `ChatPanel`/`MessageInput` (siblings; same request-object pattern as `railTabRequest`/`resumeRequest`); the consumer effect seeds `lastInsertRefTs` from the incoming `ts` so a request left over on an agent switch never re-fires on the freshly-mounted composer.

**Inline image thumbnails for human `@`-references (#2146).** A HUMAN message's `@<path>` reference to a raster image (an upload token, or a path typed/spliced in via the #1699 Reference-in-chat button) renders in the transcript as a small clickable thumbnail in place of the raw token; clicking it opens the *same* `FileViewerModal` fullscreen preview the Workspace tab's file tree already uses (no second viewer). The thumbnail fetches through the *same* `/api/agents/:id/workspace/image` endpoint and magic-byte gate (`admin-console/api/image-type.js`) as the Workspace preview — no new image-serving path. Detection (`chat-image-refs.js`, pure/unit-tested) is a client-side extension filter only (`png`/`jpg`/`jpeg`/`gif`/`webp`); a `/context`-mount path (leading slash) is never treated as an image reference, and a load failure (deleted/unreadable file, or a mislabeled extension the server rejects) degrades to the plain `@<path>` text rather than a broken-image icon. **Agent-authored messages are never parsed and trigger no fetch** — the security boundary (AC4): only `Message.jsx`'s human branch calls the parser. A message with no image reference takes the exact same render path as before (byte-identical, no parsing overhead beyond one regex scan).

**Context-folder browsing (#1286, multiple folders #1290).** An agent with host-folder mount(s) (#1283) grows one **virtual read-only root per folder** in the same tree, keyed by its in-container target — `/context` for a single legacy folder, `/context/<slug>` for each folder in a multi-folder set. The leading slash, which a real relative workspace path can never have, means a workspace folder literally named `context` cannot collide. It is not served from this container: the host folder exists only inside the *agent's* container, so `GET /api/agents/:id/context/{status,tree,file,image,download}` proxies `/internal/agents/:id/context/{tree,file,download}`, which runs a one-shot `list`/capped-`read` via `orchestrator/container-exec.js` (libpod exec create → start → docker-frame demux) inside that container. Pure rules — request-side path guard, the browse program, output parsing, the error-code → HTTP table — live in `orchestrator/context-browse.js`; containment is enforced **twice**: no `..`/absolute path in, and realpath containment under `/context` in the container (an escaping symlink is listed as `other` and never readable). Requires the agent to be running (`409` otherwise). Frontend source routing is the pure `workspace-source.js` (`isContextKey`/`workspaceUrl`/`withContextRoot`).

**Context-folder writing (read-write mounts).** A **read-write** mount (`readWrite: true`) also gets upload / delete / drag-move in the same tree; a **read-only** mount stays browse-only (its rows offer no write action). Symmetric to browsing: the mutation runs inside the agent's container and the console is a proxy — `DELETE /api/agents/:id/context/file`, `POST .../context/move`, `POST .../context/upload` proxy `/internal/agents/:id/context/{file,move,upload}`. Delete/move run a one-shot Python `WRITE_PROGRAM` (realpath containment under `/context`, sibling of the browse program) via `orchestrator/context-write.js`; upload streams the raw bytes (no base64) into the container through the libpod **archive** endpoint (`putArchiveToContainer` in `container-exec.js`) after a `prepare-upload` exec validates the target dir + de-duplicates the name, packaged by the dependency-free ustar builder `orchestrator/tar-single.js`. Guarded **three** ways: per-mount `readWrite` gate in the orchestrator route (403 on a read-only mount), realpath containment in-container, and the **kernel EROFS backstop** (a read-only bind mount rejects every write regardless). A move is a rename within **one** mount — cross-folder and workspace↔context drags are refused (`sameWritableSource`); the mount **root** itself is never a drag source or deletable (you delete files *in* it, not the mount point). Frontend gating is mount-aware in `workspace-source.js` (`isReadOnlyKey(key, status)`, `mountForKey`, `writeApiBase`/`apiRelOf`), threaded through `WorkspaceBrowser.jsx` from the `/context/status` payload.

**Markdown safety.** Sanitized with `rehype-sanitize` (GFM `defaultSchema` + `style`/`data-*` for `rehype-pretty-code`), run *after* pretty-code so highlighting survives. Both Shiki themes shipped; active theme via CSS keyed off `<html data-theme>`.

**Meeting Note-Taker — persistent transcription overlay (#1296).** Quick voice dictation (the `MessageInput` mic tap → `useVoiceRecorder` → draft insertion) is page-scoped and unchanged. **Meeting mode** is a separate, explicitly-chosen action: the mic becomes a split control (chevron → "Record meeting for &lt;agent&gt;") that starts an **app-level** transcription session which survives all client-side navigation — the mic is never released when the composer unmounts. The engine is `TranscriptionSessionProvider` (`src/context/TranscriptionSessionContext.jsx`, mounted in `src/index.jsx` inside `AuthProvider`): it owns the `MediaStream`, records in fixed **120 s segments** (each a standalone webm/opus blob transcribed via the existing single-shot `POST /api/transcribe`, up to 2 retries → a `[transcription gap]` marker on failure, never aborting the session), and on **Stop** assembles the ordered segment texts into one message auto-sent to the originating agent's DM room (`POST /api/matrix/agents/:id/dm` → `.../rooms/:roomId/messages`). A `TranscriptionOverlay` (portaled to `document.body`, rendered once in `Shell.jsx`) shows the live card (recording/paused/transcribing, elapsed, agent name → chat, Pause/Resume · Stop · Cancel) on every page. Recovery: after each transcribed segment the segment *texts* (never audio) are persisted to `localStorage['transcription.recovery']`; a `beforeunload` guard + an on-mount recovery banner (Deliver partial / Discard) cover a hard reload/crash. One active session per tab. The deterministic core (state machine, segment assembly, transcript/message formatting, recovery-record shape) is the pure, `node --test`-covered `src/context/transcription-session.js` (per the #651 pure-module convention); the provider is the thin browser adapter. Backend: the only server change is `admin-console/api/transcribe.js`'s STT timeout, now env-configurable via `STT_TIMEOUT_MS` (default **60 s**, was a hard-coded 15 s — a 120 s segment can exceed it on CPU).

**Helper Chat — app-level Help panel (#1735).** A **Help** button (`HelpCircle`) in the Shell header right-cluster toggles a non-blocking slide-in assistant available on **every** route. The engine is `HelperChatProvider` (`src/context/HelperChatContext.jsx`, mounted in `src/index.jsx` alongside `TranscriptionSessionProvider`) so the panel **and its conversation survive all client-side navigation**; consumers reach it through the `useHelperChat()` hook. `HelperChatPanel` (`src/components/HelperChatPanel.jsx`) is rendered once in `Shell.jsx` next to `TranscriptionOverlay` — a right-edge `position:fixed` panel with **no backdrop**, off-screen (`translateX(100%)` + `pointer-events:none`) when closed. On submit it POSTs the newest **≤20** turns plus the current route + friendly route label to `POST /api/helper/chat` (the #1734 endpoint) and streams the `{type:'delta'}`→`{type:'done'}` NDJSON into a streaming assistant bubble (the shared animated `ThinkingDots` indicator while streaming, matching Agent Chat — #1779). The deterministic core — message-list transitions, request shaping (trim to `MAX_TURNS`=20), NDJSON line framing (`splitNdjson`) and the static `SUGGESTED_PROMPTS` empty-state chips — is the pure, `node --test`-covered `src/context/helper-chat-model.js` (per the `transcription-session.js`/#651 convention); the provider is the thin browser adapter owning the `fetch` + stream reader. Route grounding uses the `BREADCRUMBS` map + `breadcrumbFor`, extracted from `Shell.jsx` into shared `src/components/breadcrumbs.js` (single source of truth for both the breadcrumb bar and the panel's route label). Markdown reuses Agent Chat's `SafeMarkdown` + `ensureAgentChatMdCss` (now exported from `pages/agent-chat/Message.jsx`) for the same sanitised-markdown/Shiki pipeline in both themes; the animated loading indicator reuses the exported `ThinkingDots` (its `@keyframes dotPulse` self-injected via `ensureThinkingDotsCss`, with a `prefers-reduced-motion` fallback) and the composer reuses the shared `useVoiceRecorder` hook for a quick-dictation mic (record → `POST /api/transcribe` → appended to the draft), so both the loading indicator and the mic reach visual/functional parity with Agent Chat without a parallel implementation (#1779). A tab bar (#1737, extended to **[This page] [Ask] [Tour]** in #1873 — see below) switches the panel between the **Ask** chat face and a guided **tour** face (`HelperChatTour`, `src/components/HelperChatTour.jsx`): a chapter list + per-chapter step carousel whose data comes from `GET /api/helper/tour-manifest` (served via a guarded loader that degrades to `{chapters:[]}` until the #1736 manifest data lands) and whose progress persists under the `helper-chat.tour-progress` localStorage key; `useHelperChat().openTour()` opens the panel straight into tour mode — used by the Overview face's **Take the Tour** CTA (inherited from the retired `/getting-started` page, #1874) and a CTA in the chat empty state. The tour view's deterministic core (chapter ordering by `order`, the progress shape, resume/step math) is the pure, `node --test`-covered `src/context/tour-view.js`.

**Flow Copilot — app-level in-flight turn (#2087).** A Flow Copilot turn takes 20–60 s (up to three LLM calls through the repair loop). Its state used to live entirely in `FlowCopilotPanel`, so three ordinary actions destroyed it **silently** (the `fetch`/reader kept running and set state on a dead component): navigating away from `/flows`, closing the Copilot panel (the mount is conditional), and switching flow (`Briefings.jsx`'s `key={proc}` remounts the subtree). Ownership moves to `FlowCopilotProvider` (`src/context/FlowCopilotContext.jsx`, mounted in `src/index.jsx` **above the router** — the same reason `TranscriptionSessionProvider` (#1296) / `HelperChatProvider` (#1735) are, and per **D1** deliberately *not* navigation-blocking); consumers reach it via `useFlowCopilot()` and the panel is now a thin renderer over it (`viewFor(scope)`). What lives in the provider is the **turn**, not the chat: the `fetch`, the NDJSON reader, the live phase, the preview-graph call, and a finished turn's proposal kept *unapplied* until Apply/Discard (**D4**) — at most one turn per browser tab (**AC4**, a single slot; a second start is refused with a stated reason via `busyElsewhereRefusal`/`decideCopilotAction`), each bound to its scope (`isBindableScope`/`pendingForScope`, with the mid-turn draft-rename `rekeyTurn`/`rekeyPending` following the binding). The durable chat history does **not** move — it stays the draft's own `copilot.messages` written through the #1071 `onApplyBundle`/`onPersistHistory` path (no second persistence mechanism, **AC7**). A running turn now surfaces on **every** route through `FlowCopilotIndicator` (`src/components/FlowCopilotIndicator.jsx`, portaled to `document.body`, rendered once in `Shell.jsx` just below `TranscriptionOverlay`): it names the flow, offers a jump back and an explicit **Stop**, and is hidden only while the Copilot panel is open on the originating flow (**AC13**, the `indicatorState` rule). **Stop** aborts the client `fetch`, which closes the socket, which is the `res.on('close')` the route already watched — so one client action ends both the delivery *and* the LLM loop, and a tab close takes the identical path (**D2/AC14**). Server-side, `httpPostJson` (`admin-console/api/api.js`) gained an optional 5th `signal` param (every existing four-arg caller stays byte-identical) and `runCopilot` (`admin-console/api/flow-copilot.js`) now takes a `signal`, checks it at the top of every repair iteration, and throws `COPILOT_ABORTED` (`isCopilotAborted`) even when a best-effort bundle exists — so a stopped turn leaves **no** proposal and no partial draft (**AC9/AC11**); `X-Assist-Model` rides on the provider's fetch (**AC16**). The deterministic core (scope binding, `pendingForScope`/rekey helpers, `indicatorState` + phase labels, `busyElsewhereRefusal`/`decideCopilotAction`) is the pure, `node --test`-covered `src/pages/process/flow-copilot-turn.js` (per the #651 convention); the provider is the thin browser adapter.

**Actionable timeout card + generate a large flow in parts (#2131).** Two halves plus a confirmed deadline. (1) **The failure card reads as guidance, not a transport dump.** The route classifies a failed turn with `classifyTurnFailure` (`admin-console/api/flow-copilot.js`) and emits a stable **`code`** (`llm-timeout`/`no-parseable-bundle`/`llm-failed`) on the NDJSON error event; `copilotErrorInfo` (`flow-copilot-view.js`) maps a timeout-class failure to a plain-language headline (`COPILOT_TIMEOUT_HEADLINE`) plus two actionable hints (`COPILOT_TIMEOUT_HINTS` — *describe the flow in smaller parts* / *switch to a faster assist model under Settings → Model*), and the raw transport string moves into a `<details>` *Technical detail* expander (`ErrorRow` in `FlowCopilotPanel.jsx`). Deliberately **not** a blanket `status === 502`: the route's own envelope uses 502 for `no-parseable-bundle`/`llm-failed` too, so junk JSON must not be told to pick a faster model — an upstream 502/504 arrives inside the detail string and is caught by `TIMEOUT_MESSAGE_RE` instead. (2) **A large flow is generated in bounded parts.** After a timeout-class failure of the FIRST whole-bundle call, `runCopilot` retries as two smaller calls via `generateBundleInParts` — part 1 (`roles`) runs against the role-less base bundle and gets the whole-bundle contract, part 2 (`wiring`) runs against part 1's result and gets the **ops** contract (`patchRole` per role + labels/gates/humans), each capped at `MAX_TOKENS_PART` (10000). It **reuses the two contracts `buildCopilotSystem` already has** (no new prompt vocabulary), the merged bundle goes through the identical sanitize → STRICT validate → lint → repair gate, and repairs after a split re-point at the merged bundle (a small op list, not a second full-size regeneration). It is a **fallback, not a size heuristic** (`canGenerateInParts` + `isTimeoutFailure`, the single predicate both halves key on): only on a fresh whole-bundle generation, only once, only after a timeout — so `AC4` (a single call that succeeds never splits) is byte-identical rather than argued. A failed split re-throws the ORIGINAL timeout with the fallback's reason appended, so *smaller parts / faster model* stays the right guidance. The panel shows *Generating in parts (X/N)…* via `phaseLabel`. (3) **The per-call deadline was the whole remaining turn budget**, which would starve the split retry; it is now capped at `COPILOT_CALL_DEADLINE_MS` (`AEE_COPILOT_CALL_DEADLINE_MS`, default half `COPILOT_STREAM_DEADLINE_MS`). Wiring guarded by `copilot-timeout-wiring.test.js`.

**Context-help foundation — page-aware "This page" help (#1872, epic #1870, concept `docs/context-help-system-concept.md`).** The unified help system's plumbing: per-page help is authored as small markdown **topics** under `admin-console/api/helper-chat/help-topics/<id>.md` (front-matter `id, routes[], title, summary, seeAlso[], askPrompts[], actions[]` + body). These files are the **single source** read by two consumers with no vendoring/parity test (unlike the docs/** corpus copies): the new `GET /api/helper/page-help` endpoint (returns the whole small topic set, cached, behind the `/api` gate — the client resolves route→topic locally) **and** the chat grounding (`corpus.js` folds the concatenated topic bodies into the system prompt at the runtime marker, and `handler.js` threads the request's `currentRoute` so the model is pointed at the current page's topic). **The chat fold is route-scoped (#2006):** the request's context resolves through the *same* `resolveHelpContext` the panel uses and only that topic plus its `seeAlso` neighbours are folded in (no-route fallback: the Overview topic plus its neighbours — deliberately not every topic), with the narrowing stated in the prompt so the assistant points at the other page instead of inventing an answer. That is what stopped `SYSTEM_PROMPT_CHAR_LIMIT` being raised a fourth time: the number now means "docs corpus + the biggest single page neighbourhood" (worst context: `/quickstart` at 315 448 of 340 000 — 92.8% used, 24 552 left (#2170 SHRANK the crown — retiring the `/environments` help topic and folding only the still-live half into `agent-chat.md` outweighed the small new `environment-templates.md` topic, which isn't on this crown's `seeAlso` chain at all — down from 324 900; see `corpus.js`'s comment for the breakdown) — the crown moved BACK off `/chats` (which had held it at 314 511) when #2085 dissolved the Access page: `access-users.md` was folded into `users.md` and the `/teams` `seeAlso` link `agent-chat.md` carried was dropped rather than re-pointed, since the successor surface is the Share dialog on that very page and Administration is System-Admin-only — so `/chats` lost a whole neighbour topic; #1930 moved the crown here by removing the Agents page, so `/flows` lost its `/agents` `seeAlso` neighbour and fell to 258 761 while `/quickstart`, whose `create.md` links to the `/chats` topic that absorbed the deleted `agents.md`, became the biggest neighbourhood — it had been the no-route Overview fallback at 262 568 since #1996) and `corpus.test.js` asserts it via `measureWorstCaseSystemPrompt()` over every context a topic claims. A new page's topic no longer moves that worst case at all; expanding an existing topic still enlarges its own page's neighbourhood and those of the pages linking to it (asserted in `help-registry.test.js`). The docs `CORPUS_FILES` half is unchanged and still counts in full on every request. Pure server core `admin-console/api/helper-chat/help-registry.js` (`parseTopic` front-matter parser, `buildRegistry`/`topicForRoute` dynamic/alias/redirect-aware resolution mirroring `breadcrumbs.js`, `foldTopics`, and the `baselineTopic` §3.4 template E16 authors against) + pure client core `admin-console/src/context/page-help-view.js` (route→topic resolution, Overview fallback decision, `askPrompt`→Ask hand-off shaping). **Route-coverage guarantee (§3.4):** `admin-console/api/helper-chat/help-routes.js` is the route topology (`PAGE_ROUTES` show a topic or the Overview fallback; `ALLOWLIST` = structural redirects/aliases/dynamic-children/stubs), and `help-coverage.test.js` fails CI when an `App.jsx` route is unclassified (or a listed route is stale) — so a new page cannot ship help-less. The pilot **Overview** topic (`help-topics/overview.md`, `routes: []` → the no-route fallback, G1's target) proves the path end-to-end. **The panel "This page" face (C2, #1873)** adds a `mode: 'page'` third face to `HelperChatPanel.jsx` and the `[This page][Ask][Tour]` tab bar (the old `Chat` tab is **renamed to `Ask`**; the internal mode id stays `'chat'`): the header Help button and every page's shared **`PageHelpButton`** ("?" affordance, `src/components/PageHelpButton.jsx`) open the panel on `'page'` via the new `useHelperChat().openHelpFor()`; the provider fetches `GET /api/helper/page-help` once and resolves the live route → topic locally (`pageHelpState`), rendering the topic's title/summary/sanitised-markdown body, its `seeAlso` "Related" links (router-navigate) and its `askPrompts` chips (which hand off to Ask pre-seeded via `askHandoff`), with an explicit **"← Overview"** Home affordance and the Overview fallback when a route has no topic. **The panel never opens on its own (#2069).** C2 shipped a **first-run auto-open** nudge — once per topic-bearing route per user, tracked in `localStorage help.seenRoutes.<username>` — which was removed once Phase 3/4 had given nearly every route its own topic and "once per route" had become an unrequested panel on almost every page. The panel now opens only from an explicit user action (header **Help**, a `PageHelpButton`, the Agent Chat row menu's Help entry, `openTour()`); there is no preference, no storage state that can re-arm it, and pre-existing `help.seenRoutes.*` keys are left inert. Guarded against reintroduction by `src/pages/non-route-help-wiring.test.js` (no `setIsOpen(true)` reachable from a route-keyed effect, no `seenRoutes` key in the source). The durable lesson, recorded in concept §9 decision 3: a "once per surface" nudge is bounded by the eventual number of surfaces, not by its frequency per surface. The panel/PageHelpButton JSX stays uncovered per #651. **Getting Started retired (G1, #1874):** `GettingStarted.jsx`, its sidebar entry (the whole **Help** nav group) and its `route-title.js` entry are gone; its six concept cards + "where to go next" row are now the Overview topic's body, `seeAlso` links and the new front-matter **`actions[]`** CTA list — `{ label, to }` navigations plus `{ label, action: "tour" }`, which the face wires to `openTour()`, with one optional `primary: true` accent (parsed by `help-registry.js`, normalized client-side by `topicActions()` in `page-help-view.js`, rendered as the CTA row of the "This page" face). The `/getting-started` **path** stays in `App.jsx` as a `GettingStartedRedirect` (opens the Help panel, then `<Navigate to="/chats">`) and moves from `PAGE_ROUTES` to `ALLOWLIST` + `REDIRECTS` in `help-routes.js`/`page-help-view.js`, so an old bookmark lands on the app home with the Overview open instead of a blank route.

**Context-help Phase-3 extraction — per-page topics (E1 #1875 onwards).** Each extraction ticket moves one page's large explanatory box into its own topic and leaves the page with **title + one-line description + `PageHelpButton`**. First one shipped: **`help-topics/agents.md`** (`routes: ["/agents"]`) — it absorbed the Agents page's "what is an agent" box, the full role catalogue (the `ROLE_INFO` `body` copy, incl. the *Agent Team is not a Team access scope* warning), the lifecycle states and the Disable / Reset-to-stub / Delete distinction; `ROLE_INFO` now keeps only the one-line card `short` + `when`, and `RoleDetailBanner` is a one-liner plus a `PageHelpButton`. Because the page that used to explain a concept is gone, each authored topic is guarded by **`help-topics-content.test.js`** — a content guard over the *shipped* topic files (baseline shape, declared routes really resolve, every `seeAlso` target is a route `help-routes.js` knows, and a list of concept fragments that must survive a reword); later extraction tickets add one entry per topic to its `TOPIC_CONTENT` table. **The pilot topic was itself retired by #1930:** the page it was extracted from is gone, so `help-topics/agents.md` was deleted and its material folded into `help-topics/agent-chat.md` — the role catalogue as a `## The agent roles` chapter, the lifecycle/Disable/Reset-to-stub/Delete material as `## Where the Agents page went` — and `/agents` (plus the `/blueprints` alias) became `REDIRECTS` to `/chats`, so an old link resolves to the Agent Chat topic instead of losing its help. `ROLE_INFO` and `RoleDetailBanner` did **not** go with the page: #1929 had already moved them into `agent-lifecycle/agent-form-fields.jsx`, which both the create form (`/create`) and the edit form (the chat **Settings** tab) render, so the one-liner + `PageHelpButton` shape E1 gave them survives — its "?" carries no `section` and therefore resolves against whichever of those two routes is live. Per-field inline help at the point of decision (Skills / System Prompt / Plugins / Host context folders, the delete-dialog knowledge note) deliberately **stays on the page** — the epic removes the big boxes, not the live field hints — and is summarised in the topic as well.

**Context-help E2 — the Flows topic (#1876).** `help-topics/flows.md` (`routes: ["/flows"]`) is the single home for the flow-wiring concepts: the *Outcome → Label → Consumer* hand-off vocabulary, Drafts vs live Teams and Materialize, the eight hand-off kinds of the edge chooser, human **gates** (👤 → flow) vs **brakes** (flow → 👤), named human roles, per-agent Outcomes (`exactly one` / `any of`) and Inputs, Stages, Anomalies and the niche BriefingPack mode. `Briefings.jsx` lost BOTH of its own help surfaces — the dismissible "Welcome to Flows" `InfoBanner` (with its `aee.flows.infoBanner.dismissed` localStorage key) and the page-local `HelpPanel` slide-in drawer — and its left-list header "?" is now the shared `PageHelpButton`. The concept side panels keep a one-line blurb plus a `PageHelpButton` instead of their long prose (`LabelRegistryPanel` gate/brake, `HumanRegistryPanel` how-humans-act, the `EdgeDialogs` hand-off chooser); the per-tile one-liners, the dynamic Inputs/Outcomes hints and `process-toolbar-hints.js` disabled-reasons stay put as live in-context hints. **Widened to a per-surface reference (#1984, epic #1981).** E2 left the console's busiest page — a canvas, six side panels, a docked Copilot rail and a family of edge dialogs — behind one page-level "?" on the flows-list header, so a reader inside the label registry got the whole topic from the top. The topic is now ten clearly-headed chapters — **What Flows is · The flows left list · The canvas · Wiring hand-offs: the edge chooser · Human gates, brakes and named human roles · Outcomes and inputs, per agent · Stages · Anomalies · Improvements · Describe the flow: the Copilot** — and ten "?" affordances deep-link into them: the flows-list header (chapter 1), the canvas toolbar (3, the surface that had no affordance at all), the hand-off chooser (4), the label and human registries (both 5 — the two halves of one story), the Routing tab's Inputs header (6), the Stages panel (7), Anomalies (8), Improvements (9) and the Copilot header (10). Slugs are single-sourced in `src/pages/process/flows-help-sections.js` — the one registry that is NOT flat in `pages/` (the call #1990/#1993/#1994/#1989 made to avoid a one-file directory), because `pages/process/` already holds two dozen of this page's pure modules and eight of the ten call sites. **Chapter 2 carries no "?" of its own**: its surface *is* the button that opens chapter 1, deliberately landing one heading above it so the reader gets the definition and the list rather than the list alone (scrolling up is the direction nobody tries). **This is where the z-index bug #1990 flagged and left is fixed**: seven Flow-editor `SidePanel`s (60/61) now publish a help subject — `modal:flow-{edge,labels,humans,stages,node,anomalies,improvements}`, none claiming a topic, all falling through to `/flows` — so a "?" inside one no longer opens the help panel *behind* the surface it explains; `FlowCopilotPanel` deliberately publishes none, being a flex child of the canvas rather than an overlay, and `NodeInspector` publishes on `RoutingSection`'s behalf since it owns the panel body. **Two of the ticket's own premises were wrong and the topic now says the opposite** (both verified against the code): the left list has **Teams and Drafts only** — the read-only *Role templates* blueprint scope is discarded outright (`?proc=templates` is dropped by `Briefings.jsx`) and the BriefingPack list went with #1965, so the surviving BriefingPack surface is the top-bar **Import packs** migration aid; and the edge chooser's tiles are **per drag-source** (`PROCESS_CREATE_TYPES` in `connection-capabilities.js`) — an *agent* source offers label / issue-channel / PR-channel / artifact plus a collapsed *Parent / child*, while **GitHub trigger** and **Webhook** hang off the `@github` / `@external` source nodes, and `dependency` is no longer an explicit tile at all (an artifact with no name degrades to one). Guarded on both sides: `src/pages/process/flows-help-extraction.test.js` (page-side, 14 tests) and a `chapters` list in the `flows` `TOPIC_CONTENT` entry. `/flows` is the system prompt's worst-case grounding context, so the growth is measured, not assumed: 238 417 → **244 687** of 340 000.

**Context-help E3 — the Knowledge topic (#1877).** `help-topics/knowledge.md` (`routes: ["/knowledge"]`) is the single home for the densest concept page: Memory vs Knowledge vs Skills, the three-step learning flow with the three entry kinds (fact / entity / convention), **converged** knowledge, the per-agent **digest**, the domains / consolidate / distill power tools, and the full **Autonomy** 0–3 ladder, **Review** mode and **promotion gates** table. `KnowledgeFlow.jsx` lost its dismissible "How it works" intro box (with the `kf.introSeen` localStorage key, the reopen button and the `HowItWorks` component) and the header now carries the shared `PageHelpButton` next to the title. Crucially the long copy also came out of the **shared** `KnowledgeLearningConfig.jsx`, which the Agent Team wizard's Knowledge step renders too: `AUTONOMY_LEVELS` keeps only the short per-level `desc` (the long `tip` is gone — the topic is the single source both call sites point at) and the three `PanelSection` blurbs are one-liners. The remaining `PanelSection` blurbs (Forgetting / Assist model / Storage) are field-level configuration microcopy at the point of decision and stay on the page, as do the empty states, the `KIND_EXPLAIN` chip tooltips and the consolidation primer. The Knowledge tour chapter's opening beat (`e2e/journeys/knowledge.yaml` → `knowledge.openOverview`) now opens the Help panel's "This page" face instead of the deleted box, so the generated `tour-manifest.json` + `steps-catalog.json` move with it. **Widened to a per-surface reference (#1985, epic #1981).** E3 lifted the intro box into one page-level topic, which left the page's individual surfaces reaching the same wall of prose from the top; the topic is now six clearly-headed chapters — **Memory vs Knowledge vs Skills · How learning works, in three steps · The per-agent digest, and what stays searchable · Autonomy, Review and the promotion gates** (keeping `### Autonomy…`, `### Review…`, `### Promotion gates…` inside it) **· Power tools: domains, consolidate and distill · Configuration: forgetting, assist model and storage** (with `### Forgetting`, `### Assist model`, `### Storage`) — and seven affordances deep-link into them: the restored page-header button (chapter 1), the *Team knowledge* column header (chapter 3, where the `digest` badges are), the Domains and Consolidation panel primers plus the skill inspector's footer (chapter 5), and two inside the *Team knowledge configuration* panel — one just above the shared learning ladder (chapter 4) and one in the panel's own lead (chapter 6). Slugs are single-sourced in `src/pages/knowledge-help-sections.js`, deliberately flat beside the page's other pure modules (`knowledge-scope.js`, `knowledge-activation-view.js`, `skill-inbox-view.js` …) rather than in a one-file `pages/knowledge/` directory — the call #1990 made for `skills-help-sections.js`. Guarded on both sides: `src/pages/knowledge-help-extraction.test.js` (page-side) and a `chapters` list in the `knowledge` `TOPIC_CONTENT` entry (topic-side). **The ladder's "?" is mounted at this page's render site, not inside `KnowledgeLearningConfig.jsx`** — the wizard's Knowledge step renders that same component, where `openHelpFor()` resolves against `/team-setup` and would land on a different topic (#1991 owns that step); the guard asserts the shared component stays affordance-free. **Two `SidePanel` surfaces now publish a help subject** (`modal:knowledge-config`, `modal:knowledge-skill`, registered in `CONTEXT_KEYS`/`OVERLAY_KEYS` with no topic of their own so resolution falls through to `/knowledge`): both host a "?" and, at z-index 60/61, would otherwise open the panel *behind* the surface it explains. **The ticket's edge case was wrong, and the topic now says the opposite:** `KnowledgeFlow.jsx` has **no** client-side permission gate at all, and every write route behind the Configure panel checks `knowledgeTeamVisible` — team *visibility*, not `canManageTeam` — so any member who can see a team can change its autonomy level, review mode, gates, forgetting horizon, assist model and storage location. Guarded as a concept so a reword cannot restore the "Team-Admin-gated" reading. The topic also gained the two configuration subjects it never had (the **Assist model** — why an autonomous curator/distillation run cannot use the header's per-user picker, and that OAuth seats are never offered because that path is not billable — and **Storage**, including the pre-save reachability probe, the protected-branch warning and that relocating never moves what is already stored) plus what the digest actually does (conventions first, then converged, then recent, until a fixed token budget is full; everything past it stays searchable; retired, merge-superseded and review-pending entries never appear, and an entry past its validity date drops out on its own).

**Context-help E4 — the Skills topic (#1878).** `help-topics/skills.md` (`routes: ["/skills"]`) is the single home for the Skills concepts: what a skill is (`SKILL.md` plus assets), the **Global / Team / Agent** tiers and the own-team-pool-first resolution order, the three mechanics behind the page's verbs — a **grant** (the list of names an agent is entitled to), **seeding / activation** (when the files are actually copied into the workspace) and **Install into agent** — the Promote / Move / Adopt / Distill / Merge / Import-Export table, what an overwrite replaces, what merging does to provenance and grants, and Skills vs Knowledge vs Memory. `SkillsRegistry.jsx` lost its four-paragraph intro box, its inline "See how the knowledge system works →" jump-off and the `← Knowledge` back-link under the title (the topic's `seeAlso` carries the Knowledge link now); the header is title + one-line description + the shared `PageHelpButton`, and the merge dialog's lead is a single line. The action-panel notes that sit next to a button (the agent-skill "copy it to a team first" hint, the Adopt-vs-Move-to-team blurb, the Retire-source-skills checkbox hint, the Distill dialog and the three overwrite `ConfirmDialog` warnings) stay put as point-of-decision microcopy. **Widened to a per-surface reference (#1990, the first child of epic #1981).** The topic is now six clearly-headed chapters — **What a skill is** (incl. what the viewer's Content / Versions / Lineage / Usage tabs show and the drift states) **· The three tiers, and which one an agent gets · Grant, activation and Install into agent · The actions: promote, move, adopt, distill, merge, import and export · Overwrite and merge: what each one replaces · Skills vs Knowledge vs Memory** — and each action surface carries a "?" that opens the panel **at its chapter** (slugs single-sourced in `src/pages/skills-help-sections.js`, deliberately flat beside the page's other pure modules rather than in a one-file `pages/skills/` directory; page-side guard `src/pages/skills-help-extraction.test.js`, topic-side `chapters` list in `help-topics-content.test.js`). **Two things the ticket's premises got wrong, both found by reading the code.** (1) E4's claim that an install is "a one-off push that a later workspace reset does not re-seed" is **the opposite of what the code does**: `POST /api/skills/install` delegates to `/internal/agents/:id/skills/activate`, whose `setAgentSkillActive` writes the grant (`skills[]`, or a member's `skillsOverride`) *before* the additive workspace sync — "the grant edit is what makes install/activate survive a full re-seed (AC1)" — so grant vs install differs in *when*, not in how long it lasts; the topic now says so and the `writes the grant` concept guards it. (2) "Keep the existing shared button" had nothing to keep: the E4 header (`<h1>` + description + `PageHelpButton`) was **silently dropped by a mid-run merge** with the #1847/page-actions header unification (merge commit `1ebc6603` resolved the header block wholesale to main's side), so `/skills` reached main with no page-level "?" at all — restored here into the page-action header slot, which since that change *is* the page's only header. **The two overlay surfaces publish a help subject** (`modal:skill-detail`, `modal:skills-merge`, registered in `CONTEXT_KEYS`/`OVERLAY_KEYS` with no topic of their own so resolution falls through to `/skills`): a `PageHelpButton` inside the detail `SidePanel` (backdrop z-index 60) or the merge dialog (1000) would otherwise open the panel **behind** the surface it explains, since `panelZIndex` lifts it above the modal layer only while an overlay is active. The same latent bug exists on `/flows` (`EdgeDialogs` renders a "?" inside a `SidePanel` with no subject) and is **flagged, not fixed** here.

**Context-help E5 — the Plugins topic (#1879).** `help-topics/plugins.md` (`routes: ["/plugins"]`) is the single home for the plugin-system concepts, because this ticket also **removed the standalone `/plugin-system` page outright** (concept §9 — no redirect): `PluginSystemHelp.jsx`, its route + lazy import, the "Learn about plugins" link on the Plugins page, and its `BREADCRUMBS` / `ROUTE_TITLES` / `PAGE_ROUTES` entries are all gone. The topic carries the plain-language "what a plugin is", the **install vs grant** split (System Admin installs + starts the broker and grants nothing; Team Admin grants, members inherit), the closed capability enum and the platform gate, **scope granularity** per team vs per agent and the per-scope quota, what activating does (live additive sync, no respawn, the call shows up as an ordinary Bash step in the session log), plugins with their own embedded UI, the typical use cases, the platform-plugin-vs-agent-plugin disambiguation — plus a **Deep dive** half (how the broker+CLI pair works technically, and the whole build-a-plugin reference: `plugin.json` fields, the broker's per-call obligations, the CLI contract, the optional UI extension and the Broker SDK). `Plugins.jsx` is title + one-line description + the shared `PageHelpButton`; the install dialog keeps its per-capability risk cards and quota line (a decision surface) behind a one-line lead, and the uninstall dialog's grants-stay-in-place prose is one line with the detail in the topic. **Widened to a per-surface reference (#1986, epic #1981).** E5 put the longest topic on any admin page — the install/grant story *and* the whole build-a-plugin reference, since the same ticket deleted the only other home for it — behind one page-level "?"; the topic is now eight clearly-headed chapters — **What a plugin is** (with `### Typical use cases`, `### Platform plugin vs Claude Code agent plugin`) **· Install and grant are two different steps · Capabilities and the platform gate · Scope granularity and quota · What activating does — and what uninstalling undoes · Plugins with their own UI · Assigning a plugin to a team on this page · Deep dive: how a plugin works, and how to build one** (`###` sub-headings for the broker+CLI pair, `plugin.json`, the broker's obligations, the CLI contract, the UI extension and the Broker SDK) — and five surfaces deep-link into them: the page header (chapter 1), the install confirm (chapter 3 of the ticket's 2/3/4 span — the middle one, so all three are within one scroll of the landing point), the uninstall confirm (chapter 5), the `PluginTeamAssign` control on an installed card (chapter 7) and a **restored deep-dive pointer** at the foot of the list (chapter 8 — E5 removed the "Learn about plugins" link along with `/plugin-system`, which left the build-a-plugin reference reachable only by scrolling). Slugs are single-sourced in `src/pages/plugins-help-sections.js`, deliberately flat beside the page's other pure modules (`plugin-registry-view.js`, `plugin-grant-view.js`, `plugin-team-assign-view.js`) rather than in the one-file `pages/plugins/` directory the ticket's Scope-in named — the call #1990/#1993/#1994/#1989 all made. **Open question 1, answered: the deep dive stays ONE chapter** with `###` sub-headings, because the ticket's own surface table names exactly one deep-dive affordance and a split would leave a chapter no "?" could reach. **The E5 header button was not actually there:** like `/skills` in #1990, the page-level "?" was lost when the #1847 page-actions unification took over the header, so `/plugins` reached main with no page-level help at all — restored here into the page-action slot, which since that change is the page's only header. **Both confirm dialogs publish a help subject** (`modal:plugin-install`, `modal:plugin-uninstall`, registered in `CONTEXT_KEYS`/`OVERLAY_KEYS` with no topic of their own so resolution falls through to `/plugins`) — a `ConfirmDialog` is the shared `Modal` at z-index 9999, so without the `panelZIndex` lift a "?" inside one opens the panel *behind* the decision it explains; publishing from the dialog-body components (which `ConfirmDialog` mounts only while open) is also what makes the ticket's System-Admin-only edge case fall out for free. The team-assign "?" is mounted **above** the no-team branch, since the reader told they have no team to assign to is exactly the one who needs that chapter. Guarded on both sides: `src/pages/plugins-help-extraction.test.js` (page-side, covering `PluginTeamAssign.jsx` too) and a `chapters` list in the `plugins` `TOPIC_CONTENT` entry. Budget: the worst-case grounding context (`/flows`) is **unmoved** — this PR does not touch its `seeAlso` neighbourhood, so `measureWorstCaseSystemPrompt()` returns the same length on `main` and this branch (verified identical); the `/plugins` context itself grows 212 895 → 217 678 of 340 000.

**Context-help E6 — the Working Environments topic (#1880).** `help-topics/environments.md` (`routes: ["/environments"]`) absorbed the page's "What is a Working Environment?" box — the reusable-workspace-recipe definition, the what-an-agent-inherits list (repositories / GitHub token / `mcpServers` refs / model provider / `additionalContext`), and the *same recipe, own isolated workspace* point — and folds in the concept material that was only in `docs/working-environments.md`: repo-less environments (and their GitHub-channel trade-off), the SSH-URL-needs-a-key vs HTTPS-URL-needs-the-token rule, `readOnly` repositories, and the fact that repositories are cloned **at creation** so an edit reaches existing agents only through **Sync all agents** (`POST /api/environments/:id/sync-agents`, #1342 — replaced by #2066's save-time offer, see the propagation note above). `WorkingEnvironments.jsx` keeps a one-line description plus a `PageHelpButton` labelled *"What is a Working Environment?"*, and every per-field hint inside the create/edit form (SSH-key, URL probe, branch, read-only, token, provider, CLAUDE.md context) stays at the point of decision. **Widened to a per-surface reference (#1987, epic #1981).** E6 left one topic behind one page-level "?" on a page whose real decision surfaces all sit *inside a side panel*; the topic is now seven clearly-headed chapters — **What a Working Environment is** (with `### What an edit reaches, and what it does not`) **· Repositories** (with `### Read-only repositories`, `### Bringing existing agents up to date`, `### The "agents not up to date" badge` — both renamed/added by #2066) **· Credentials · MCP server references · Model provider binding · Additional context · Toolchains** (with `### The Toolchains panel`) — and five affordances deep-link into them: the page header (chapter 1), the *Git Repositories* section header (chapter 2), the propagation sub-section *inside* chapter 2 — whose "?" **moved with its surface in #2066**, from the removed *Sync all agents* panel onto the save-time `RepoPropagationDialog`, which therefore also had to register the `modal:env-propagation` overlay (a full-screen dialog at 9999 would open the help behind itself, the `modal:user-delete` story) — the *GitHub Token* section header (chapter 3) and the System-Admin Toolchains panel (chapter 7) — **joined by the *MCP servers* section (chapter 4) once #2051 gave that form its picker, six affordances now**. Slugs are single-sourced in `src/pages/environments-help-sections.js`, flat beside the page's other pure modules (`working-env-submit-state.js`, `toolchain-select-view.js`) rather than in the `pages/environments/` directory the ticket's Scope-in named — the call #1990/#1993/#1994/#1989 all made. **Both side panels publish a help subject** (`modal:environment-form`, `modal:toolchains`, registered in `CONTEXT_KEYS`/`OVERLAY_KEYS` with no topic of their own so resolution falls through to `/environments`): four of the six "?" (the MCP one added by #2051) live inside the create/edit `SidePanel` (z-index 60/61), so without the `panelZIndex` lift they would open the help *behind* the form they explain. Chapters 5–6 carry **no** "?" — the provider select and the CLAUDE.md field keep their existing point-of-decision hints; **chapter 4 gained an MCP server/credential picker and its own "?" in #2051**, and because the form still omits `mcpServers` from the `PUT` unless the selection actually changed, saving it cannot wipe a set the wizard wrote. **One factual correction rides along:** the Description field's hint said *"Shown only in this console — not passed to the agent"*, but `prompt-assembly.js` writes both the environment **name and description** into the agent's `CLAUDE.md` above its repository list — the hint now says so, since a reader who trusted it could put a private note in a field the agent reads. The topic also gained what no surface stated: the per-edit reach table (repositories reach existing agents only via **Sync all agents**; token/provider/toolchain changes **recreate** the env's running agents; an SSH-key selection changes nothing until the next clone; `additionalContext` reaches an existing agent only the next time its prompt/skills/plugins are saved), that the URL probe is **advice, not a gate** (an all-unreachable environment saves), that `readOnly` is an instruction in the prompt and **not a file permission**, that an HTTPS URL with no token **refuses the clone** rather than falling back to the SSH key, that the propagation **does** fetch, switch a branch and repoint a remote but only on a clean and fully pushed clone (#2066 retired the older *never re-clones/fetches/switches branch* claim rather than rewording it), that a **removal deletes nothing, ever**, that the badge is derived from the agents' actual workspaces rather than remembered from the last save, and that the propagation reaches disabled agents but skips un-materialized stubs, and that a delete refusal names only the *first* agent using the environment (including one the caller cannot see). Guarded on both sides: `src/pages/environments-help-extraction.test.js` (page-side, mutation-proved) and a `chapters` list in the `environments` `TOPIC_CONTENT` entry.

**This topic was itself retired by #2170:** the page it was extracted from is gone (see *Working Environments* above), so `help-topics/environments.md` and `environments-help-sections.js`/`environments-help-extraction.test.js` were deleted. The material that still describes a live surface — repositories, credentials, MCP references, the propagation dialog — was folded into `help-topics/agent-chat.md` (the Settings tab's Working Environment section now hosts it); the Toolchains chapter moved to the new `help-topics/environment-templates.md`, whose page (`EnvironmentTemplates.jsx`) is the only surviving host of that panel.

**Context-help E7 — the Access topic (#1881).** `help-topics/access-users.md` (`routes: ["/teams"]`) absorbed the `Teams.jsx` intro box: an **access scope** — internally a `Team` — is the *human* collaboration & visibility tenant that owns **exactly one**-to-many agents, Agent Teams, orchestrators, Working Environments and team-scoped credentials, and it is emphatically **not** the *Agent Team* squad built by the Agent Team Wizard (the topic carries that distinction as a side-by-side table, since the page that used to state it is gone). The topic additionally records what the removed box never said but the code enforces: the `default` scope every pre-scope resource was adopted into, the three roles as they actually live (`user.systemAdmin` global flag vs `team-admin`/`team-member` memberships, one record per user+scope so a re-add *updates their role in place*, fail-closed for a membership-less user), what a scope gates server-side per `admin-console/api/scope.js` (list filtering by `teamId`, `visibility: 'private'` agents visible only to their owner, `canManageTeam` → 403 on management calls), that Agent-Team members inherit their tenant from the parent, and the delete rules (the `default` scope is protected, any other is refused while it still owns resources). `Teams.jsx` keeps a one-line description plus a `PageHelpButton`; the `ROLE_HINTS` legend and the read-only/`Users`-page footnotes inside the Manage panel stay at the point of decision. The sibling `/users` page was deliberately left out of this ticket and is covered by E16 (#1891), which gave it its own `users` topic.

**Context-help E8 — the Model Providers topic (#1882).** `help-topics/models.md` (`routes: ["/models"]`) is the single home for the provider/auth concepts: what a Model Provider is (URL + credential + discovered model catalog, bound by a Working Environment or an agent — never a model directly), the agent-override → environment-default → `.env`-fallback precedence table, and the two auth types side by side — **`api-key`** (Bearer against a Base URL, any Anthropic-Messages-API endpoint, token at `0600` and never read back, legacy providers behave identically) vs **`oauth-token`** (a `claude setup-token` seat, no Base URL, `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN` injected and the `ANTHROPIC_*` variables stripped, so it **bypasses any configured proxy** and its gateway-side logging/budget; the login's one-time code is redeemed server-side). The Consumer-Terms **single-user** constraint keeps its consequences here too (per-seat Enterprise tokens rather than one shared seat, rate limits shared with your own Claude sessions, server-side assist calls deliberately skipping this auth type), as do what Test-connection / Discover-models / the health poller actually do and why a probe always names the credential it used. `ModelRegistry.jsx` lost its intro paragraph (header is now one line + the shared `PageHelpButton`) and the long `AUTH_TYPE_OPTIONS` `desc` rationale is one line per option — the radios are a live choice, so they keep a short label. The OAuth box **keeps** the single-user rule itself, because it guards a required confirmation tick; only its rationale moved, with a contextual `PageHelpButton` next to it. Point-of-decision microcopy stays: the Base-URL "must speak the Anthropic Messages API" hint, the `claude setup-token` step instructions in the login/probe dialogs, and the restart-agents dialog. The identical single-user paragraph in `SetupWizard.jsx` is a *different* page (`/quickstart`) and is untouched by this ticket. **Widened to a per-surface reference (#1988, epic #1981).** E8 left one topic behind one page-level "?", which put the page's two real decision surfaces — the auth-type choice and the probe buttons — a whole document away from their own subject; the topic is now six clearly-headed chapters — **What a Model Provider is** (with `### Which model an agent actually uses` and `### The two ways a provider authenticates`) **· API key / proxy authentication · Anthropic subscription authentication** (with `### Single-user only — the compliance constraint`) **· Test connection and Discover models · Health: the poller and the Health column · Changing credentials and deleting** — and **five** affordances deep-link into them: the page header (chapter 1, the existing shared button, now naming its chapter), the *Authentication* row, the OAuth box (the existing #1882 button, re-pointed), the Test/Discover row (chapter 4) and the **Health** column header (chapter 5, in its own `stopPropagation` wrapper — DataTable attaches an onClick to *every* header cell and merely no-ops it inside for a `sortable: false` column, so the wrapper is what keeps the "?" inert if the column ever becomes sortable). Slugs are single-sourced in `src/pages/models-help-sections.js`, deliberately flat beside the page's other pure modules (`llm-probe-state.js`, `provider-health-view.js`, `model-provider-submit-state.js`) rather than in the one-file `pages/models/` directory the ticket's Scope-in named — the call #1990/#1989/#1993/#1994 all made. **Two decisions worth knowing.** (1) The ticket's surface table maps the auth-type radios *and* the OAuth box to "chapter 2/3", but the radios are **one** control serving two chapters, so their single "?" **follows the selection** (`section={isOauth ? …oauthAuth : …apiKeyAuth}`) — a fixed chapter would be wrong half the time — while the OAuth box's button, whose label promises *"what this means for a team"*, is pointed at the `###` **inside** chapter 3 that carries exactly that, not at the chapter's top. (2) The form **publishes an overlay subject** (`modal:model-provider`, registered in `CONTEXT_KEYS`/`OVERLAY_KEYS` with no topic of its own so resolution falls through to `/models`): three of the five "?" buttons live inside a shared `SidePanel` at z-index 60/61, so without the `panelZIndex` lift they would open the help panel *behind* the form they explain — which is the state the #1882 OAuth-box button has been in since it shipped. The topic also gained what the page's table never explained: what each column shows, that the `.env` fallback row is read-only and reports `—` because nothing probes it, why a probe **names the credential it used** (pasted / stored / none, and what a `401` means in each case), that a probe is blocked outright in **three** states — while a subscription login is pending, for a subscription provider carrying no token at all (the state a fresh create sits in until you log in or paste one, so not an edge case), and for an `api-key` provider with no Base URL — and that `Unknown` is an honest never-measured status rather than a warning — health being a **signal, not a gate**. Guarded on both sides: `src/pages/models-help-extraction.test.js` (page-side) and a `chapters` list in the `models` `TOPIC_CONTENT` entry (topic-side). No point-of-decision microcopy was traded for a "?": the Base-URL hint, the `claude setup-token` instructions, the probe's own credential note, the restart-agents dialog and — above all — the OAuth box's required single-user tick are re-asserted by the guard.

**Context-help E9 — the MCP Servers topic (#1883).** `help-topics/mcp.md` (`routes: ["/mcp"]`) absorbed the page's intro box (the **three-part model** — a catalog of *servers* whose `env`/`headers` template marks a secret slot with `${credential}`, a separate *credential* store, and a **Working Environment** that pairs the two) and the *"where does `required` come from"* `InfoTip` (now a one-liner: the status is derived from the template on every read via `requiresCredential`, never operator-set). It also writes down what neither surface said: the seed-time step **merges** the substituted stanza into *each agent's own* `.claude/settings.json` under `mcpServers.<id>` (other settings survive), a `${credential}` with **no** bound credential has that single key **dropped** plus a seed warning (the literal placeholder never reaches a workspace, the agent is still created, so the call fails at runtime unauthenticated) and the converse bound-but-unused credential is ignored; the per-transport field split (`stdio` → `command`/`args`/**env**, `http`/`sse` → `url`/**headers**, the two shapes **mutually exclusive** on the wire); that the *Default server* on a credential is a **hint only**, not a pairing; that secrets are readable only as a **fingerprint** and rotation means add-new-and-re-point; that substitution happens **once at seed time**, so an edit reaches only agents seeded afterwards and a registered server arrives on the agent's **next invocation**; and that a delete is refused while an environment still references the server/credential (**detach it there first**). Flagged by E9, fixed by #2051: the Working Environments **form now pairs a server with a credential directly** (before, an environment's pairs could be set only through the API / at team creation); the pairs remain *visible* read-only in the Agent-Team wizard's Knowledge step, and a single agent can still override the inherited list via `mcpServersOverride` on its own agent form (Agent Chat's **Settings** tab since #1930 removed the Agents page). `McpServers.jsx` is one-line description + the shared `PageHelpButton`; every per-field hint in the two create forms (the `${credential}` template hint, the transport option labels, the 0600 secret-storage note, the id help) stays at the point of decision. **Widened to a per-surface reference (#1989, epic #1981).** E9 left one topic behind one page-level "?", which put the page's only real decision surfaces — the two **create forms** — a whole document away from their own subject; the topic is now five clearly-headed chapters — **The three-part model · The server registry and its transports · The credential store and `${credential}` substitution · Environment pairing and the seed-time merge · Deleting a server or a credential** — and four affordances deep-link into them: the page header (chapter 1, the existing shared button, now naming its chapter), a lead-row "?" in the server create panel (chapter 2) and in the credential create panel (chapter 3), and one beside the *required* column marker (chapter 3, in its own `stopPropagation` wrapper so asking the question does not re-sort the sortable column). Slugs are single-sourced in `src/pages/mcp-help-sections.js`, deliberately flat beside the page's other pure module (`mcp-server-submit-state.js`) rather than in the one-file `pages/mcp/` directory the ticket's Scope-in named — the call #1990/#1993/#1994 all made. **Both create panels publish a help subject** (`modal:mcp-server`, `modal:mcp-credential`, registered in `CONTEXT_KEYS`/`OVERLAY_KEYS` with no topic of their own so resolution falls through to `/mcp`): they are shared `SidePanel`s at z-index 60/61, so without the `panelZIndex` lift a "?" inside them would open the help panel *behind* the form it explains. Chapters 4 and 5 carry **no** "?" — the pairing's own surface is the Working Environments form (which since #2051 has the MCP picker and carries the "?" for it there, not on this page) and the delete rule is already stated in the `ConfirmDialog` that enforces it; the panel renders the whole topic, so both stay one scroll from wherever a deep link lands. The topic also gained what the page's tables never explained: what each column shows, that there is no edit action on either table, that an unreadable or unregistered pair is skipped entirely (distinct from the dropped-key case), and that a refusal names only the *first* environment holding the entry. Guarded on both sides: `src/pages/mcp-help-extraction.test.js` (page-side) and a `chapters` list in the `mcp` `TOPIC_CONTENT` entry (topic-side).

**Context-help E10 — the Credentials topic (#1884).** `help-topics/credentials.md` is the first **multi-route** topic (`routes: ["/ssh-keys", "/github-tokens", "/my-credentials"]`, all three already in `PAGE_ROUTES`): the "why both an SSH key and a GitHub token" defense-in-depth prose existed in **five** places (the three credential pages plus both SetupWizard credential steps), so it is authored once here and each page keeps title + one-line description + `PageHelpButton`. The topic carries the SSH-vs-PAT job split as a table (transport over `ssh://` vs the Events API / `gh` / HTTPS git; deploy-key narrowing; `GH_TOKEN`/`GITHUB_TOKEN` injection; fingerprint/preview-only readback; SSH-URL-needs-a-key vs HTTPS-URL-needs-the-token), the **blast-radius** rationale stated as what each leak *cannot* do, and what no box ever said but `docs/credentials.md` + the code define: the three **ownership tiers** and which page shows which, the strict personal privacy guarantee (no `scope.all` override, no administrator **break-glass**, offboarding deletes rather than transfers), the per-operation-class runtime resolution (`personal → team → system` as a *preference among tiers*, attributed writes **refused** rather than silently dropping to a shared identity, polling always shared), and the rotate-**in-place** (personal, respawns the owner's agents) vs **create-new-and-repoint** (team/system, Edit is rename/reassign only) split plus the `Used by` delete block. Deliberately left inline as point-of-decision instructions: the one-time-secret warnings, the classic/fine-grained scope checklists and every **SAML SSO** `Configure SSO` step in the create/rotate dialogs. The SetupWizard's two credential steps now render **one shared** `TwoCredentialsNote` (single source) and — since E17 (#1892) — offer this very topic inline under the step via `SetupStepHelp`, since the first-run screen replaces the Shell that mounts the panel; `credentials-help-copy.test.js` is the source-reading drift guard over both properties. **Widened to a per-surface reference (#1993, epic #1981).** Being multi-route is exactly why this topic needed chapters first: three pages resolve to one file, so a reader arriving from **GitHub Tokens** used to land at the top of an explanation whose first third is about SSH. The topic is now six clearly-headed chapters — **Why both an SSH key and a GitHub token · SSH keys · GitHub tokens · My Credentials · Ownership tiers and privacy · Rotation and runtime resolution** — and each of the three page headers deep-links its "?" to chapters 2/3/4 respectively (slugs single-sourced in `src/pages/credentials-help-sections.js`, flat beside the pages' other pure modules per the #1990 call; page-side guard `src/pages/credentials-help-extraction.test.js`, topic-side `chapters` list in `help-topics-content.test.js`). **Open question 1 answered: no.** Chapters 1/5/6 are the cross-page story and get **no** "?" of their own — the panel renders the *whole* topic and merely scrolls to the requested chapter, so they are one scroll from wherever a deep link lands; a fourth "Learn more" affordance would be a second entry point into the same file, and the ticket's own surface table names exactly three. Two consequences worth knowing: the **SetupWizard's** two credential steps alias this topic through `CONTEXT_KEYS` and render its **whole body** inline via `SetupStepHelp`, so that collapsible block grew with the topic (collapsed by default, no deep-link — the wizard is explicitly out of scope here); and My Credentials' "?" is deliberately mounted **outside** the `canOwn` branch, so a reader who *cannot* own personal credentials still reaches chapter 4 to find out why (asserted by ordering in the page-side guard; since #2059 that branch is a fail-closed guard rather than the env single-admin's permanent state). The topic also gained three accuracy corrections from reading the pages: the delete dialog's in-use list is **advisory** (the button stays enabled; the *server* refuses), a Team Admin never sees edit/delete on a **system**-scoped credential, and a system credential assigned to no team stays visible to System Admins only.

**Context-help E11 — the Token Usage topic (#1885).** `help-topics/token-usage.md` (`routes: ["/token-usage"]`) absorbed the page's multi-paragraph intro banner (what the page shows + how the Statistics and Work-items tabs differ), the *reasoning* behind the tokens-not-costs caveat, and the full attribution-tier explainer that used to be the long `TIER_SIGNAL_HELP` InfoTip string. `TokenUsage.jsx` is now the one-line `PAGE_DESCRIPTION` plus a `PageHelpButton`; `TIER_SIGNAL_HELP` keeps only the four signal names in strongest-first order (size-guarded by a `token-usage-view.test.js` assertion so the topic's table cannot creep back into the page), and `COST_NOTE` keeps the *rule* ("token counts, not costs") because it sits directly above the figures it guards. The topic additionally records what neither surface ever said: only **agent runs** produce usage records — the console's own one-shot assist calls (Flow Copilot, *Generate agent prompt*, the improvement analyst, Ask) never appear here; the page is **System-Admin-only** (403, not a filtered view); a record's cost field is deliberately never read, which is *why* nothing is priced; `Group by model` fans a run across its per-model split so a run without per-model detail counts in the totals but in no model row, and a record with no team/provider/model lands in an explicit `unknown` bucket; attribution is **strongest-signal-wins** (flow > created > event > branch) so *Signals* is a tally, not overlapping guesses; the `shared` / `flow-mismatch` flags; why the unattributed remainder is always reported and excluded from the Ø-per-work-item averages; and that records are filed under the day the run **happened** (the agent's own timestamp, so a spooled record lands in its real day) with no automatic retention on the store. The per-tile `STAT_TILE_HELP` / `WORK_ITEM_TILE_HELP` InfoTips stay at the figures they explain. **Widened to a per-surface reference (#1994, epic #1981).** The topic is now four clearly-headed chapters — **What the page shows** (with `### Token counts, not costs`, `### The four token classes`, `### Choosing a time range`) **· The Statistics tab · The Work items tab** (with `### Coverage, and the unattributed remainder`) **· How runs are attributed** (with `### The two flags`, `### Which day a run lands in`) — and each is the landing target of a "?" on the matching surface, via the #1972 deep-link mechanism. The slugs live once in `src/pages/token-usage-help-sections.js` (`TOKEN_USAGE_HELP_SECTIONS`, flat beside `token-usage-view.js` rather than in a one-file `pages/token-usage/` dir — the `skills-help-sections.js` call). **Where the affordances sit:** the page-header button (now naming chapter 1 explicitly instead of relying on it being first), one inside each of the two tab labels, and one beside each of the two attribution InfoTips (the *Signals* column header in the Work-items table, the *Tier* column header in the per-run detail). The tab "?" is wrapped in the same `stopPropagation` span the sortable *Signals* header uses, so asking what the other tab shows does **not** navigate you to it. No surface here is a modal, so — unlike #1990 — no `useHelpSubject` overlay registration is needed. Page-side drift is guarded by `src/pages/token-usage-help-extraction.test.js`; the topic side by a `chapters` list in the `token-usage` `TOPIC_CONTENT` entry. Every point-of-decision hint E11 protected stays on the page, `TIER_SIGNAL_HELP` included — its size guard is untouched and still passing.

**Cost & quota attribution for globally-shared model providers (#2137, follows #2136).** A **global** model provider (#2136) has no team, so `teamId` stopped being a sufficient accounting key on a usage record.
