Two fields make that spend attributable again, on a strict who-knows-what split: the **agent** stamps `requestedBy` in `build_usage_record` (the Matrix sender of the chat turn, `null` for an autonomous run — the one thing the control plane cannot observe), and the **orchestrator**'s `enrichUsageRecord` resolves it into `attribution: {userId, kind}` (`requesting-user` → `agent-owner` → `unattributed`) and stamps `providerTier` (`global`/`system`/`team`, or `null` for a provider that no longer exists), then **drops** the raw Matrix id — the usage store has no retention by design, so a billing-adjacent log must not accumulate raw chat identities (the resolved account id is stable across a Matrix rename). **Both new fields are written unconditionally**, unlike the null-guarded `teamId`/`roleId`/`provider` backfills beside them: the agent can resolve neither, so a body-supplied value is self-asserted and is overwritten — an `if (out.attribution == null)` guard there would let an agent charge its spend to any account it names. The autonomous key reuses `resolveAgentOwnerId` (a team-member resolves to its parent Agent Team's owner, one hop) so a triggered run's *cost* and its *attributed commits* cannot point at two different people. All decisions are the new pure `orchestrator/usage-attribution.js`. **Read side:** the aggregator (`admin-console/api/usage-summary.js` ⇄ byte-identical `orchestrator/usage-summary.js`) gains `user` and `providerTier` groupings (both land a resolveless or pre-#2137 record in `unknown`), the CSV/JSON export gains `attributedUserId`/`attributionKind`/`providerTier` columns, and the Token Usage page gains a **User** and a **Provider tier** Group-by (`token-usage-view.js` — `providerTierCell` → Global/System/Team/Unknown, `userGroupCell` → the resolved display name or **Unattributed**). **The per-user axis is withheld from agents and enforced server-side:** `AGENT_GROUP_BY` in `orchestrator/usage-query.js` excludes `user` (the console breakdown is System-Admin-only #801, and a team-scoped answer would hand one agent every colleague's spend into an LLM context) while offering `providerTier`; the on-agent `usage` CLI whitelist is only an affordance and is drift-guarded against that server constant, since an agent can call the internal endpoint directly. **Additive only** — a pre-#2137 record groups exactly as before and answers the new groupings as `unknown`; no enforcement is added (caps stay #1484), and because console assist calls still emit no usage record at all, a global provider bound as an `assistLlmEndpointId` still spends unmetered (a known remaining gap, out of scope here). Wiring is source-guarded by `orchestrator/usage-attribution-wiring.test.js` + `token-usage-attribution-wiring.test.js`; `help-topics/token-usage.md` documents the new Group-by options.

**Context-help E12 — the GitHub Event Log topic (#1886).** `help-topics/github-events.md` (`routes: ["/github-events"]`) absorbed the page's multi-clause intro (a **purely observational** log; **Processed events** = the history vs **Pending queue** = the actionable backlog) and both tab leads' re-enumeration of the outcomes, which now read as one line each. The topic carries the outcome table (`delivered` / `digested` / `dropped (throttle | horizon | label-filter | internal-dedup)` / `nobody's listening`, each with a "should you worry?" column) plus a `my agent never woke up` decision path — and records what no on-page copy ever stated: that a repository is polled **only** when some Working Environment holds a GitHub token for it *and* an enabled agent has an enabled `github-event` trigger on it (so an unsubscribed repo, or one whose only subscriber is disabled, yields **no rows at all** — not even *nobody's listening*), that the Events API is lossy by design (no `pull_request.synchronize`), that freshness is a per-repo **seen-id set** rather than a timestamp so the first poll after a restart only baselines, that the poller is **not the only writer** (internal-channel `aee-emit` hand-offs and console-applied labels via the GitHub Issues plugin land here too, tagged with a `source` the UI never renders), that **loop protection is frequency-only** with no source filtering, that pending entries have **no expiry** (they wait for a disabled agent indefinitely; the drain also pauses while a loop holds the workspace), the **7-day** retention + best-effort writing, and that the page is **not team-scoped** (any signed-in user sees every repo's events and can cancel any agent's queued entry — with one gated exception since #2102: **Reassign to…**, the Pending-queue action that hands a waiting *GitHub* entry to a different same-team agent instead of only cancelling it, is Team-Admin-gated on *both* the losing and the gaining agent — injecting work into an agent's queue is operator-initiated trigger-firing at a distance, which #2041 §7/D9 holds Team-Admin-only, so `GET/POST /api/agents/:id/pending/:entryId/reassign-targets|reassign` do not ship ungated the way their read/cancel neighbours do; the picker offers only same-team agents with an enabled trigger that would accept this exact event, the move re-stamps the entry with the *target's* own trigger id and removes-at-source first as the atomic claim — `orchestrator/pending-reassign.js`). `GithubEventLog.jsx` keeps the Shell-header title, a one-line `PAGE_DESCRIPTION` + the shared `PageHelpButton`, and every point-of-decision hint stays: the per-chip `outcomeTooltip`, the collapsible *"What do these outcomes mean?"* legend next to the chips it decodes, the `kindTooltip` badges and both teaching empty states. New `src/pages/github-events-help-copy.test.js` is the source-reading drift guard (it strips comments before asserting a phrase is gone, so a comment naming what moved is not a false positive). **Two docs corrected as a deliberate deviation** (both are in the Helper-Chat corpus, so leaving them would have contradicted the new topic inside one prompt): `docs/triggers.md` and `docs/credentials.md` both claimed the poller *reads the `gh-as-agent` identity markers and drops self-caused events* — it never has (`github-poller.js` states the opposite explicitly), so both now describe the throttle + burst digest as the actual backstops. **Flagged, not fixed:** a burst fold is persisted as `status: 'delivered'` with a separate digest flag and nothing ever writes `status: 'digest'`, so the page's *digested* chip is currently unreachable, digest rows render as ordinary deliveries whose session deep-link resolves to nothing, and `hill-climb-sweep.js`'s `digestFolds` counter is always 0; the topic states this honestly as a caveat. **Widened to a per-surface reference (#1995, epic #1981).** E12 lifted the intro and the two tab leads, which left the page's actual *surfaces* undocumented; the topic is now four clearly-headed chapters — **What the GitHub Event Log is** (observational · retention + best-effort · not team-scoped) **· Processed events** (the eight-column table read left-to-right, where the events come from, and the non-poller writers plus the `source` the UI never renders) **· Outcomes** (the chip table, the `my agent never woke up` path, and *When a repository is polled, and when it is not*) **· Pending queue** — and three surfaces carry a "?" that opens the panel **at its chapter**: the page header (chapter 1, the existing shared button, now naming its chapter explicitly), the Processed-events table toolbar (chapter 2) and — because that tab documents two chapters — the collapsible outcome legend it deepens (chapter 3), plus the Pending-queue toolbar (chapter 4). Slugs are single-sourced in `src/pages/github-events-help-sections.js`, deliberately flat beside the page's other pure modules (`pending-queue-view.js`, `event-outcome-view.js`, `github-event-log-type.js`) rather than in a one-file `pages/github-events/` directory — the same call #1990 made for `skills-help-sections.js`. Guarded on both sides: the existing `github-events-help-copy.test.js` gained the page-side half (every slug a call site names is a real heading; the outcomes "?" is anchored to the `<OutcomeLegend />` render sequence, since the processed tab hosts two), and the `github-events` `TOPIC_CONTENT` entry gained the topic-side `chapters` list. No behaviour changed and no point-of-decision microcopy was traded for a "?" — the legend, the `outcomeTooltip`/`kindTooltip` hovers and both teaching empty states are re-asserted by the guard.

**Context-help E13 — the Maintenance Mode topic (#1887).** `help-topics/maintenance-mode.md` (`routes: ["/maintenance-mode"]`) absorbed the page's intro paragraph and the bordered **"While active"** box — the `cron`/`once` suppression with **exactly one** catch-up run, the durably-queued-but-undrained `http`/`github-event` entries, and the nothing-is-force-stopped point — and writes down what neither surface said: activating only raises one flag (`/workspace/maintenance/state.json`, read by the console, the orchestrator and every agent container via `GET /internal/maintenance-mode`) so the mode is a **soft, cooperative pause**, not the passive signal the old intro claimed — the console *does* lock **every** Agent Chat composer and the New-session button platform-wide while it is up (#502), and the orchestrator's own hourly flow-anomaly-history and daily hill-climb-metrics timers plus the on-demand Anomalies/Improvements panels return early with a `{ maintenance: true }` notice. It also records the two honest caveats the box hid: the **grace period is a display countdown no backend component reads** (only the binary `active` flag is enforced — nothing is force-stopped when it starts or when it hits zero, and the mode stays on until an admin ends it, capped at 24 h), and the active-session counter is **advisory** (platform-wide `activityState === 'busy'` across every team; nothing stops you restarting into a busy agent, which then leaves a `#506` in-flight marker and the *"⚠️ Trigger possibly lost"* DM). `MaintenanceMode.jsx` keeps a one-line description plus the shared `PageHelpButton`, and — per the E8/E11 rule that copy qualifying a live figure or a consequential control stays put — the remaining-grace note keeps only *"it does not end maintenance mode on its own"* and the Activate form keeps a one-line lead plus a second contextual `PageHelpButton` where the box used to sit. Page-side drift is guarded by `admin-console/src/pages/maintenance-mode-help-extraction.test.js` (comment-stripped source assertions).

**Context-help E14 — the creation & onboarding topics (#1888).** One ticket, three topics, because the onboarding path spans four routes. **`help-topics/create.md`** (`routes: ["/create", "/quickstart"]`) absorbed the launcher's tile prose and the one-assistant page's intro/detail pair plus **both** branches of its "What happens here" block: the four equally-ranked entry paths and what each produces (a *draft* that is not live yet · the guided wizard · the fast lane), the *a single agent is simply a team of one* framing — which carried the honest caveat that **Grow into a full team creates a new team** rather than converting the existing assistant until **#2052 replaced that dead end with a real conversion** — and what the fast lane actually does. Reading the two endpoints supplied what no on-page copy said: the repository path probes the repo over SSH (validating the key *and* resolving the default branch from the remote's own HEAD), then **reuses** an environment already pointing at that URL **and reuses an existing standalone agent on it** (the success card reads *Agent reused*), which is why the **Name and Model Provider fields are not used at all on the repository path** — the agent is named after the repo and bound to the environment's/default provider. **Widened by #1956:** the topic now describes **five** ways in, the fifth being the direct **Create a single agent** form the launcher hosts itself, plus a *One assistant, or one agent?* chapter — because the two single-agent paths now sit next to each other and differ in *who decides*: the fast lane infers the environment, the branch and the name from a repository, the direct form infers nothing and needs an environment that already exists. **Re-ranked by #2027:** the topic now presents those five ways as **ranked** rather than equally-weighted — the hero *Chat with one assistant*, the three-card flow group whose heading defines "a flow", and the collapsed *Advanced options* holding the renamed *Configure an agent manually* — plus the per-entry `→` outcome lines and the "what you get" schematics, and it states the deliberately-understated canvas promise (an empty flow prompts you to add the first agent; the guided first step is a separate improvement). **`help-topics/team-setup.md`** (`routes: ["/team-setup"]`) absorbed the wizard header's restatement + its Agent-Team-vs-access-scope line, the Working-Environment step intro and the Knowledge step's long prose: what each of the five steps decides, why only **global-pool** skills are grantable there (the team tier does not exist yet) and why the MCP set is read-only, what `Create team` provisions (one env · one `team` agent · one `per-invocation` `team-member` per role with its own `triggers.json` and DM room), the label-suffix rule for two teams on one repository, extend-mode/collision, the reverse-order rollback + Retry, and that the wizard is **create-only** (grow via the Flow editor or **Add member**). **`help-topics/role-templates.md`** (`routes: ["/role-templates"]`) absorbed the Agent Templates intro paragraph and the invocation-mode field help: Built-in / Edited / Custom, that an edit only reaches **future** agents because each one is an independent copy, the three role types (incl. `pattern` as a Flow-Weaver scaffold), and that **per-invocation** additionally marks an agent *autonomous*. Kept on the pages per the epic's rules: the one-assistant **prerequisite warning cards** + the disabled-submit checklist, the wizard's **go-live notice** (a consequence before a consequential click — only its "disable them afterwards" advice moved, and it gained a second contextual `PageHelpButton`) and its label-suffix hint, the `StepProcess` empty state, and the Role-Templates select option labels + the built-in-override warning. `RoleTemplates.jsx` has no own `<h1>`, so it follows the E6/E7 header pattern (one-line description + `PageHelpButton` as the body's first child). Drift guard: `admin-console/src/pages/onboarding-help-copy.test.js` (comment-stripped source reads over all four pages). **`create.md` widened to a per-surface reference (#1992, epic #1981).** E14 left the two pages it serves reaching one flat topic from the top, so it is now four clearly-headed chapters — **The entry paths** (with `### What each tile produces`, `### A draft, a wizard, a fast lane, or a form`, `### One assistant, or one agent?`) **· A single agent is a team of one** (whose sub-chapter was `### Growing creates a new team, it does not convert this one` until #2052 replaced it with `### Two ways to grow: convert this assistant, or build a new team`) **· The repository fast lane** (with `### Without a repository`, `### With a repository`, `### Why Name and Model Provider are not used here`) **· What you need before you start** — and four surfaces carry a "?" that opens the panel **at its chapter**: the launcher header (chapter 1, the existing button now naming its chapter), the one-assistant page header (chapter 3 — the "what gets created here?" question both submit paths raise), the **team-of-one note** at the foot of that page (chapter 2 — the ticket's surface table wrote "2/3" against the header, read here as two affordances, since the note is the surface that actually raises growing-into-a-team and is always rendered) and the **disabled-submit checklist** (chapter 4 — chosen over the three warning cards because it is the one surface naming *every* outstanding requirement for the submission as filled in, while each card already carries its own direct fix-link). Slugs are single-sourced in `src/pages/create-help-sections.js`, flat beside `create-launcher-view.js`/`quickstart-view.js` per the #1990 call. **Down to three affordances (#2045).** The one-assistant form dropped the **team-of-one footer card** (the *"A single agent is simply a team of one. Grow into a full team →"* block, its link and its "?") and the **description under the GitHub Repository URL field** — a second call-to-action and a hint restating the field's own `(optional)` label and placeholder, both competing with the one action `/quickstart` exists for. Consequences worth knowing: `QUICKSTART_COPY` is down to `heading` + `intro` (`teamOfOneNote`/`growCtaLabel` are **deleted**, not left unrendered) and `fastLaneCopyForcesModeChoice` no longer *requires* the "team of one" wording — a guard demanding copy whose surface is gone is what keeps dead strings alive; it still rejects the mode-picker phrasings #1667 removed. `CREATE_HELP_SECTIONS.teamOfOne` is deleted for the same reason (no call site ⇒ not a slug registry), while chapter 2 itself stays a chapter, guarded by heading rather than by constant. The **framing is not lost**: the launcher hero promises growing into a team where the choice is made (#2027) and chapter 2 keeps the reasoning, one scroll from either landing point. The topic's *"Grow into a full team takes you to…"* sentence named a control that no longer exists, so it now points at the wizard via **+ Create** and says in one clause where the old link went (the `help-topics/agent-chat.md` "where did it go?" precedent) — which is why a removal PR *raised* the grounding worst case. The HTTPS→SSH normalisation nuance the field hint carried was already in chapter 4 (*"the clone always runs over SSH, even when you paste an HTTPS URL — it is normalised"*), so nothing was added for it. **This is the epic's first single-file/two-route topic where both routes are ordinary pages** (E10's is three routes but also aliased into the SetupWizard), so the page-side guard `src/pages/create-help-extraction.test.js` additionally asserts the topic keeps claiming *both* `/create` and `/quickstart` — losing one would silently drop that page's "?" to the Overview fallback and make every `section=` on it a no-op; the topic side is the `chapters` list in the `create` `TOPIC_CONTENT` entry. New material the pages never carried: what a **draft** being not-live actually means, that growing carries nothing across on its own, what the SSH probe's two jobs buy you, and why a warning card and the submit checklist can legitimately disagree.

**Context-help E15 — the Agent Chat topic (#1889).** `help-topics/agent-chat.md` (`routes: ["/chats"]` — the dynamic `/chats/:agentId` collapses onto it, so a specific agent's room resolves to the same topic) absorbed the side-panel explainers: the six trigger types in plain language (**cron** with its skip-if-still-running tick, **once** self-deleting, **http** with its own `X-Hook-Secret`, **github-event** via the ~30 s poller with the label **claim** swap, **data flow** = a push subscription, **loop**), what an **outcome** is (the *Outcome → Label → Consumer* half the agent owns, `exactly one` vs `any of`, and why the panel is **read-only on purpose** — outcomes are flow wiring, authored in the Flow Editor), and what **retiring** a learning does. It also writes down what no panel said: a **loop is a launched run, not a subscription**, which is why the Triggers list never shows one (composer loop icon / Flows self-edge instead); each try starts with a **fresh context window** so state must go to disk; the four terminal states (**done / exhausted / paused / stopped**); the agent-wide **workspace gate** (#1157) that makes every other trigger *and* chat message wait for the **whole** loop while `http`/`github-event` work stays **durably queued**; that a GitHub wake fires for the agent's **own** pushes too (hence `git pull` → relevance check → `[PASS]`); and that retiring is a **reversible archive** (archived-id sidecar + re-projection out of the per-agent digest, git-committed, un-retirable on the Knowledge page) which is **refused outright** for an entry that backs a promoted skill. `TriggersPanel.jsx`'s loop and data-flow banners plus the flow-verifier rationale are one-liners; the panel toolbar carries the shared `PageHelpButton`, and the bespoke chat header (agent name, not a page title — the #1847 exception) carries the page-level one next to the `LearningBadge`. Retained per the E8/E11 rule that copy qualifying a consequential control stays at the point of decision: the **loop launcher** keeps "each run starts fresh" plus its stop contract (#1120) with a second contextual "?", the `OutcomesPanel` **empty state** keeps its full "signals nothing → no teammate is woken" pointer, and the retire dialog keeps the reversibility sentence. Deliberately *not* given a "?": the shared `TriggerForm` itself, since `EdgeDialogs`/`RoutingSection` render it on `/flows` and `/process/:teamId`, where `openHelpFor()` resolves from the **live** route — a route-overriding help button is #1892's territory. **Widened to the whole page (#1972).** E15's deliberate scope left most of Agent Chat undocumented, so the topic is now a per-surface reference: chapters for **What the Agent Chat page is · The agent list · The chat header · The message composer · The right rail** (with `### Sessions — watching a run`, `### The session log and the TODO panel`, `### Workspace — browsing the files`) **· Triggers · Outcomes · Knowledge · Settings — configuring the agent**, and each of those surfaces carries a "?" that opens the panel **at its chapter**. Deep-linking is the mechanism this ticket added (concept §9.5): `openHelpFor(route, { section })` stores a chapter slug + a nonce (so pressing the same "?" twice re-scrolls) and `PageFace` scrolls its own scroller to the first `h2`/`h3` whose **slugged heading text** matches — no `rehype-slug`, no `{#id}` syntax, no change to the shared `SafeMarkdown`/`SANITIZE_SCHEMA` that Agent Chat renders messages with. An unmatched slug renders the topic from the top, which is deliberately also the behaviour for a surface the current agent lacks (no context folders, no team → no Outcomes tab). The slugs live once in `src/pages/agent-chat/agent-chat-help-sections.js` (`AGENT_CHAT_HELP_SECTIONS`) — every call site imports them and `agent-chat-help-extraction.test.js` asserts each one is a real heading, because a reworded heading otherwise degrades **silently**; `help-topics-content.test.js` gained an optional per-topic `chapters` list guarding the same thing from the topic side. **Where the affordances sit:** the rail header row (`AgentChat.jsx`, beside the disabled-agents opt-in — it sat beside the "+" until #1956 moved that to the page header), the session toolbar (beside *About this chat*), the composer's status row, the Outcomes / Knowledge / Settings / Workspace toolbars, the Sessions tab's always-present *Finished sessions* row, and both per-session toolbars for the session log (`SessionLogPanel`'s own header is suppressed when embedded, so the log's "?" rides the toolbar directly above it). The **row context menu** is the one exception: `RowContextMenu` walks a flat list of real entries by index for keyboard traversal and a `PageHelpButton` would be a button inside a button, so the affordance is a menu **entry** — pure `helpMenuItem()` in `rail-lifecycle.js`, appended last and `separated`, chat-only for the same reason as `markUnreadItem` (the Agents page rendered the shared `buildAgentMenu` and had its own topic — #1930 removed it, leaving the chat as the descriptor's only renderer), dispatched by `RailLifecycleHost` before the per-target switches. Every point-of-decision hint E15 protected stays on the page — the guard now asserts that explicitly.

**Context-help E16 — baseline coverage for the last uncovered routes (#1891, epic #1870 Phase 4).** The "works for all pages" guarantee, made enforceable rather than aspirational. Two **net-new** topics (no box to extract — authored from the code): `help-topics/about.md` (`routes: ["/about"]`) explains that the version is **the git commit the bundle was built from** and not a release number (`-dirty` = the build tree had uncommitted changes, `unknown` = no git metadata; embedded at build time, so it never changes at runtime — the sidebar footer shows the commit's own timestamp, this page the hash), how the contributor list is derived from git history at build time (agent identities on `…@agents.local`/`…@agent`/`…@local` and `[bot]` names filtered out, identities merged across e-mails/spellings, ranked by a commit count that is never displayed, whole section self-hiding on a metadata-less build — so a missing name means one of exactly two things), why the CC-BY-4.0 **Parakeet** attribution has to live on a page of its own, and — since there is **no in-app feedback form** — what to hand a problem report (the hash, the route, and the right evidence surface). `help-topics/users.md` (`routes: ["/users"]`) is the route E7 knowingly skipped: it absorbed the `Users.jsx` intro box and adds what no surface stated — the **Subject** is the login identity and is **immutable**, a sign-in tries the **user records first and only then** the env single admin (`ADMIN_USERNAME`/`ADMIN_PASSWORD`, which is why that admin never appears in the list and a password-less record is the normal SSO state), System Admin is a **global flag** while Team Admin is a **membership**, a record with no membership is **fail-closed** (signs in, sees nothing — the top cause of "my console is empty"), and **Delete is an offboarding**: it pauses the departing human's agents (drained in place, even team-locked ones) and deletes their **personal** SSH keys and GitHub tokens *before* removing the record, best-effort — a failed offboarding still deletes the user. `About.jsx` and `Users.jsx` now carry a one-line `PAGE_DESCRIPTION` + the shared `PageHelpButton` (the Users one-liner keeps the **Access** hand-off, because a record alone grants nothing), guarded by `src/pages/baseline-coverage-help-copy.test.js`. **Coverage is now a ratchet, not a checklist:** `help-routes.js` moves `/wizard/team` out of `ALLOWLIST` into `PAGE_ROUTES` (it renders the whole Agent Team Wizard and shows that page's help through the alias — classifying it as "no help of its own" was the one place the topology mis-described what a user sees), adds `TOPIC_PENDING` (page route → the ticket that owes it a topic) and the pure `classifyAppRoutes()`; `help-coverage.test.js` fails when a page route falls back to Overview **without** a named owner, and — since an assertion that has only ever run against a correct `App.jsx` could be silently vacuous — proves the guard fires by running the same classifier over a synthetic source (AC4). New `help-baseline.test.js` holds **every** topic in `help-topics/` (not a listed subset, so later-authored topics are covered automatically) to the §3.4 floor: sectioned body over a minimum length, ≥2 `askPrompts`, ≥1 `seeAlso` pointing at a known route, routes declared and uniquely owned — which is how "raise the thin pages to the baseline" is enforced instead of eyeballed. `page-help-view.test.js` finally **diffs the two route-collapse copies** (client `ALIASES`/`REDIRECTS`/`canonicalRoute` vs the server `help-routes.js`), the drift that would have made the panel and the chat grounding resolve different topics; `breadcrumbs.js` gains the missing `/about` and `/wizard/team` labels, which were reaching the assistant as raw pathnames.

**Context-help E17 — non-route help contexts (#1892, epic #1870 Phase 4).** Three help-worthy surfaces have no route at all, so a pathname could not address them: the **Settings** dialog (an overlay on top of whatever page you are on), the **first-run setup wizard** (rendered *instead of* the Shell, before any route exists) and the **individual steps inside a wizard** (sub-states of one route). The panel's context therefore widens from a bare pathname to **`{ route, overlay, step }`**, and a topic claims a **namespaced context key** in the same `routes:` front-matter list it claims a pathname in — `<kind>:<name>` for an overlay (`modal:settings`, `wizard:setup`) and `<base>#<state>` for a sub-state (`/team-setup#process`), neither of which can collide with a pathname. Resolution is **most-specific-first** (`overlay#step → overlay → route#step → canonicalRoute#step → route → canonicalRoute`, then the Overview): the overlay leads because it is literally on top of the page, the route candidates still follow it, and **closing the overlay just drops the first candidates** — which is what hands help back to the page underneath with no teardown logic (`helpContextKeys`/`resolveHelpContext` in `page-help-view.js`, mirrored server-side in `help-routes.js` + `registry.resolveHelpContext`/`topicForContext`, both copies diffed by the `page-help-view.test.js` parity block). Because an overlay or a step is known only to the component rendering it, those components **publish** it: the new **`useHelpSubject({ overlay, step, active })`** hook (`HelperChatContext.jsx`) pushes a subject onto a small stack while mounted and retracts it on unmount, and the **topmost** subject wins — so Settings opened on top of the Agent Team Wizard shows the dialog's help and closing it falls back to the step's, with no co-ordination between the two components (pure `upsertSubject`/`removeSubject`/`topSubject`). The panel's z-index is a decision, not a constant: `panelZIndex(context)` lifts it **above** the `Modal` layer (9999) only while an overlay subject is active, so help *for* a dialog is visible beside it while ordinary page help still sits below dialogs and side panels as before. **Surfaces wired:** `SettingsModal.jsx` publishes `modal:settings` + its active section (gated on `open`) and carries its own `PageHelpButton`, answered by the net-new **`help-topics/settings.md`** — the first topic keyed on a non-route context, and the place that finally states that the **Assist model is not the model your agents run on**, that subscription (`oauth-token`) providers are deliberately not offered for console assist calls, and which of the four preferences live in the browser vs on your user record. `TeamSetup.jsx` publishes its current step from a new `STEP_HELP_IDS` list (index-aligned with `STEPS`; the ids are the contract, the labels are display copy) and gained a `PageHelpButton`. `SetupWizard.jsx` cannot use the panel at all — it replaces the Shell, so `openHelpFor()` would flip state behind a blank screen — and instead renders an inline collapsible `SetupStepHelp` over the same resolved topic; its three steps reuse the topics the corresponding pages already own through the CONTEXT_KEYS **`alias`** field (`wizard:setup#llm` → `/models`, `#ssh-key` → `/ssh-keys`, `#gh-token` → `/github-tokens`), so the first-run flow is not help-less and there is **no second copy of the prose** — E10's multi-route `credentials` topic answers the two credential steps, E8's `models` topic the provider step, and a step whose aliased topic were ever missing renders no help box rather than an empty one. Step keys are **optional coverage by design**: while no topic claims `/team-setup#process`, the resolver falls through to the wizard's own route topic, so a step is never emptier than the page — which is how #1888 can make a step's help step-specific later without touching this wiring. Guarded by two new tests: `help-context.test.js` (key well-formedness, every alias points at a real page route, no topic may claim an unregistered key, resolution/fallback over both the shipped topics and fixtures, each assertion re-run over a synthetic input so it cannot be vacuous) and `src/pages/non-route-help-wiring.test.js` (comment-stripped source reading: every step id a wizard renders has a registered context key, `STEP_HELP_IDS` stays index-aligned with `STEPS`, `SettingsModal` still gates its subject on `open`, and `SetupWizard` never calls `openHelpFor`). `/quickstart` is recorded in `SINGLE_STEP_PAGES` as a one-page form rather than a stepped wizard, so the absence of step keys there reads as a decision.

**Context-help #1996 — the small-page collection (epic #1981).** One ticket, six topics, because six pages each have only one or two surfaces and do not warrant a per-surface ticket apiece. Every topic is deepened; a deep-link is added **only where a real second surface exists**, which is the ticket's own bar. **Settings** (`modal:settings`) gets the cleanest wiring in the epic: its "?" already lives *inside* the section pane and therefore re-renders per section, so `section={SETTINGS_HELP_SECTIONS[active.id]}` delivers "one ? per section" with **no new affordance at all** — the registry is keyed on the dialog's own section ids and `settings-help-extraction.test.js` asserts `SETTINGS_SECTIONS` and the registry hold exactly the same key set, so a fourth section cannot ship chapter-less. **Maintenance Mode** goes from two "?" to three, split by the page's two mutually-exclusive *states* rather than by topic: the activate form deep-links to *Activating it and the grace period* and the while-active card to *While active* — until now the mode being **on** was the one state of that page with no contextual affordance, because the form that had one is not rendered then. **Access** and **Users** put a "?" inside their `SidePanel`s, and Users additionally inside its delete `ConfirmDialog`; **Agent Templates** puts two inside its editor (the *Invocation mode* and *Advanced JSON* fields). **About** gets no deep-link — the ticket says that is the correct outcome for a one-surface page, not a gap. Four new overlay keys (`modal:team-manage`, `modal:user-edit`, `modal:user-delete`, `modal:agent-template`) are registered in all three places; `modal:user-delete` is the first on a `ConfirmDialog` (the shared Modal at **9999**, versus a SidePanel's 60/61), which is exactly why it needs the `panelZIndex` lift most — without it the help would open *behind* the irreversible decision it exists to inform. Because a hook cannot live in a `message={<>…</>}` prop expression, the Users delete body is extracted into its own `DeleteUserMessage` component; `ConfirmDialog` renders `null` while closed, so the subject mounts and unmounts with the dialog and needs no `active:` flag. `RoleTemplates.jsx` had **no** `useHelpSubject` at all — harmless only while its panel hosted no "?". **Three factual corrections the code forced, all verified by reading it:** the maintenance topic claimed *"Starting a new session is blocked too"* — **Reset Session and Clear History are not maintenance-gated**, only the composer and the quick-reply buttons are; the grace countdown is **never sent to the agent containers at all** (only the boolean reaches them), which is the mechanical reason it cannot be an enforcement timer; and the role-templates topic claimed pattern roles are *"kept out of the wizard's ordinary role offering"* when **nothing anywhere filters on `category`** — the select's own option label still promises exactly that, so the label is flagged as stale rather than reworded (Scope-out protects it). Each correction is pinned as a `concepts` anchor so a reword cannot quietly restore it. **This is the first #1981 child to move the worst-case grounding number to a *different context*.** `/flows` is untouched at **259 301** — none of the six topics is in that neighbourhood, which was the whole expectation — but `access-users.md` is named by the **Overview** topic's `seeAlso`, and on main the no-route fallback (Overview + its seven neighbours) stood at 259 283 against `/flows`' 259 301: **18 characters apart**. Widening Access in that fold was enough to overtake it, so the worst case is now the no-route context at **262 568 of 340 000** (77.2% used). The durable lesson is not about Access: once two contexts converge, the worst case stops being a property of the busiest page, and a child that checks only `/flows` will report "unmoved" while having moved it — sweep with `measureWorstCaseSystemPrompt()` and read the `context` it returns, not just the length. Per-context growth, measured against an `origin/main` worktree rather than carried forward: `modal:settings` +6 116, `/teams` +6 259, `/users` +6 259, `/about` +4 696, `/maintenance-mode` +3 530, `/role-templates` +2 846. Guarded by five new/extended page-side extraction tests plus `chapters` lists on the five deep-linked topics.

## Key Files

| File | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `docker-compose.yml` | All four services + volumes |
| `.env` | Secrets: Matrix tokens, LLM proxy URL + auth token (gitignored) |
| `Makefile` | Build/start targets (`up`, `down`, `logs`, `build`, `ps`, `seed-*`) |
| `orchestrator/orchestrator.js` | Backend: agent lifecycle, container spawning, env-aware seeding, internal HTTP API |
| `orchestrator/toolchain-registry.js` | Pure decision core for the per-Working-Environment agent toolchain (#1940), byte-identical at `admin-console/api/toolchain-registry.js`: `validateToolchain`/`normalizeRegistry` (the registry entry shape — the only place a client-supplied image reference is validated), `resolveAgentImage` (env → toolchain → image, with the `AGENT_IMAGE` fallback and the named refusals for an unregistered toolchain / a runtime mismatch), `missingImageMessage` (the AC7 wording both spawn adapters render). #1941 adds the SOURCE side: `validateToolchainManifest`/`toolchainFromManifest` (the `toolchains/<id>/toolchain.json` schema, projected onto the registry entry the same validator governs) and `resolveToolchainImage` (deployment prefix + release tag joined onto the manifest's bare tag at spawn time) |
| `toolchains/` | Toolchain variant image sources (#1941): `toolchains/<id>/` = `toolchain.json` + `Dockerfile` (`FROM as-agent:latest`, add-only) + the variant's `toolchain.env`/`cache-env.sh`; `toolchains/_shared/` holds the stack-agnostic `aee-toolchain` bootstrap (pin resolution + workspace-cached runtime), `base-contract-check.sh` (the CI/release gate that makes "derive, never fork" enforceable) and `verify-toolchain.sh` (the AC5-AC9/AC12 demonstration harness). Built by `make toolchain-images`, seeded by `make seed-toolchains` |
| `orchestrator/spawn-container.js` | Spawns/kills the per-agent Podman container over libpod; the agent image arrives through the seam (`spawnContainer({ image })`, #1940) and `DEFAULT_AGENT_IMAGE` is exported as the installation-wide default only; network + FS SubPath + dedicated-volume + optional host-folder isolation (one or more folders via `contextMount`/`contextMounts`, read-only or read-write per folder — `buildContextMountSpecs`). `removePerAgentNetwork(id, { graceful })` / `ensureAgentContainerGone(id, { graceful })` take a per-role flag (#1791): `graceful` keeps the SIGTERM→commit→autoremove poll for a bp-instance; non-BP agents force-remove immediately |
| `orchestrator/team-conversion.js` | Pure decision core for converting one standalone agent into a team of one, in place (#2052): `assessConversionEligibility` (one closed set of refusal codes, shared by the menu predicate, the API and the orchestrator's own re-check), `deriveTeamId`/`deriveTeamDisplayName` (`<agent-id>-team` / `<Display Name> Team`) and `planTeamConversion` — the new `role: 'team'` record, the member patch (incl. the legacy *exclusive* prompt shape and an explicit `roleId`), the fields to unset, which tenant-pool skills need copying into the squad pool, and which `team`-granularity plugin scope ids flip. Executed by `Orchestrator.convertAgentToTeam()` behind `POST /internal/agents/:id/convert-to-team` |
| `orchestrator/agent-repositories.js` | Pure decision core for **agent-scoped repositories** (#2067) — a repository attached to ONE agent, outside its Working Environment: `normalizeAgentRepositories` (the stored shape — the environment card's four fields and nothing else, so no per-repository credential can enter through this door), `resolveAgentRepositories` (the merged environment+agent view every surface reads, each entry carrying its `dirName`, its `scope` and whether the environment has since `shadowed` it), `hasAnyRepository` (the widened Git-section gate), `credentialRefusal` (the AC19 pre-clone refusal, naming which credential was tried), `planAgentRepoAttach` (the three directory-name collisions told apart, ordered so the actionable fix comes before the credential problem), `planAgentRepoDetach` (refuses an inherited repo; keeps the working copy when the environment now declares the same directory), `removalFacts` (the AC9 confirmation's facts — uncommitted changes and unpushed commits by count, or an explicit "clean") and `attachAvailability` (team record / stub / no-rights / busy, in that precedence). Re-exports `repoDirName` from `repo-sync.js` rather than re-deriving the shared clone convention. Executed by `agentRepositoriesView`/`attachAgentRepository`/`detachAgentRepository` in `orchestrator.js` |
| `orchestrator/agent-delete-plan.js` | Pure fast/background-delete decisions (#1791): `isGracefulDeleteRole(role)` (only `bp-instance` keeps the graceful container-exit window) + `TeardownRegistry` (in-memory "teardown in progress" guard the delete fast phase arms and the background phase clears, so a same-id re-create can't collide with an in-flight teardown whose config.json is already gone — #1525). `DELETE /internal/agents/:id` returns after a fast phase (stop → plugin reap → delete config), reaping network/volume/Matrix in the background. #1960 adds `cascadeChildIds(parentId, parentCfg, configs)` — which agents go with a deleted parent (team → every member, materialized and stub; an unreadable parent config → the same set, so an interrupted delete is repeatable; any other role → none) |
| `orchestrator/agent-orphans.js` | Pure orphan detection for the #1960 one-off cleanup: `classifyOrphan`/`planOrphanCleanup` (a `team-member` whose parent cannot be resolved — `no-parent-id` / `parent-missing` / `parent-not-a-team`, kept apart because they are three different mistakes), `formatOrphanLine` (the dry-run listing, also written to the orchestrator log before the first deletion). Deliberately narrow: only `team-member` is classified, and the record reports `materialized` without ever echoing the Matrix token |
| `orchestrator/teams-store.js` | Source of truth for the human **Team** data model (pure factory) |
| `orchestrator/team-admin-succession.js` | The D4 last-admin rule (#2081, concept §2.3), pure over a membership array: `adminIdsOf`/`isSoleAdmin`/`soleAdminTeamIds`, `planAdminHandover` (removal **and** demotion — refuse unless a successor already in that team is named, else return the promotion to apply in the SAME write) and `planUserDeletion` (refuse while the user is the last admin of any team, naming every one of them). Enforced by `teams-store.js` rather than by §2.3's three api.js routes, so the orchestrator's own `/internal/admin/*` writers are covered too |
| `orchestrator/default-team-adoption.js` | Pure decisions of the Default-Team adoption pass (#1457): `needsAdoption` (`teamId === undefined` only — a personal credential's explicit `teamId: null` must never be adopted into a team), `planAdoption`/`adoptionCount` (which records still need the stamp, and an honest changed-record count), `planBootstrapMembership` (the bootstrap admin's Default-Team `team-admin` membership, planned only when no membership for the pair exists in *any* role, so an upsert cannot re-promote an admin a human demoted). `bootstrapTeams`/`backfillResourceOwnership` are not exported from `orchestrator.js`, so every judgement worth asserting lives here |
| `admin-console/api/auth-mode.js` | The `AUTH_ENABLED` single-user ⇄ multi-user switch (#1457): `resolveAuthEnabled`/`describeAuthMode` (off by default — this is a migration flag, and an unrecognised value is off *and* reported), `authModeLogLine`, `singleUserElevationWarning` (the one footgun: single-user mode on a stack with named users grants each of them full visibility). Threaded into `computeScope`/`describeAuthz` at the single `getScope` chokepoint in api.js. Note the deliberate asymmetry — the env flag defaults **off**, `computeScope`'s `authEnabled` parameter defaults **on**, so a missing argument can never silently grant `all: true` |
| `orchestrator/human-matrix-identity.js` | Pure localpart grammar for the per-human Matrix identity (#1458, epic #2076 P1): `humanMatrixLocalpart`/`humanMatrixUserId` (deterministic `@u-<16 hex of sha256(subject)>:local`, derived from the **stable subject** so an off-boarded-then-re-onboarded human recovers the same account, normalised exactly as `teams-store.js` `getUserBySubject` compares a subject), `isHumanMatrixUserId`/`isServiceMatrixUserId` (the strict human-vs-service partition docs/multi-user-concept.md §2 requires for the audit trail) and `isReservedHumanLocalpart` — the reservation `ensureMatrixUser` enforces at the **single choke point** every Matrix account in the stack is created through, deliberately as narrow as the minted shape so `u-payments` stays a legal agent id. A blank subject throws rather than minting one shared account |
| `orchestrator/human-matrix-store.js` | Server-side store for a human's Matrix access token (#1458): `<root>/users/matrix/<userId>.json`, mode 0600 / dir 0700, atomic write, UUID-validated path, fail-soft reads. Deliberately **not** a field on the user record: `teamsStore.sanitizeUser()` is a convention applied at the store boundary, and `users/index.json` is additionally read directly by the admin-console (`getScope`, `describeAuthz`, the personal-credential owner check), so the split makes the leak structurally impossible instead of review-dependent; the record carries only the non-secret `matrixUserId` (`teamsStore.setUserMatrixIdentity`). Wired by `ensureHumanMatrixIdentity`/`deprovisionHumanMatrixIdentity` in orchestrator.js off `POST`/`DELETE /internal/admin/users` — see docs/multi-user-concept.md Amendment 1 for why provisioning hangs off record creation rather than first login |
| `orchestrator/ownership-migration.js` | Pure decision core for the ownership data model + the D6 migration (#2077): `resolveScopeOwner` (the §8.1 owner rule — first `team-admin` by earliest `createdAt`, else first member, else unset), `planOwnerBackfill` (which configs still need an `ownerId`, and the ones whose scope has no member to inherit from — reported, never fabricated), `planMembershipRepoint` (the additive *(user ↔ Agent Team)* fan-out, deduplicated on `(userId, teamId)` with `team-admin` winning a tie), `ownableRole` (D8 — only `team` and `agent`). The fs half is `migrateOwnershipModel()` in orchestrator.js |
| `orchestrator/resource-tier.js` | The ONE definition of the `personal` / `team` / `system` (shared) ownership triple every tiered resource carries (#2083, epic #2076 P5), byte-identical at `admin-console/api/resource-tier.js` and two-sided-guarded: `normalizeTier` (a record with no `scope` reads as team-tier, so every environment/MCP server/credential on disk keeps working; no `|| 'default'`), `ownsPersonalTier` (the no-`scope.all` privacy rule, shared by all three kinds), `isSharedTier` (what the boot adoption pass keys on so a shared record's deliberate `teamId: null` is never adopted away), `tierTeamIds`/`tierUsableByTeam`, `tierOwnershipFields` (the persisted triple, written explicitly so a client cannot smuggle a field into the shape the predicates read) and `isRetier` — the predicate that tells a self-service content edit from the System-Admin-only act of deciding **which** teams reach a record. The global model-provider rule (#2136 `global: true`) deliberately does **not** live here — `isGlobalProvider` is in `admin-console/api/llm-endpoint-global.js`, applied at the provider call sites only, because this shared module also shapes SSH keys and GitHub tokens where a `global` flag must stay inert (finding 2; a comment here guards against re-introducing it) |
| `admin-console/api/llm-endpoint-global.js` | The single home for the model-provider `global: true` rule (#2136). **Reachability:** `isGlobalProvider(ep)` (`global === true && scope === 'system' && authType !== 'oauth-token'`, re-read on every call) — composed at the provider call sites (api.js `providerVisible`/`providerUsableByTeam`, scope.js `assistSelectableEndpoint`), never inside the generic scope/tier predicates that also gate secrets (finding 2). **Publish gate:** `resolveGlobalFlag({ wantGlobal, currentGlobal, scopeAll, isOauth })` → the flag to persist, or a refusal — 403 `global-system-admin-only` for a non-`scope.all` caller changing the flag, 400 `global-oauth-unsupported` for a subscription seat; a no-op (flag unchanged) is never gated, so a Team Admin editing an already-global provider's models is not refused; only a literal boolean `true` publishes (stray value fails closed), and a kept flag is cleared on the unholdable oauth transition (finding 1). Called by both `POST` and `PUT /api/llm-endpoints` in api.js |
| `orchestrator/agent-team.js` | Pure access-boundary accessor (#2078, epic #2076 P2), byte-identical at `admin-console/api/agent-team.js` and two-sided-guarded by `agent-team.test.js`: `resolveAgentTeam(cfg)` — a `role: 'team'` is its own boundary, a `role: 'team-member'` is bounded by `parentTeamId`, everything else (a standalone `role: 'agent'`, and any config too broken to name a team) resolves to `null` so ownership decides (D8) — plus `isOwnerScopedAgent`. No `loadAgentConfig` injection, no parent read, no `'default'` fallback: fail-closed on every unexpected shape. **Not** `resolveAgentTenant`, which survives as the credential/provider axis until P5 |
| `orchestrator/skill-grants.js` | Source of truth for the team-owned skill grant (`effectiveSkills`) |
| `orchestrator/skill-commands.js` | Pure core for custom slash-command provisioning (#1796): parse a `commands/*.md` command's `description`, namespace + project it into `.claude/commands/<skill>/`, build the `seeded-commands.json` manifest; `scanProvisionedCommands` (#1797) enumerates the actual `.claude/commands/` tree (workspace = source of truth, so hand-added commands surface too) for the `GET /api/agents/:id/commands` list |
| `orchestrator/credential-resolution.js` | Pure personal-credential resolution decision core (#1569 Phase 3): `resolveAgentOwnerId` (owner from persisted config; team-member resolves to parent team's owner), `findPersonalCredential` (owner's usable personal index entry), `findPersonalCredentialState` (#2077 — the same lookup reporting whether the owner has a credential of this kind AT ALL, separately from whether it is usable), `resolveCredential` (personal-preference + refuse-on-missing for attributed writes; shared-eligible for reads/polling; no-op when no `ownerId`). **#2077 moved the switch**: it was owner *presence*, which was only safe while nothing populated `ownerId` — once the D6 backfill stamps every agent, presence would have put the whole installation on the refusal path (personal credentials are opt-in, so no owner has one) and no agent could open a PR. It is now owner *participation*: an owner with no personal credential of a kind takes the shared credential exactly as today, an owner whose credential is present-but-unusable is still refused. **#2138 adds a live, reachable refusal that does not wait on the scope probe**: `findPersonalCredentialState` takes an optional `buildLiveUserGate` result (`{known,isLive}`, `known:false` for an empty/unreadable user index → pre-#2138 behaviour, so an unreadable index never refuses every write at once), and an owner who is **no longer a live user** (a deleted account whose personal PAT is still on disk) is reported present-but-not-usable, routing an attributed write into `resolveCredential`'s refusal with the distinct `buildOrphanRefusal` (`owner-not-a-live-user`, telling the operator to finish the offboarding and revoke at GitHub — not the `personal-credential-required` message, which cannot apply to a deleted account); reads/polling still resolve shared. `selectOrphanedOwnerIds` selects the deleted owners still holding a personal credential or a running agent for the boot-time `Orchestrator.reconcileOrphanedOffboardings()` replay (idempotent, `known:false` gate → empty selection, never a whole-installation drain). The present-but-unusable (scope) refusal stays unreachable until an `isUsable` probe lands. `selectAgentIdsForOwner` (#1570 Phase 4: owner-scoped respawn selection — the agents whose resolved credential is a given human's personal one), `selectPersonalCredentialIds` (#1571 Phase 5: offboarding — the ids of a departing human's personal credentials to auto-delete; a team/system entry is never selected) |
| `orchestrator/usage-attribution.js` | Pure attribution decisions for a token-usage record (#2137, follows #2136): `resolveUsageAttribution({requestedBy, users, ownerId})` → `attribution: {userId, kind}` (`requesting-user` → `agent-owner` → `unattributed`, strongest signal first) and `providerTierOf(index, providerId)` → `global`/`system`/`team`, or `null` for a provider no longer registered. The **agent** stamps only `requestedBy` in `build_usage_record` (the chat sender, `null` for an autonomous run — the one thing the control plane cannot see); the **orchestrator**'s `enrichUsageRecord` reads the users index + `resolveAgentOwnerId`, writes both fields **unconditionally** (unlike the null-guarded `teamId`/`roleId`/`provider` backfills — the agent can resolve neither, so a body value is self-asserted) and **drops** the raw Matrix id (the usage store has no retention by design). Read-side `user`/`providerTier` groupings live in `usage-summary.js`; `AGENT_GROUP_BY` in `usage-query.js` withholds `user` from the agent-facing `usage` surface (System-Admin-only, #801) while offering `providerTier` |
| `orchestrator/prompt-assembly.js` | Pure single source for system-prompt composition (ordering, gating, memory hint) |
| `orchestrator/context-browse.js` | Pure guard/program/parsing for browsing the `/context` mount |
| `orchestrator/context-write.js` | Pure guard/program/parsing for writing (delete/move/upload) into a read-write `/context` mount |
| `orchestrator/tar-single.js` | Dependency-free ustar builder for the archive-endpoint upload path |
| `orchestrator/container-exec.js` | One-shot command execution + archive PUT inside a running agent container over libpod |
| `orchestrator/github-poller.js` | GitHub Events API polling: match, throttle, burst digest, fan-out |
| `orchestrator/flow-anomaly-detector.js` | Pure deterministic stall classifier: derives flow's expected label state machine from ports; classifies four anomaly classes with age |
| `orchestrator/admission-control.js` | Pure decision core for the per-flow WIP limit (#2114): `entryTicketRef` (which ticket a pending entry is a dispatch for — a PR event keys on the issue it closes, so a PR and its issue occupy ONE lane slot), `evaluate`/`admit` (the five named reasons `no-limit` / `not-a-flow-dispatch` / `same-ticket` / `capacity` / `at-limit`, and the lane transition), `activeFlowLabels` (wake + `:wip` claim + human-gate labels, never `:completed`), `reconcileInFlight` (release from GitHub truth, fail-closed on an unswept repo, with a short grace window covering an internal-channel emit's asynchronous label mirror), `promoteHeld` (one FIFO pass that continues past an entry it cannot admit, so a same-ticket hand-off is never stuck behind an unrelated new ticket) and the lane hygiene helpers. No fs, no network, no clock of its own — `now` is always injected |
| `orchestrator/ticket-reconciler.js` | Pure decision core for the ticket ledger (#2115): `buildTicketLedger` (the per-team ledger over a GitHub snapshot + the flow model), `buildTicketModel`/`entryWakeLabels`/`terminalConsumerIds`/`labelDepths` (graph position, derived — no label name appears in this file), `gateWakeEdges`/`mergedConsumers` (the gate→consumer edges and gate-only members `buildFlowModel` discards), `deriveState` (the nine-state lifecycle, PR evidence outranking labels), the seven one-per-code blocker rules (`checkSuperseded`/`checkStaleBase`/`checkConflict`/`checkCiRed`/`checkApprovedNotMerged`/`checkClaimedDoneNotMerged`/`checkOrphaned`, each separately exported so one fixture exercises exactly one), `parseEpicId` (the prose forms this team writes; `null` rather than a guess) and `recurrenceFor` (sightings from the `anomaly-history` substrate). No fs, no network, no `Date.now()` — `now` is always injected, and a guard asserts all three |
| `orchestrator/ticket-reconcile-sweep.js` | Pure GitHub-JSON → reconciler shaping (#2115), the half `flow-anomaly-sweep.js` deliberately drops: `issuesClosedByPr` (the STRICT linkage direction — closing keywords only, since a false link merges two tickets into one row), `checkRollup` (failure only on a CONCLUDED negative conclusion; `none` kept apart from `success` so "no CI" is never read as "green"), `reviewDecision` (latest non-COMMENTED review per reviewer, one CHANGES_REQUESTED outranking any approvals), `addedFilesOf`/`baseFileIndex` (the `superseded` inputs, with `complete: !truncated` as the load-bearing field), `issueToTicketObject`/`prToTicketObject` (incl. `MERGE_STATE_MAP`, REST `mergeable_state` → the GraphQL-style vocabulary §4.1 is written against, and the three-valued `mergeable`), `linkIssuesToPrs` (the loose direction, over RAW PR items so the head-branch half survives) and `resolveTicketThresholds` |
| `admin-console/src/pages/process/flow-control-view.js` | Pure view decisions for the Flow Control board (#2116): `LIFECYCLE_STAGES`/`TICKET_STATES` (the swimlane columns, mirrored from the reconciler and drift-guarded against its source), `BLOCKERS` (badge label + blurb per reason code; severity is NOT mirrored, each ticket carrying its own), `ticketCard` (one ledger row → one render-ready card — and the module `ticket.labels` is demonstrably never read, which is how AC2 is proven), `deepLinks`/`synthesizeUrl` (issue + PR targets, preferring what GitHub returned and degrading to plain text rather than a broken link — the `GithubEventLog.jsx` `issueUrl` shape), `groupIntoLanes` (empty lanes kept, unknown states into a trailing `Other`, the ledger's own most-actionable-first order preserved), `bucketFor`/`epicRollups`/`rollupSummary` (the four exhaustive cross-lane buckets) and `boardSummary` (header counts + the maintenance / no-repo / partial-sweep banner conditions). `FlowControl.jsx` is the thin renderer; JSX stays uncovered per #651 |
| `orchestrator/flow-anomaly-sweep.js` | GitHub/session-log → detector objects adapter; I/O helpers + per-team threshold merge |
| `orchestrator/setup.sh` | Register the admin Matrix user, print the access token |
| `agent-image/` | Per-agent image `as-agent:latest`; entrypoint `scripts/agent-entrypoint.py` is the live runtime. `agent-image/CLAUDE.md` is baked to `/opt/ace/CLAUDE.md` as the image's reference copy of the container's own conventions (git/`gh-as-agent`, `aee-artifact`, `aee-emit`, `knowledge`, `usage`, `ticket.json`) — **not** the instructions an agent reads: those are assembled per agent by `prompt-assembly.js` into `/workspace/agents/<id>/CLAUDE.md`, and no runtime path reads the baked file (its one consumer is the toolchain `base-contract-check.sh` existence probe). It was rewritten off BriefingPack framing in #1969 (epic #1961) and is guarded by `scripts/test_bp_removal_guard.py` |
| `admin-console/api/api.js` | Express API: agents, environments, ssh-keys, skills, models, mcp, `POST /api/teams`, agent-templates |
| `admin-console/api/team-residue.js` | Pure residue classifier + routing decision for team-create (`classifyMemberResidue` / `decideTeamCreate`); shared by both `POST /api/teams` handlers so their collision/orphan routing can't drift (#1525) |
| `admin-console/api/creator-membership.js` | The two decisions self-service team creation adds (#2081): `planCreatorMembership` — write the creator's `team-admin` grant only when THIS request minted the team (an upsert on an extend would silently promote a Team Member who adds a role), and never for a caller with no user record, exactly like `ownerFor`; and `creatorEnvironmentOwnership` — the team-tier stamp for the AUTO-created Working Environment, which previously carried no tier at all and was therefore invisible to its own team. Both handlers of `POST /api/teams` call it; `self-service-team-creation.test.js` is the source-reading guard that they do |
| `admin-console/api/team-repo-resolve.js` | Pure repo helpers for team materialization (#1623): `reposFromEnv` (normalize legacy + modern shapes), `primaryRepo`, `isRepoLessEnv`, `repoVars` (builds `{{repoFullName}}`/`{{repoOwner}}`/`{{repoName}}`/`{{repoUrl}}`, empty strings for repo-less) |
| `admin-console/api/team-provider-check.js` | Pure decision core for blocking team creation on a platform with zero registered model providers (#2179, C2 of epic #2166): `validateTeamProviderAvailability(endpointCount)` returns `{error, code: 'no_llm_endpoint'}` or `null`, shared by both `POST /api/teams` handlers so the message/rule can't drift; `launch-team.js`'s `validateLaunch`/`launchBlockReason` mirror the same refusal client-side via an additive `hasProvider` option |
| `admin-console/api/extract-template.js` | Pure mapping core for Save as template (#1509): `buildExtractedTemplate` (whitelist builder — runtime/identity + team-composition fields can never leak), `sanitizeTemplateTrigger`, `suggestTemplateId`, `inferTemplateCategory`, `inferPatternRole` |
| `admin-console/api/quickstart-llm.js` | Pure decision core for the LLM-only Single Agent entry path (#1624): `validateQuickstartLlmInput` (no-provider / no-name / binding errors), `llmEnvId`/`llmAgentId` (slug + dedup), `quickstartLlmSystemPrompt`; `POST /api/quickstart/llm` in api.js is the thin adapter |
| `admin-console/api/llm-health.js` | Pure Model Provider health-poller logic (#1843): `backoffMs` (exponential), `applyProbeResult` (fold a probe into a persisted record), `nextDueMs`/`isDue`/`selectDueEndpointIds` (which providers to probe this tick), `isStale` (AC5 on-demand re-probe), `healthView` (client view — honest `unknown`). The `setInterval` loop + fs (`health.json`) + `probeAndRecordHealth` live in api.js |
| `admin-console/src/pages/provider-health-view.js` | Pure client view for Model Provider health (#1843): `healthCellModel`/`healthCellDetail` (Model Providers table cell tone/label) + `providerWarningText` (Agent-Chat non-blocking red-provider banner text) |
| `admin-console/api/process-template.js` | Pure builders/validators for process-template bundles |
| `admin-console/api/draft-access.js` | Pure access model for process drafts (#2068): `canAccessDraft`/`authorizeDraft`/`filterVisibleDrafts` (the ONE predicate, plus the AC7 indistinguishable-404), `newDraftAccess` (the create-time stamp, resolved from the caller), `stripAccessFields`/`carryAccessFields` (the server-owned pair on every write), `addAccess`/`removeAccess` (share/unshare, with the never-empty refusal), `stripUserFromDraft` (the user-deletion cascade, which MAY empty a set), `migrateDraftAccess`/`firstSystemAdminId` (the upgrade-day backfill), `draftAccessView` (the list's private/shared + creator name, no raw id) and `userDirectory`/`resolveAccessList` (the `{id, displayName, email}` projection the Share picker and `GET /api/users/directory` share). Contains no reference to `createdBy` in any decision — that is DD2, and a guard test enforces it |
| `admin-console/api/work-plane-access.js` | Pure work-plane access rule (#1459 §2 — GOV-ACCESS-3 / GOV-PLATFORM-4): `WORK_PLANE_AGENT_PREFIXES` + `WORK_PLANE_ROOM_PREFIX` (the express `app.use` mount list — *all* of api.js's `:agentId` family plus the Matrix room routes, each entry ending on a complete path segment so `/context` cannot swallow `/context-mount` nor `/session` swallow `/sessions`), `authorizeWorkPlane` (the ONE predicate: no scope or no resolvable agent or not `visibleAgent` → the same 404, so "not yours" is indistinguishable from "not there") and `createWorkPlaneGates` (the two express middlewares, built from injected lookups — which is what makes them reachable from a real-HTTP test, since api.js boots two listeners on require, #651). Read and write deliberately share one predicate: with the two roles that exist today `visibleAgent` already *is* the membership test, so a separate write branch would be unreachable — `work-plane-access.test.js` pins the equivalence over the user × agent × method cross-product and additionally re-derives the mount list from api.js's own route table, so a work-plane route added later without a gate fails there rather than shipping open |
| `admin-console/src/pages/process/draft-share-view.js` | Pure client decisions for draft sharing (#2068): `draftSubtitle`/`draftAccessTitle` (the rail row's private/shared line), `accessRows`/`shareCandidates`/`addControlState` (the Share panel), `removeState` (the never-empty hint, stated before the server refuses), `deleteImpactNotice` (the confirmation naming who else loses it) and `isDraftGone`/`draftWriteError` (a draft deleted or unshared under an open editor reads as a sentence, not a 404) |
| `admin-console/src/pages/agent-chat/team-share-view.js` | Pure client decisions for the Agent Team **Share** panel (#2082): `canManageMembers` (the client mirror of `canManageTeam` — the whole panel's gate, deliberately not the fetch), `directoryIndex`/`shareCandidates`/`filterCandidates`/`candidateLabel` (the D2/D11 name+email projection, re-asserted client-side, minus the people already in), `memberRows` (a membership whose user record is gone is SHOWN, not dropped — it still holds access and is still a reason a hand-over is demanded), `handoverRequirement`/`removeState`/`roleChangeState` (D4: sole admin ⇒ pick a successor from THIS team; sole admin *and* sole member ⇒ a stated dead end, not an empty picker; a promotion is never gated), `setRoleRequest`/`removeRequest` (**`POST` carries `teamId` in the body, `DELETE` in the query** — where each route's own gate reads it) and `membershipWriteError` (the store's 409 message IS the instruction and is relayed verbatim; only a 403 is reworded, because "Forbidden" does not say that your rights changed under an open panel) |
| `admin-console/api/helper-chat/help-registry.js` + `help-routes.js` | Context-help foundation (#1872): topic front-matter parser + route→topic resolution (`topicForRoute`, dynamic/alias/redirect-aware) + corpus fold + `baselineTopic` template; `help-routes.js` is the App.jsx route topology (`PAGE_ROUTES`/`ALLOWLIST`/`TOPIC_PENDING` + the pure `classifyAppRoutes()`) the `help-coverage.test.js` CI guard enforces — a page route may only fall back to Overview when `TOPIC_PENDING` names the ticket that owes it a topic (#1891), and `help-baseline.test.js` holds every shipped topic to the §3.4 floor. Since E17 (#1892) it also holds `CONTEXT_KEYS` — the non-route help contexts (`modal:settings`, `wizard:setup#<step>`, `/team-setup#<step>`, each with an optional `alias` to the page route whose topic answers it) — plus `helpContextKeys`/`resolveHelpContext`/`topicForContext`, guarded by `help-context.test.js`. Topics live in `help-topics/*.md`, served by `GET /api/helper/page-help` and folded into the chat corpus (single source, no parity test). #2006 adds the **route-scoped fold** the corpus uses: `contextTopics` (resolved topic + `seeAlso` neighbours, unresolvable neighbours skipped, Overview fallback), `foldTopicsForContext`, and the `groundingContexts`/`worstCaseFold` pair the budget guard sweeps |
| `admin-console/src/context/page-help-view.js` | Pure client core for the "This page" help face (#1872): route→topic resolution, Overview fallback (`pageHelpState`), `askPrompt`→Ask hand-off shaping (mirrors `help-registry.js`; the JSX face lands in C2 #1873). E17 (#1892) adds the non-route half: the `CONTEXT_KEYS` mirror, `helpContextKeys`/`resolveHelpContext` (overlay → step → route, most-specific-first), the `upsertSubject`/`removeSubject`/`topSubject` subject stack behind `useHelpSubject()`, `contextLabel` and `panelZIndex` (lift above the Modal layer only while an overlay is open) |
| `admin-console/api/plugin-manifest.js` | Pure validator/normalizer for a `pluginVersion: 1` plugin manifest (#1517); extended in #1798 to support command-only plugins (`commands` + `fetch` fields, no sidecar/cli) |
| `orchestrator/plugin-scope-token.js` | HMAC `{pluginId, scopeId}` scope tokens (generalised `podman-token.js`) |
| `orchestrator/plugin-scope.js` | `resolveScopeId` (team/agent granularity) + resource ownership-label helpers |
| `orchestrator/plugin-sidecar.js` | Pure sidecar lifecycle + grant-driven network attachment planners |
| `orchestrator/plugin-grants.js` | Source of truth for the team-owned plugin grant + per-grant scope granularity (`effectivePlugins`, sibling of `skill-grants.js`) (#1518) |
| `orchestrator/plugin-sync.js` | Pure workspace seed/sync planner: CLI + 0600 token config + network on grant, removed on revoke; granularity-flip re-mint (#1518) |
| `orchestrator/plugin-prompt.js` | Pure gated "Plugin Capabilities" CLAUDE.md section from the resolved grant (#1518) |
| `orchestrator/plugin-audit.js` | Pure broker audit-line shape + retention (#1518) |
| `orchestrator/plugin-registry.js` | Registry reader over `/workspace/plugins/` — re-validates on read, resolves CLI entrypoints with containment, owns the signing secret (#1547) |
| `orchestrator/plugin-runtime.js` | Impure sidecar adapter: libpod create/start/remove, capability→privilege translation, grant-driven network attachment, broker teardown (#1547); on Kubernetes delegates sidecar lifecycle to `spawn-kubernetes-plugin.js` (#1565) |
| `orchestrator/spawn-kubernetes-plugin.js` | Kubernetes plugin sidecar runtime (#1565): Deployment + Service + state PVC per installed plugin over raw-HTTPS `k8sRequest`; UI-only reachability needs no NetworkPolicy (broker-plugin NetworkPolicy split out) |
| `orchestrator/plugin-manifest.js` | Byte-identical mirror of the admin-console manifest validator (two-sided guard) |
| `broker-sdk/broker-config.js` | The orchestrator↔broker env contract (`AEE_PLUGIN_*`) + per-scope `quotaExceeded` (#1547) |
| `admin-console/src/pages/plugin-registry-view.js` | Pure registry-page decisions: capability wording, quota summary, install-button state, broker status (#1547); also exposes `platforms` + `defaultScopeGranularity` on the row for the team-assignment control (#1691) |
| `admin-console/src/pages/plugin-team-assign-view.js` + `src/components/PluginTeamAssign.jsx` | Pure decisions + thin renderer for the Plugins-page team-assignment control (#1691): `grantableTeams` (team constructs from the scoped `/api/agents`), `teamHasPlugin`, `pluginAssignmentState` (granted summary + AC6 empty / AC8 no-team states), `assignmentGranularity` (reuse manifest default), `assignmentGate` (invalid-manifest + platform-mismatch). Reuses `normalizeGrantEntries`/`platformMismatch` from `plugin-grant-view.js`; the JSX calls the existing activation proxy |
| `admin-console/src/pages/plugin-grant-view.js` | Pure grant-UI decisions: granularity selector, quota ceiling, platform match (#1518) |
| `broker-sdk/` | Dependency-free broker-obligations SDK a plugin sidecar imports: `verifyScopeToken` / `scopeFilter` / `auditWriter` / `maskSecrets` / `healthEndpoint` / `teardownScopeEndpoint` / `parkForApproval` (#1520) + `authorizeAction` (per-scope action policy, #1521); extracted from `podman-mcp/`, which is retrofitted onto it |
| `admin-console/api/plugin-ui-proxy.js` | Pure UI-extension surface (#1521): `deriveScopeList` (grant-derived scope discovery), `enforceUiRoute` (`ui.routes[].minRole`), `signViewerHeaders`/`verifyViewerHeaders` (signed viewer identity) — wired live by the §4.8 proxy (#1562) |
| `admin-console/api/plugin-ui-viewer.js` | Pure viewer decisions for the §4.8 UI proxy (#1562): `resolveViewerRole` (effective role over the plugin's granted teams), `uiSubPath` (mount-prefix strip), `visibleScopes` (per-caller scope narrowing). The console proxy `ALL /plugins/:id/ui/*` + `GET /api/plugins/:id/scopes` are thin adapters over this and `plugin-ui-proxy.js`; the sidecar address is `GET /internal/plugins/:id/ui-endpoint` and each sidecar gets an `AEE_PLUGIN_STATE_DIR` volume. **Opt-in global UI visibility (#1599):** `isGloballyVisibleUi(manifest)` (manifest `ui.global: true`, off by default) + `effectiveViewerRole(scope, grantedTeamIds, {globalVisible})` — an installed globally-visible plugin's nav entry (Shell.jsx) + general routes are shown/served to every authenticated user without a grant, admitting a grant-less caller as an ordinary `team-member` viewer so elevated (`minRole > team-member`) routes stay refused; agent capability is unaffected (grant path never reads `ui.global`) |
| `plugins/test-mgmt/` (broker `plugins/test-mgmt/broker/`) | Plugin #2 (Phase 5, #1521): test management on the Broker SDK; `aee-test` CLI + a thin credential-holding broker; first authorisation-by-granularity (`policy`) + first `requiresApproval` flow |
| `plugins/netzplan/` (broker `plugins/netzplan/broker/`) | Plugin #3 (Network Plan epic, #1563 Phase 3 + #1564 Phase 4 + #1566 Phase 6): planning board on the Broker SDK; `aee-plan` CLI with 3-rung autonomy ladder; `plan-doc.js` (id-keyed pure document model — all four §5 primitives `swimlane`/`graph`/`bipartite`/`blocks` + panel composition), `plan-import.js` (Phase 6 content migration — the positional→id `legacyBoardToPlanDoc` converter + idempotent `export`/`import` round-trip; **no engagement content committed**, concept §12), `plan-store.js` (state-volume fs adapter + timestamp/autonomy-preserving `import`), `viewer-headers.js` (§4.8 viewer-header verifier), served board UI with an Edit mode over `/api/mutate`, a board Export button + a Team-Admin `/admin` Import control |
| `plugins/github-issues/` (broker `plugins/github-issues/broker/`) | GitHub Issues plugin (#1633): first UI-bearing **and** credential-holding plugin. List/read issues + add/remove labels in the console using each granted team's env GitHub token, pushed per-scope by the orchestrator (`orchestrator/plugin-github-creds.js` + `plugin-runtime.pushScopeCredentials` → broker `PUT /internal/scope-credentials`) so the token stays server-side. Broker on the SDK: pure `github-client.js` (GitHub REST + `GITHUB_API_BASE` seam), `scope-store.js` (in-memory creds + `tokenForRepo` authz guard + last-used prefs — `pickLastUsed(scopeIds, userSub)` selects the globally most-recently-used scope by max `updatedAt` (#1650), not list order — + `flowLabels`/`labelAgents` cache), `viewer-headers.js`, served `ui/`. **Restore on reopen (#1650):** `GET /context` now also attaches each team's own `lastRepo`/`lastState` so switching back to an older project in `app.js` restores that project's remembered view; `lastUsed` is the single globally most-recent entry that `preselect()` uses on boot. State-selector changes now `persistPref()` server-side (previously only `saveLocal`). Read-only `aee-issues` CLI; label writes are the human console action. **Flow-label suggestions (#1642):** `buildTeamFlowLabels` in `plugin-github-creds.js` derives `{ flowLabels, labelAgents }` from the team's label registry + `labelFilter` triggers + `emits` — the union is pushed alongside the credential; `flowLabels` are suggested ahead of raw repo labels; `labelAgents[repo][label]` previews which enabled agents each label wakes (`''` = any-label listener). **Markdown body (#1642):** `ui/markdown.js` is a dependency-free, XSS-safe GFM-subset renderer (escapes all untrusted input, whitelist-only elements, protocol-validated links) — no build step, covered by `node --test`. **Theme (#1642):** `ui/styles.css` uses `--eb-*` design tokens (re-declared for `admin-dark`/`admin-light`/`high-contrast` because the iframe is a separate document); a pre-paint script reads `localStorage['eb-theme']` and `app.js` follows live toggles via the `storage` event (same origin as the console proxy). **Pagination + title filter (#1658):** the issue list is a discrete **Previous/Next pager** (replaces the old "Load more" append) plus a top-of-list, debounced **title filter** backed by a new `GET /api/search` route → `github-client.js` `search_issues` op (GitHub Search API, `in:title`/`repo:`/`type:issue` qualifiers owned by the broker via `buildSearchQuery`/`sanitizeSearchTerms` so a user-typed `qualifier:` can't broaden scope; state-scoped, issues-only, `shapeSearchResult`); listing + search share the pager and reset to page 1 on any team/repo/state/query change; the filter text is ephemeral (never a pref). Pager + search-vs-list + empty-state logic is the pure, `node --test`-covered `ui/issue-list.js` (UMD `window.AEEIssueList`, mirrors `markdown.js`). **Workflow-trigger buttons (#1730):** the issue-detail panel renders an **action bar** between the meta line and the description — one prominent **`Trigger <Agent>`** button per flow label that wakes ≥1 agent (from `agentsForLabel`), each showing the underlying label name and colour-coded by a new per-label `labelUsage` classification (`buildTeamFlowLabels` → `{ [label]: 'humanGate' | 'agentEmitted' }`, pushed + served on `/labels`; humanGate flag wins over an emit, unclassified → neutral); an already-applied label's button is visible-but-disabled ("already triggered", since re-adding emits no fresh `issues.labeled`). Pressing one fires the existing `writeLabel(name,'add')` immediately (no confirm). Purely additive to `renderDetail`; the bottom label list is unchanged |
| `plugins/patterns-catalog/` (broker `plugins/patterns-catalog/broker/`) | UI-only, privilege-less reference-catalog plugin: the former built-in **Patterns Catalog** page (SDLC + Mainframe Modernization catalogs) re-shipped as a plugin. Broker is a minimal static-file server on the Broker SDK — `server.js` serves `ui/` (landing `index.html` + `sdlc.html`/`mainframe.html` wrappers over the moved `*-content.html` docs) behind `verifyViewerHeaders` (`viewer-headers.js`, verbatim §4.8 scheme); no MCP, no state store, teardown is a no-op. Self-contained `aee-patterns` CLI lists the catalogs. Removed from the core console (nav/routes/pages), re-activated by install + team grant |
| `plugins/spec-kit/` | **Command-only** plugin (#1798): GitHub's spec-driven development toolkit. No sidecar, no CLI facade — manifest declares `commands: { namespace: "speckit" }` + `fetch: { source: "github", repo: "github/spec-kit", ref: "main" }`. Install fetches `.md` command files from GitHub, writes them to `<registry>/commands/`, and records the version in `installed-version.json`. Grant provisions the files into the agent workspace's `.claude/commands/speckit/` via `projectSkillCommands`. Commands invoked as `/speckit:specify`, `/speckit:plan`, `/speckit:tasks`, `/speckit:implement` |
| `admin-console/src/pages/{WorkingEnvironments,TeamSetup,ProcessCanvas,Plugins}.jsx` | env CRUD; the 5-step wizard; the Flows graph/editor (`ProcessGraphPanel`); the plugin registry + install review. `Agents.jsx` (agent CRUD) left this list with the page itself in #1930 — its forms and dialogs had already been extracted to `src/pages/agent-lifecycle/` and are hosted by Agent Chat and `/create` |
| `admin-console/src/components/virtual-window.js` | Pure, DOM-free list-virtualization windowing math (#1685): `prefixOffsets` (prefix-sum of row heights), `itemAtOffset` (binary search for the row at a pixel), `computeWindow` (visible slice + top/bottom spacer padding + total height, with overscan); unit-tested, `admin-console-src` CI job.
